       The cost of a Bellman construction depends on how its continuation is evaluated. 2
3                                                                                                         3
     A literal evaluation of the cone network in Theorem 10 visits every state label at
4

5                                                                                                         5
     The following result compiles the same continuation and original action witnesses;
6                                                                                                         6
     it neither changes the fitted function nor substitutes a newly optimized policy.
7

28   Forward and backward relaxations along each coordinate line compute all pairs (zv , ov )             28

29   using O(dS) additions and comparisons and O(S) stored values and indices. After cell                 29

1      When node coordinates, labels and L are rational, exact rational preprocessing and           1

2    comparisons implement these identities, including zero slopes, inconsistent labels and ties.   2

3    Applying the same repair map to the original returned action gives exactly the same feasible   3

     Choose now i = i∗ ( x ). At this corner an owner with a strictly smaller transformed           10

       The nodal distance transform is classical; the linear-time coordinate algorithm is
19                                                                                                  19
     described by Felzenszwalb and Huttenlocher (2012). We use it for a different com-
20                                                                                                  20
     putational obligation: continuous off-grid Bellman queries must retain the original
21                                                                                                  21

22                                                                                                  22

23                                                                                                  23

24                                                                                                  24

32   implementation, the representation part of a backward construction changes from                32
                                    NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                    43

1    O(∑t dSt+1 Qt ) to                                                                               1

5    Primitive evaluation, feasible-net construction, integration, error accounting, actor            5

6    formation, repair and output are additional terms. Exact arithmetic has a bit cost;              6

7    the count in (51) is not a claim that rational comparisons have unit physical cost. 7
8    Our records therefore report coefficient bit lengths, storage and complete process               8

9    and CPU times alongside the component counts.                                                    9

10     In the two-state investment study, S = ( N + 1)2 , J = N + 1 and M = N. At                     10

11   fixed dimension and horizon, compiling removes the extra state-cover factor from                 11

12   the dense representation workload: O( TN 6 ) becomes O( TN 4 ), plus the explicitly              12

13   charged setup and output. This does not remove the tensor cover, the action cover, or            13

14   the innovation factor. In dimension d, both S and the 2d corner factor remain visible. 14
15   The four-dimensional stress cases below are executions of this finite-dimensional                15

16   construction, not evidence that the curse of dimensionality has disappeared.                     16

31     For fixed primitive parameters and horizon, choosing the smallest sufficient dyadic            31

32   resolution gives N = O(ε−1 ). The compiled algebraic construction uses O( TN 4 ) operations,     32
     44

1    versus O( TN 6 ) for the literal dense cone evaluation, and stores O( TN 2 ) labels, owners      1

2    and actions. Executing the full dyadic ladder to this resolution has the same orders, with all   2

3    preceding attempts charged. Numerical precision, coefficient bit lengths, uncertainty-box        3

4    work and physical output latency remain separate costs; these orders are not a precision-free    4

5    or machine-independent timing claim.                                                             5

          P ROOF : The first two allocations make the right side of (54) at most ε/2. The             7

8
     acquisition and quantization terms add at most another ε/2 by (59). Compilation                  8

9
     preserves the selected policy, so it does not change this account. On the dyadic                 9

10
     ladder, the sum of every positive power of the earlier resolutions is bounded by                 10

11
     a constant times that power of its largest resolution. Summing the complete alge- 11
12
     braic operation counts and retained arrays proves the resource assertions. Effective             12

13
     certified queries at the prescribed accuracy, rather than unchecked fixed-precision              13

14
     evaluations, are part of the hypotheses.                                              Q.E.D.     14

15                                                                                                    15
       The corollary turns the compiler into a sufficient work-to-economic-accuracy
16                                                                                                    16
     allocation. A finite experimental ladder can still end before the sufficient resolution;
17                                                                                                    17
     its failure must remain a failure. Nor does more precise observation eliminate
18                                                                                                    18
     construction error: the two portions of the budget are distinct.
19                                                                                                    19

20                                                                                                    20
                      11.2. Certified numerical queries and selector ambiguity
21                                                                                                    21

22     The executable compiler computes (zv , ov ) in rational arithmetic. A value query              22

23   encloses each corner expression outward and takes the minima of lower and upper                  23

24   endpoints. For a state box contained in one cell, the possible original actors are               24

25   among its corner owners; for a box crossing cells, the union of their corner owners              25

26   is used. An owner is discarded only when its lower objective enclosure exceeds                   26

27   another candidate’s upper enclosure. Exact ambiguous point queries use rational                  27

28   comparisons and the original lexicographic rule. Uncertain-state queries retain                  28

29   the hull of all remaining actions and propagate that hull through repair and the                 29

30   dynamics.                                                                                        30

31     Thus a floating-point tie is not silently assigned to a convenient policy. A numeri- 31
32   cal implementation that deliberately returns a nonattaining index instead has to pay             32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                  45

1    the νt allowance in (42). The present frozen-object checks separate exact identities          1

2    from outward-rounding diagnostics. Overlapping machine intervals are useful                   2

3    checks; the identity theorem, rather than that overlap, proves that the compiler              3

4    represents the original policy.                                                               4

5                                                                                                  5

6
                 12. AN ORACLE-EXPLICIT CONSTRAINED INVESTMENT ECONOMY                             6

7      The following evidence is the frozen R47 study. Its results and limitations are             7

8    retained; Section 15 gives the new execution and decision evidence. We now execute            8

9    the feasible-witness construction in a coupled two-state economy. Capital affects             9

10   both future production conditions and current investment capacity. This makes fea- 10
11   sible repair part of the economic policy rather than an algebraic example appended            11

12   to a common-action calculation.                                                               12

15   the two transitions:                                                                          15

     is invariant under (52). In the ℓ1 state norm, valid primitive constants are                  29

1    The uniform tensor state grid with N subdivisions has covering radius h = 1/N. At each         1

2    node, N + 1 equally spaced feasible actions have covering radius at most k = 1/(8N ). The      2

3    repair is min{ ai , κ ( x )} and has zero extra displacement allowance. If a continuation is   3

4    L-Lipschitz, M uniform midpoint cells enclose its original continuous-law expectation after    4

5    adding the deterministic remainder L/(32M) to the numerical integration error.                 5

6                                                                                                   6

7         P ROOF : The primitive formulas give F1 ∈ [1/32, 29/32] and F2 ∈ [1/32, 27/32]            7

8    by interval bounds. The sums of absolute derivatives in each state column are at               8

9    most 11/16, and the action derivative has ℓ1 norm 3/4. Differentiating the costs               9

10   on the two shortage regions gives (53). Capacity is 1/16-Lipschitz and at most                 10

11   1/4, proving the grid and repair assertions. Within a uniform shock cell, the mean             11

12   distance to its midpoint is 1/(64M). Since the transition has shock modulus two in             12

13   ℓ1 , the expectation error is at most L/(32M). Thus quadrature does not change the             13

14   innovation law to a discrete one.                                                   Q.E.D.     14

       The two generators are the feasible cone witness and conventional fitted-value
17                                                                                                  17
     iteration in the tensor bilinear basis. Each works backward against its own fitted
18                                                                                                  18
     future, uses the same feasible action net, and evaluates the same continuous law
19                                                                                                  19
     with the preceding remainder. No optimal value or other method’s continuation is
20                                                                                                  20
     supplied as a label. Rounded labels, their valid query errors, slopes, node actions
21                                                                                                  21
     and all policy bounds are stored.
22                                                                                                  22
       The conventional actor selects a nearest state node and repairs its action at
23                                                                                                  23
     the current capacity. It receives the one-sided account rather than only the older
24                                                                                                  24
     separated certificate. For the bilinear interpolant, the Bellman-residual enclosure is
25                                                                                                  25

26                                                                                                  26
                           −et − Lt h ≤ f t − Tt f t+1 ≤ DtQ k + et + Lt h.
27                                                                                                  27

28                                                                                                  28
     The interpolation weights are nonnegative and reproduce coordinates. Their
29                                                                                                  29
     weighted mean ℓ1 distance to the surrounding vertices is at most 1/N, which
30                                                                                                  30
     proves the stated extension from the nodal enclosure.

11   continuation is bilinear. An affine function minus a bilinear function attains its                 11

12   maximum at a vertex: first maximize over one coordinate, then over the other. 12
13   The label’s feasible action, repaired at the queried state, has objective at most                  13

       In this class the previously abstract covering, integration and repair operations
19
     are finite routines with explicit costs. Let ST = ∑t<T βt , a p = Cx + K A Ca and b =              19

     β( Fx + K A Fa ) = 705/1024. Define                                                                20

22                       L = max{ L g , a p /(1 − b)},       D = Ca + βFa L.                            22

23                                                                                                      23
     The primitive recursion gives Lt ≤ L and DtQ ≤ D at every horizon. Suppose that,
24                                                                                                      24
     apart from the quadrature remainder, each nonterminal numerical query has error
25                                                                                                      25
     at most ξ and terminal labels have error at most ξ T . With M = N, Theorem 10
26                                                                                                      26
     implies
27                                                                                                      27
                                                             T

31   Thus a dyadic N ≥ 2CT,p /ε and ξ, ξ T ≤ ε/[4(ST             + β T )] suffice for loss at most ε.   31

32   Acquisition and action quantization add the explicit terms of (45); these must                     32
     48

1    receive part of the same tolerance. Fixed binary64, a fixed label lattice and a finite     1

2    three-rung study are not asserted to achieve every positive ε.                             2

3      For this two-state, one-action, one-shock implementation, Bellman queries num- 3
4    ber T ( N + 1)3 . Each has N midpoint evaluations. Direct cone evaluation examines         4

5    ( N + 1)2 cones, giving O( TN 6 ) primitive arithmetic work. Bilinear future evalua- 5
6    tion uses a local patch, giving O( TN 4 ) work for that implementation. Both store         6

7    O( TN 2 ) critic labels and actions. The present batched program additionally holds        7

8    O( N 3 ) state–action arrays and O( BN 2 ) temporary cone entries for fixed batch size     8

9    B. Feasible acquisition and repair add their recorded deployment work, rather than         9

10   being included at zero cost.                                                               10

11     With pb -bit arithmetic and multiplication cost M( pb ), the corresponding arith- 11
12   metic upper bounds acquire a factor M( pb + T + log N ), with coefficient conversion,      12

13   comparison and serialization charged separately. The precision must also meet              13

14   the query enclosure requirement. These are algorithmic upper bounds, not lower             14

15   bounds for all conventional methods. Proposition 13 shows that the identical native        15

16   envelope has the same leading factors as its ReLU realization. No representation           16

17   label removes the tensor dependence.                                                       17

                            12.2. Execution and acquired deployment
21                                                                                              21

22     The fixed ladder is N = M = 4, 8, 16, with three isolated processes per method and       22

23   economic cell. The 24 services store all 72 rungs, including failed attempts. Targets      23

24   are 4, 2, 1, 1/2 in the normalized cost units. They test the construction-to-certificate   24

25   service; a mathematically valid coarse bound is not automatically an economically          25

26   adequate tolerance. The full attained bounds and work therefore accompany the              26

27   target counts.                                                                             27

28     Both methods attain the target 4 in all twelve services, at the last rung, and nei- 28
29   ther attains 2, 1 or 1/2 within this cap. The witness’s final bound is smaller in each     29

30   economic cell, but bilinear fitted-value iteration is faster in all twelve common suc- 30
31   cessful comparisons. These coarse all-state certificates establish an executed feasible    31

32   construction, not an economically precise solution at the unachieved tolerances. The       32

17     For each of the twelve distinct saved witness policies we also execute the selector                  17

19   Exact robust repair and downward action spacing 2−12 give 72 deployment cases. 19

21   midpoint. The binary64 score allowance is uniform by (46); the grid records exercise                   21

22   implementation and repair activity rather than replacing that proof.                                   22

24   zation is charged explicitly. At exact acquired input the small nonzero numerical                      24

25   selection allowance remains in the exact records even when it rounds to zero in this                   25

26   table. At a nonzero radius the acquisition term is positive; the total bound is not                    26

27   reported as the exact-state certificate.                                                               27

1    piecewise-affine knots. Conditional expectation and then integration over the initial            1

3      Every simulated bin generates lower and upper score endpoints. When a state                    3

4    enclosure meets a selector boundary, the calculation encloses all possible actors and            4

5    subsequent states. It neither drops the path nor substitutes an unverified midpoint              5

       T HEOREM 18—Simultaneous policy comparison and adoption: Conditional on the                    12

13
     frozen policies and the independent uniform-bin model, the endpoint intervals constructed        13

14
     with (55) and (15) cover all declared expected policy-cost differences simultaneously with       14

15
     probability at least 0.99. If an interval [ Lmn , Umn ] has Umn < 0, policy m has smaller        15

16
     expected cost than n on this event. For a previously specified tolerance η, Umn ≤ η certifies    16

17
     noninferiority within that tolerance, whereas [ Lmn , Umn ] ⊂ [−η, η ] certifies two-sided       17

18
     proximity.                                                                                       18

19
       More generally, fix a reference policy b and known implementation charges k m . Suppose        19

20
     simultaneous intervals [ Lm , Um ] enclose Jm − Jb for a finite candidate catalogue, including   20

21
     [0, 0] for b. Select m
                          b to minimize Um + k m . On the coverage event,                             21

22                                                                                                    22
                             Jmb + k mb ≤ min{ Jm + k m + (Um − Lm )}.                        (56)
23                                         m                                                          23

24   Thus an economic choice made after seeing all intervals retains its declared uncertainty         24

25   account. The catalogue comparison is within one common economic task and initial-state           25

26   law, not across different prices, horizons or evaluations.                                       26

27                                                                                                    27

28     P ROOF : The score identity identifies the expected difference. Its interval end- 28
29   points are bounded independent observations within each group. Apply the one- 29
30   sided empirical Bernstein inequality of Maurer and Pontil (2009) to each lower                   30

31   and upper endpoint and take a union bound over 240 tails. The numerical enclo- 31
32   sures only enlarge the valid intervals. Independence between different contrasts is              32
     52

1    unnecessary. On the resulting event, Jmb − Jb ≤ Umb and Um ≤ Jm − Jb + (Um − Lm ). 1
2    Minimize the former upper bound plus the known charge to obtain (56).                       Q.E.D.    2

3                                                                                                          3

4      The study fixes η = 1/1024 in its normalized cost units before execution. It is                     4

5    a stated decision tolerance, not an estimated welfare threshold. Zero remains the                     5

6    superiority threshold. An interval merely intersecting zero meets neither criterion                   6

7    automatically. The fixed pseudorandom implementation supplies repeatable inputs;                      7

8    it does not prove the independent sampling model. The theorem states that model                       8

9    explicitly. No claim about initialization risk or the distribution of newly trained                   9

10   policies follows from these frozen-policy comparisons.                                                10

29                                                                                           29
                       13.2. Economic interpretation and the NBO program
30                                                                                           30

31     The new evidence connects a feasible implementation to two distinct economic          31

32   statements. First, the acquisition-aware theorem bounds the loss of a policy that can   32
     54

1    actually be executed under uncertain state measurement and capacity constraints. 1
2    Second, the direct experiment certifies proximity of the original scalar policy costs       2

3    within an explicitly chosen decision tolerance, so that a user of that catalogue may        3

4    consider implementation charges without treating separate regret bounds as values. 4
5    These statements are stronger than a certificate comparison alone.                          5

6      Neither statement implies that a neural representation is indispensable. The iden- 6
7    tical native envelope is a mathematical control, and the conventional fitted-value          7

8    and spline implementations remain strong alternatives. The constrained experiment           8

9    demonstrates two-state continuous-uncertainty construction and nontrivial repair,           9

10   not a high-dimensional scaling result or an estimated economic application. Its tight       10

11   targets remain unachieved. The direct cost experiment does not cover the new con- 11
12   strained policies or nonlinear recursive utility; its expectation-specific conclusion       12

13   is not transferred to those settings. The controlled-diffusion, recursive-preference,       13

14   endogenous-preference, temporal-self and game developments below retain their               14

15   own conditions and original role in the NBO program. Their preservation is not              15

16   counted as a new comparative execution.                                                     16

17                                                                                               17

18          14. PRICED OBSERVATION AND DIRECT CONSTRAINED-POLICY DECISIONS                       18

19                                                                                               19
       A state-error radius is an economic input when acquiring more precise infor-
20                                                                                               20
     mation is costly. We specify a finite-bit observation technology and a controller
21                                                                                               21
     replacement charge. These are primitives of a theoretical decision problem in re-
22                                                                                               22
     source units, not estimates from a calibrated economy. They permit a decision
23                                                                                               23
     interpretation without treating an interval containing zero as equality or treating an
24                                                                                               24
     all-state regret bound as a ranking of two policies.
25                                                                                               25

26                                                                                               26

27                          14.1. Choosing the observation precision                             27

28
       On [0, 1]d , a b-bit sensor reports, in each coordinate, the containing dyadic interval   28

29                                                                                               29
     and its midpoint. The right endpoint 1 belongs to the last interval. The resulting

3    the affine capacity economy this lower capacity is computed exactly from the lower                     3

4    box coordinates. The acquisition repair therefore has χt = 0 in Corollary 12.                          4

5      Suppose the same b is chosen before date zero and each coordinate-bit costs λ > 0                    5

6    per date. The observation fee is paid in the same additive resource units as the                       6

7    objective. With Bt = ∏s<t β s , it is Cb, where C = λd ∑t<T Bt . Write G for the full- 7

20     P ROPOSITION 19—Optimal precision for the certified economic account: On a                           20

26                                                                                                          26

27                                                                                                          27
     account; it need not minimize the unknown actual policy cost as a function of precision.

1      This calculation provides an explicit information-price comparative static: an              1

2    increase in λ weakly decreases the smallest minimizing precision, while an increase           2

3    in the sensitivity account A weakly increases it. The result concerns the guarantee,          3

4    not a fitted distribution of measurement errors. The executed curves use every                4

5    b = 4, . . . , 16 and every λ ∈ {1/16384, 1/4096, 1/1024}. All curves and endpoint            5

6    choices are retained, rather than reporting only an attractive radius.                        6

7                                                                                                  7

8                              14.2. The actual constrained-policy cost                            8

9      Fix the two-state witness and bilinear-FVI policies stored at the finest original           9

10   R47 resolution, N = 16. In each economic cell, the two policies are evaluated with            10

11   the same observation contract: exact state, six bits per coordinate, or ten bits per          11

12   coordinate. The finite-bit policies use robust capacity repair and action spacing             12

13   1/4096; the exact-state policies are unquantized. Thus there are three distinct pairs         13

14   of implemented policies, not three observations of an assumed continuous actor.               14

15     For an initial law µ, the estimand is                                                       15

20   sensor. The paired policies share innovations but follow their own endogenous                 20

21   state paths. This is their actual cost difference, not a difference of regret certificates. 21
22   A raw path score is used here; the earlier own-continuation score remains a valid             22

23   alternative when its signed support is sharper.                                               23

     If replacing either installed controller costs k > 0 once at date zero, an interval strictly
14                                                                                                       14
     inside (−k, k ) proves that neither replacement recoups that fee in expected resource cost for
15                                                                                                       15
     that task. An interval with upper endpoint below zero ranks the witness policy below FVI; a
16                                                                                                       16
     lower endpoint above zero ranks it above FVI. Containing zero does not establish equality.

22                                                                                                       22
     (2009) to the lower and upper endpoint samples, with a union bound across 2K
23                                                                                                       23
     one-sided assertions; certified moment and arithmetic allowances enlarge, rather
24                                                                                                       24
     than shrink, the intervals. Finally, switching from FVI to witness changes cost by
25                                                                                                       25
     Dµ,b + k, while switching in the opposite direction changes it by k − Dµ,b . Both are
26                                                                                                       26
     positive when | Dµ,b | < k.                                                              Q.E.D.

28     The experiment fixes K = 48, n = 262144 and α = 0.01 before execution. An exact                   28

29   rational exponential bound verifies that ℓ = 10 is sufficient. Forty-bit bins cover the             29

30   continuous initial and innovation laws. The initial laws are independent uniform co- 30
31   ordinates and the three fixed vectors (1/8, 1/8), (1/2, 1/2) and (7/8, 7/8), crossed                31

32   with all four horizon–price cells and three sensors. A fixed pseudorandom stream                    32
     58

1    makes the records reproducible; it is not a proof that those indices are independent     1

2    random variables.                                                                        2

3      The replacement fee is k = 1/64, one quarter of the maximal quadratic investment       3

4    expenditure at p = 1. It is an explicit theoretical transaction charge. We do not        4

5    calibrate it to welfare data, choose it after inspecting the intervals, or infer an all- 5
6    state ranking from the four initial laws. Reporting the zero threshold separately        6

7    keeps the decision statement distinct from a claim of neural superiority.                7

8                                                                                             8

9          15. COMPLETE COMPILED-POLICY SERVICES AND ECONOMIC COMPARISONS                     9

10                                                                                            10
       The new study separates three questions: the cost of representing a fixed NBO
11                                                                                            11
     policy, the complete cost of constructing and certifying a policy, and the actual cost
12                                                                                            12
     of using one controller rather than another. A representation benchmark cannot by
13                                                                                            13
     itself answer either of the latter questions. Conversely, a conventional comparator
14                                                                                            14
     receives the same innovation law, feasibility conditions and all-state comparison
15                                                                                            15
     principle, not a deliberately inefficient implementation chosen to favor the neural
16                                                                                            16
     operator.
17                                                                                            17

18                                                                                            18
                     15.1. Design, stopping rules and conventional baselines
19                                                                                            19

20     The design was fixed before the complete execution. The principal catalogue uses       20

21   the original constrained two-state economy of Section 12, horizons T = 2, 3, prices      21

22   p = 1, 4, and N = M ∈ {4, 8, 16, 32, 64}. The three methods are the compiled cone        22

23   witness, uniform tensor FVI and error-driven nonuniform tensor FVI. There are            23

24   three isolated process repetitions for each method and economic cell: 36 services and    24

25   180 rungs. Every rung starts from primitives and uses its own future continuation. 25
26   All rungs are executed, including those after a target crossing; first-target work       26

27   charges the complete prefix, including every prior failure.                              27

28     The conventional adaptive method forms an own-future pilot on a 5 × 5 grid,            28

29   uses its coordinatewise second differences to allocate nonuniform dyadic knots, and      29

30   certifies with the actual largest coordinate gaps. Its pilot, allocation and repeated    30

31   target evaluations are charged. The section on nonuniform fitted-value comparison        31

32   in the supplement specifies the fixed heuristic and proves the strengthened nearest- 32
                                      NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                         59

1    node certificate on these unequal grids. It is a conventional adaptive-coordinate FVI                  1

2    comparator, not a sparse-grid method or an unrestricted optimal adaptive partition. 2
3      All methods share outward arithmetic, feasible action fractions and midpoint                         3

4    integration with its deterministic remainder under the continuous uniform law. 4
5    Each service includes a finite acquired-state deployment checksum and durable                          5

6    checkpoint output. Complete prefix wall and CPU times, method-specific eval- 6
7    uation counts, coefficient bit lengths, stored actor scalars, checkpoint bytes and                     7

8    peak resident memory are recorded. The internal clock excludes startup and the                         8

9    common non-economic warm-up; a separate process clock includes them. No inde- 9
10   pendent cost comparison is hidden inside that boundary: its joint service is reported                  10

11   separately below.                                                                                      11

                   15.3. Direct constrained-policy decisions and information prices
16                                                                                                            16

17     The direct comparison freezes the original N = 16 witness and FVI policies. It does                    17

18   not select the new R48 policies after examining their costs. Forty-eight policy-pair                     18

19   tasks cross the four economic cells, four initial laws and three observation contracts                   19

20   of Section 14. The full study contains 48 × 262144 = 12582912 paired paths. Nu- 20
21   merical path widths, every unresolved actor or sensor branch, endpoint moments,                          21

22   simultaneous intervals and decisions are deposited. Table XVI summarizes inter- 22
23   val hulls across the four declared initial laws; its rows are not all-state confidence                   23

24   bands. The supplement reports every interval separately. The zero threshold and the                      24

25   replacement-fee threshold 1/64 are different decision questions. A no-recoupment                         25

26   result is conditional on that predeclared theoretical fee. It neither establishes exact                  26

27   policy equality nor measures empirical welfare equivalence.                                              27

28     The sensor-price experiment evaluates all thirteen precisions b = 4, . . . , 16 at each                28

29   of the three declared information prices, for every original witness checkpoint. 29
30   Table XVII gives the finest original resolution; the evidence includes all coarser                       30

31   checkpoints and every candidate precision, not only the minimizing choice. The op- 31
32   timum minimizes the uniform augmented upper account. Since the full-information                          32

                                      15.4. What changes in the evidence
26                                                                                                              26

27     The compiler theorem supplies a constructive work reduction for a defined NBO                            27

28   backend without changing its policy. The complete catalogue measures that backend                          28

29   against uniform and error-driven conventional FVI with all failed resolutions in- 29
30   cluded. Direct constrained costs and priced observation support explicit economic                          30

31   decisions for fixed policies under declared information contracts. These results                           31

32   extend the original program, rather than replace it with a different subject.                              32

18                                                                                                             18

19     The historical adverse findings remain: the original nonlinear optimizer catalogue,                     19

20   selected residual-only recertifications, unresolved neural–ridge signs, and mixed- 20
21   precision timing records are not reclassified. Identical native and ReLU envelopes                        21

22   remain one mathematical construction. The new deterministic repetitions do not                            22

23   estimate a population success probability for nonconvex optimization; the direct                          23

24   confidence statements concern their fixed policies under the sampling model; the                          24

25   stress clocks do not identify hardware-general scaling. The comparative claims                            25

26   in this article are restricted to the objects actually proved or executed, while the                      26

27   controlled-economy and application-specific theoretical scope remains intact.                             27

28                                                                                                             28
                             16. ECONOMIC APPLICATIONS AND CONCLUSION
29                                                                                                             29

30     The original applications concern policy-dependent recursive utility, endogenous                        30

31   preference specifications, temporal selves, controlled diffusion problems, and dy- 31
32   namic games. Their complete economic statements and proofs are retained without                           32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                               65

1    alteration. The centered and transport bounds explain how an NBO can support              1

2    economic counterfactuals when changes in future primitives are controlled. The pol- 2
3    icy theorem explains what must be verified for the actual policy. Neither statement       3

4    supplies an equilibrium or transversality condition absent from the application.          4

5      The nonlinear investment evidence adds an executable link from primitives to            5

6    learned continuations and directly certified policies. Its retained-policy diagnostic     6

7    demonstrates that a failed tight certificate can reflect the resolution of verification   7

8    rather than the quality of the deployed policy. Its direct coupled comparison answers     8

9    a different question—the relative cost of two policies—without confusing separate         9

10   regret bounds with a method contrast.                                                     10

11     The acquired-state result makes that returned policy implementable under a              11

12   stated information and arithmetic contract. The constrained economy supplies finite       12

13   covering, integration and repair routines; the direct scalar experiment supplies          13

14   simultaneous tolerance-based cost comparisons and a catalogue-adoption rule. 14
15   Their distinct assumptions and work accounts remain visible.                              15

16     The compiler adds a policy-preserving computational step to this program. It            16

17   connects the explicit neural continuation to an executable representation whose           17

18   per-query work no longer scans the full state cover. The economic implementation          18

19   adds two further choices: how precisely to acquire the state, and whether replacing       19

20   an installed controller pays for itself. The first is governed by a priced, certified in- 20
21   formation account; the second by direct expected costs with an explicit replacement       21

22   charge. They are not inferred by ranking regret bounds.                                   22

23     The empirical content is correspondingly separated. Frozen-object experiments           23

24   identify representation costs for the same policy; complete services compare fresh        24

25   own-future constructions against conventional FVI; direct intervals concern fixed         25

26   policies and declared initial laws. The higher-dimensional executions expose the          26

27   remaining tensor and innovation costs. They do not constitute a dimension-free            27

28   approximation theorem, a calibration to welfare data, or a reliability law for unre- 28
29   stricted neural training. Within these boundaries, the operator supplies a construc- 29
30   tive route from economic primitives through a feasible implemented policy to a            30

31   verified resource decision.                                                               31

32                                                                                             32
     66

1      Neural Bellman Operators thus organize construction, certification, economic            1

2    reuse, and resource accounting around the policy actually returned. Their scientific      2

3    assessment requires all of these objects. Flexible conventional methods remain            3

4    important competitors; numerical activation is not a work saving, and a fixed             4

5    catalogue is not a general reliability theorem. Theorem 5 and Corollary 6 now supply      5

6    a separate deterministic reliability statement for an explicitly constructed neural       6

7    backend. They do not retroactively prove convergence of the earlier optimizers. 7
8    Preserving those distinctions makes the original economic program testable while          8

9    retaining its full theory and applications.                                               9

10                                                                                             10

11                                                                                             11
                     APPENDIX A: R EADING AND PRESERVATION MAP
12                                                                                             12

13     The main article is the authoritative R48 exposition of the existing Neural Bellman     13

14   Operators paper. It includes the controlled-economy formulation, centered transport       14

15   and policy comparison, constructive continuation and original witnesses, acquired- 15
16   state feasibility, exact compilation, and the economic decision analysis. The technical   16

17   supplement supplies the proofs and numerical accounts needed for those results,           17

18   as well as every new direct interval and the complete first-target time partitions. 18
19   All R47 main and supplement labels, including their inherited results, remain in the      19

20   corresponding active documents. The current response addresses the R47 report             20

21   item by item.                                                                             21

22     The experimental sections distinguish frozen historical evidence from new ex- 22
23   ecutions. Earlier optimizer, precision, scalar and coupled policy records are not         23

24   regenerated or relabeled. The full historical theory and application companions           24

25   are retained as ordinary files in the revision tree; preservation is not asserted to      25

26   be a new validation of every application. The main finite-horizon certificate needs       26

27   the monotonicity, cash-invariance, feasibility and comparison assumptions stated          27

28   here. The direct sampling result additionally uses conditional expectation and its        28

29   sampling model. Controlled diffusions, infinite horizons, recursive utility domains       29

30   and equilibrium applications retain their separate analytic conditions.                   30

31     The source tree contains the ordinary manuscript, proofs, bibliography, table           31

32   generator, scientific sources, complete frozen records and build instructions. A clean    32
                                NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                              67

1    rebuild audits the records and regenerates the documents without a new scientific      1

2    run or an expiring artifact download. The publication audit binds the review anchor,   2

3    scientific source, evidence, tests and compiled documents. These checks establish      3

4    a reproducible submitted object; they are not a substitute for mathematical peer       4

5    review.                                                                                5

1                       APPENDIX: C URRENT R52 DEVELOPMENT                                     1

2                                                                                              2
                               APPENDIX B: I NTRODUCTION
3                                                                                              3

4      A continuation value is useful to an economist because it changes a decision. Its       4

5    prediction error is not the loss of the policy actually implemented. Nor is a smaller     5

6    upper bound on that loss evidence that one policy has lower cost than another. When       6

7    observation, action selection and verification are costly, the numerical procedure        7

8    and the economic decision have to be evaluated together.                                  8

9      We develop Neural Bellman Operators as a policy-evaluation and feasible- 9
10   improvement method. The economic object remains a controlled economy with                 10

11   policy-specific continuations. The operator constructs each date’s continuation us- 11
12   ing its own fitted future, returns a feasible policy, and accounts for its loss against   12

13   the Bellman optimum. A general signed policy sandwich permits arbitrary fitted            13

14   candidates. A constructive affine–ReLU backend additionally produces the candi- 14
15   date from primitive moduli, without assuming that a nonconvex training algorithm          15

16   eventually succeeds. Exact compilation retains the original action witness through        16

17   off-grid evaluation and state-dependent feasibility repair.                               17

18     The principal question in this revision is how that construction produces a cer- 18
19   tified economic answer with finite resources. State coverage, action search and           19

20   integration have different error coefficients. Tying them to one grid size conceals       20

21   the allocation problem. We derive a separated primitive budget and an optimal             21

22   continuous allocation for its leading work proxy, including lower resource bounds         22

23   and dyadic implementation. The empirical comparison then records the complete             23

24   positive-tolerance frontier of a fixed common resource ladder, not only an isolated       24

25   favorable target. All failed earlier attempts belong to the work required to return a     25

26   policy.                                                                                   26

27     The next question is what the returned policy does. We evaluate the actual policies     27

28   at the declared first crossings, even when two generators cross at different reso- 28
29   lutions. Independent interval simulation preserves the original continuous initial        29

30   and innovation laws and encloses discontinuous actors and robust capacity repair. 30
31   Conditioning on construction information permits simultaneous cost inference after        31

32   this selection. The result does not infer a policy ranking from two certificates and      32
                                 NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                               69

1    does not extrapolate three deterministic timing repetitions into an optimizer-success    1

2    probability.                                                                             2

3      Two economic decisions make this distinction operational. First, a controller          3

4    replacement is worthwhile only if its actual gain exceeds the replacement fee. We        4

5    identify the directional gain intervals and the full break-even fee account, retaining   5

6    but no longer relying on a single generous charge. Second, observation precision is      6

7    chosen on the same finite bit grid on which the implemented policies are evaluated. 7
8    A simultaneous upper-net-cost selector has a regret bound relative to the best           8

9    precision in that catalogue. The bound holds for every nonnegative information           9

10   price on one coverage event. This is a result about actual net policy cost, different    10

11   from minimizing a conservative all-state loss account.                                   11

12     These advances do not depend on calling a classical envelope neural. A native          12

13   min-plus envelope and its exactly equivalent ReLU circuit are one method with            13

14   two encodings. The original-owner compiler is available to both. We compare that         14

15   method with uniform multilinear fitted-value iteration (FVI) and two separately          15

16   recorded conventional refinement rules. The original curvature-bisection block           16

17   remained uniform; the later graded block was nonuniform. A new residual-driven           17

18   comparator below records its realized geometry and all pilot work. Each generates        18

19   its own future labels and receives the same feasible action menu, continuous-law         19

20   integration and one-sided economic criterion. The evidence must determine the cost       20

21   of the returned policy as well as certificate sharpness. No uniform neural dominance     21

22   is built into the design.                                                                22

23                                                                                            23

24     Related methods. Lipschitz extension supplies the cone construction (McShane,          24

25   1934), and separable distance transforms supply its linear-work nodal engine             25

26   (Felzenszwalb and Huttenlocher, 2012). Approximation in min-plus spaces has              26

27   an established dynamic-programming literature: Lakshminarayanan and Bhatnagar            27

28   (2014) study min-plus projected Bellman equations and supremum-norm errors. 28
29   Bellman-inequality methods likewise construct lower value bounds and policies;           29

30   Wang et al. (2015) use iterated inequalities with linear or semidefinite optimization. 30
31   Our argument does not claim priority for these principles. Its subject is their con- 31
32   nection to an own-future feasible policy, original-owner continuous compilation,         32
     70

1    acquired-state implementation, complete resource allocation and direct economic                        1

2    comparison of the returned objects. The empirical-Bernstein component uses the                         2

3    bounded-sample inequality of Maurer and Pontil (2009); conditional selection and                       3

4    numerical path enclosures specify the estimand to which it is applied.                                 4

5      The broader NBO program includes controlled diffusions, recursive utility, en- 5
6    dogenous preferences, temporal selves and games. Those results and their as- 6
7    sumptions are retained in the complete development edition and its full proof                          7

8    supplement, together with the original training, matrix-factor and precision experi- 8
9    ments. The present article supplies a self-contained constructive theorem–evidence                     9

10   chain. Its direct-cost statistical theorems concern expected discounted costs, not an                  10

11   undeclared extension to every nonlinear recursive application. This organization                       11

12   preserves the paper’s economic subject while making the current contribution and                       12

13   the actual comparison burden explicit.                                                                 13

14     The additional improvement result addresses the economically important case                          14

15   where the certified construction returns a more costly policy than a conventional                      15

16   competitor. We preserve that incumbent and permit only actions whose actual                            16

17   reference advantage is certified nonpositive after the numerical and observation                       17

18   allowances. At the final date of the original investment model, exact continuous- 18
19   law integration makes this gate executable without assuming a successful policy- 19
20   value fit. Both generators receive the same repair. The resulting experiment isolates                  20

21   removable final-action loss from the policy gap that remains; it does not rename the                   21

22   compiler as a uniquely neural advantage.                                                               22

23                                                                                                          23

24          APPENDIX C: T HE ECONOMIC OPERATOR AND ITS POLICY ACCOUNT                                       24

25     Consider dates t = 0, . . . , T and an invariant compact state domain X ⊂ Rd . At                    25

26   date t, the action belongs to a nonempty compact set At ( x ). The transition is                       26

27   Ft ( x, a, Zt+1 ), the current cost is ct ( x, a), and the terminal cost is g( x ). Write B0 = 1       27

28   and Bt = ∏ j<t β j , with β j > 0. An implemented policy includes its observation rule,                28

29   action selection and feasible numerical realization, not just its stored parameters.                   29

30     For a continuation v, define                                                                         30

31                                                                                                          31
          Qt (v; x, a) = ct ( x, a) + β t ρt (v( Ft ( x, a, Z ))),   Tt v( x ) = inf Qt (v; x, a).   (65)
32                                                                             a∈ At ( x )                  32
                                  NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                  71

1    Throughout the deterministic comparison, the evaluations ρt are monotone and                 1

2    cash invariant on a common domain containing all functions and constant translates           2

3    used below. They are therefore nonexpansive in the supremum norm. Continuous                 3

4    objectives, effective compact feasible correspondences and measurable minimizers,            4

5    or measurable arbitrarily near-minimizers with their error charged, are assumed. 5
6    The stipulated conditional recursion gives Bellman comparison against all ad- 6
7    missible adapted controls. In the experiments ρt is conditional expectation, costs           7

8    are bounded and innovations have their original continuous uniform law. The                  8

9    direct-cost inference in Section J is specific to expectation; it is not automatically an    9

10   inference theorem for nonlinear recursive utility or games.                                  10

11     A Neural Bellman Operator is a construction and evaluation procedure for (65). 11
12   It forms targets using its own fitted f t+1 , returns a neural continuation and a feasible   12

13   policy, and records an economic error allowance. It does not receive the optimal             13

14   continuation as a training label. Arbitrary trained networks can be evaluated by the         14

15   first result below. The subsequent constructive backend specifies how a network              15

16   and its actor are obtained without assuming successful nonconvex optimization.               16

       A policy certificate answers a safety question relative to the optimum. It need
20                                                                                                  20
     not answer the comparison with an installed controller. We now make actual cost
21                                                                                                  21
     reduction a constraint on the improvement step. The continuation representation,
22                                                                                                  22
     including a neural realization, supplies proposals; a centered, policy-specific evalua-
23                                                                                                  23
     tion determines whether to implement them. The incumbent is always an admissible
24                                                                                                  24
     fallback.

17     The improvement principle is related to approximate policy iteration with safe- 17
18   guarded updates, including Metelli et al. (2021). We do not claim that monotone                               18

19   policy improvement itself is new. Here the relevant connection is between a signed                            19

20   own-policy evaluation account, action-centered numerical errors, original-owner                               20

21   construction, and the actual information-constrained action. The test is available                            21

22   to neural and conventional proposals alike. An exact native encoding of a ReLU                                22

23   continuation does not become a second independent method.                                                     23

17     C OROLLARY 26—Acquired-cell final improvement: In the declared investment                         17

18   economies, final conditional cost H ( x, a) = c( x, a) + βEg( F ( x, a, Z )) is strictly convex     18

19   in a, with second derivative at least 2p. Fix an observation cell C and the implemented             19

20   incumbent action aC . Choose a lattice minimizer aC∗ at the reported midpoint on the cell-wide      20

21   feasible set. Accept it only if an outward enclosure of H ( x, aC∗ ) − H ( x, aC ) is nonpositive   21

22   for all x ∈ C. Keep the incumbent on every other cell and at all earlier dates. The resulting       22

23   acquired policy weakly improves actual expected discounted cost for every initial state. This       23

24   guarantee does not compare it to a different full-information incumbent.                            24

25                                                                                                       25

26     The lattice is {k/4096 : k ∈ N0 }, truncated by the minimum capacity on the eight- 26
27   bit cell. Convex derivative bisection identifies the two neighboring lattice actions;               27

28   their actual cost contrast selects the midpoint proposal. The gate compares its cost                28

29   with the incumbent on the entire cell, not just at the midpoint. Exact rational point               29

30   comparison resolves a numerically ambiguous proposal tie. An uncertain true-state                   30

31   box may intersect several cells; the simulator retains their action hulls rather than               31

32   selecting a favorable cell.                                                                         32
     80

1      For each generator, old and repaired policies share their earlier trajectory. Their                        1

2    discounted cost difference therefore equals the conditional final contrast. Centering                        2

3    cancels state-only costs and tightens its interval enclosure. When two generators are                        3

4    compared, their trajectories differ and are simulated separately using common inno- 4
5    vations. Both comparisons concern actual policies; neither is obtained by subtracting                        5

6    two policy-loss certificates.                                                                                6

       The preceding improvement result compares an implemented policy with its
10                                                                                                                10
     own incumbent. We now connect that comparison to the original Bellman optimum.
11                                                                                                                11
     This distinction matters economically: a policy can improve strictly while remaining
12                                                                                                                12
     more costly than a conventional alternative. The result below preserves the original
13                                                                                                                13
     objective and gives explicit sufficient conditions for closing its optimality gap; it
14                                                                                                                14
     does not infer those conditions from a small empirical residual.

31     The hypotheses separate three computational tasks. A trained continuation must                          31

32   be evaluated relative to the policy actually retained; the action search must ap- 32
     82

1    proximate the original feasible infimum; and the comparison must be valid over                    1

2    the available information set. A guarantee for only one of these tasks does not                   2

3    imply the theorem. The same conclusion applies to native and neural representa- 3
4    tions when they implement the same objects. In particular, it does not turn their                 4

5    common compilation saving into a neural-specific advantage. Safe policy improve- 5
6    ment is an established principle; the result here supplies the signed-width and                   6

7    acquisition-aware error account for the incumbent-preserving NBO construction.                    7

         The directed-cover assumption is substantive when constraints move with the
18                                                                                                     18
     state. It is not implied by a dense grid at the representative alone. A failed cover or
19                                                                                                     19
     a cell that cannot certify a comparison retains its incumbent; it must not be counted
20                                                                                                     20
     as a successful accuracy refinement.

22        P ROPOSITION 29—Sharpness of the evaluation-width coefficient: Under the theo- 22
23   rem’s assumptions, the coefficient two on β t wtk+1 in (83) cannot be uniformly replaced by a     23

24   smaller constant, even for a one-period deterministic problem with two actions, an exact          24

25   greedy proposal, and exact contrast arithmetic.                                                   25

26                                                                                                     26

27       The theorem is constructive but conditional on verified error budgets. It does not            27

28   assert that a continuous neural fit uniformly approximates a discontinuous acquired- 28
29   policy value, or that refining a fixed acquisition contract eliminates its information            29

30   floor. The executed investment study below certifies a final-date improvement. It                 30

31   is not an execution of T full improvement passes, nor numerical evidence of the                   31

32   finite-sweep near-optimality conclusion. Exact finite-state regression tests exercise             32

4                APPENDIX G: P OLICY- RELEVANT CONTINUATION ERRORS                                          4

5      A continuation error matters for an investment decision through the actions it                       5

6    changes. The signed-width account above removes a datewise level error. It can nev- 6
7    ertheless charge for a nonconstant error that has the same conditional expectation                     7

8    under every feasible action. We now obtain a sharper account without changing the                      8

9    Bellman optimum, the acquisition contract, or the incumbent-preserving rule.                           9

     selected proposal alone is sufficient for safety, but not for the accuracy conclusion,
17                                                                                                          17

1                      G.1. Action-null components and neural realizations                             1

2                                                                                                      2
       Define the action-null space at date t by
3                                                                                                      3

4                  Nt = {n : ( Pta − Ptb )n( x ) = 0 for every x and a, b ∈ At ( x )}.                 4

5                                                                                                      5
     This is a linear space of bounded measurable functions, not a statement about
6                                                                                                      6
     the accuracy of a training procedure. Constants belong to it; exogenous payoff
7                                                                                                      7
     components can make it larger. Values differing by an element of Nt have identical
8                                                                                                      8
     action contrasts at date t, although their levels and state dependence need not agree.
9                                                                                                      9

10     C OROLLARY 32—Certified removal of action-null error:                Suppose etk+1 = nkt+1 + 10
11   ukt+1 , where membership nkt+1 ∈ Nt is established and ukt+1 has a verified range of width        11

12   vkt+1 . Then Rkt ≤ vkt+1 is available, independently of the amplitude of nkt+1 . A constructive   12

13   way to obtain this width is to evaluate the corrected critic hk + nk by the original signed- 13
14   residual recursion. The action comparisons using hkt+1 and hkt+1 + nkt+1 coincide.                14

15                                                                                                     15
       The qualification “established” is important. Arbitrarily declaring part of a fit to
16                                                                                                     16
     be null invalidates the certificate. If an exact null component is known, symbolic
17                                                                                                     17
     cancellation before interval arithmetic avoids subtracting two large levels. If it is
18                                                                                                     18
     only approximately null, its verified nonzero action contrast must be added to the
19                                                                                                     19
     allowance.
20                                                                                                     20

21     P ROPOSITION 33—A nonconstant ReLU component in the original economy:                           21

22   In the investment dynamics (112), for each declared dimension d ≥ 2, every bounded                22

23   measurable function of y1 − 2y2 is action-null under the common innovation. In particular,        23

24                                                                                                     24

25                           n M (y) = M (y1 − 2y2 + 1)+ ,         M ≥ 0,                              25

26                                                                                                     26
     is a one-unit ReLU realization with oscillation 2M on the state cube. At the final date, the
27                                                                                                     27
     critic h T = g − n M has global evaluation width 2M but zero action-contrast error. The
28                                                                                                     28
     contrast-aware terminal policy is therefore exactly the unperturbed policy of Corollary 26;
29                                                                                                     29
     its statewise cost guarantee is unchanged for every M.
30                                                                                                     30

31     This result supplies a representation-level robustness property of NBO, not an                  31

32   identified advantage over a native encoding of the same continuation. It also iden- 32
     86

1    tifies an avoidable source of conservatism in a global critic-error account. It does             1

2    not remove an economically relevant continuation error, a capacity constraint, or an             2

3    information floor. Full proofs and an exact finite-state reference implementation are            3

4    in the supplement.                                                                               4

       The action-contrast result permits an error component to be large when it is
8                                                                                                     8
     irrelevant to a decision. A second source of conservatism remains: taking an absolute
9                                                                                                     9
     value before comparing two actions discards the direction of a useful continuation
10                                                                                                    10
     difference. We retain that direction throughout the verification calculation. The
11                                                                                                    11
     policy generator, the original economic primitives, and the Bellman optimum are
12                                                                                                    12
     unchanged.

3                                                                                                               3
       This result does not replace Theorem 30. The earlier theorem is useful when
4                                                                                                               4
     only a symmetric error bound is available. The present certificate is useful when a
5                                                                                                               5
     numerical calculation retains signed endpoints. In either case safety for a proposed
6                                                                                                               6
     action is weaker than accuracy against the full feasible infimum. The action cover
7                                                                                                               7
     in (91) is indispensable for the latter. A small gap on a finite proposal menu alone is
8                                                                                                               8
     not a small Bellman gap.

          The choice h = 0 is a valid verification gauge: e = J π , the running residual is                               8

9
     the incumbent’s stage cost, and the terminal residual is g. It does not change                                       9

10
     the previously constructed policy or claim that a zero critic is an effective policy                                 10

11
     generator. Under this gauge, a common-innovation rollout of stage-cost differences                                   11

12
     computes the signed recursion directly. Its computational cost must therefore be                                     12

13
     charged as policy evaluation. Neither a neural critic nor a native representation                                    13

     receives that work for free.                                                                                         14

1         The acquired actor is discontinuous at cell boundaries. Its action range on a rectan- 1

4    intervals are integrated by enclosures on dyadic bins with their exact probabilities. 4

18                 H.4. Approximately null components and misspecification                      18

19     An exactly null direction is a special case of a quantitative bound. Let n(y) =          19

20   Mσ(v⊤ y + b), where σ is one-Lipschitz. For a verified coupling of the actual action       20

21   kernels, suppose s( x; a, b) bounds E|v⊤ (Y a − Y b )|. Then                               21

22                                                                                              22

23                              |( Pta − Ptb )n( x )| ≤ | M|s( x; a, b).                 (96)   23

24                                                                                              24
     This follows directly from the Lipschitz inequality and integration. For a common-
25                                                                                              25
     innovation affine exposure Y a − Y b = B( a − b), the bound is | M | |v⊤ B( a − b)|. If
26                                                                                              26
     v = v0 + δv, B = B0 + δB, and v0⊤ B0 = 0, the non-null coefficient is
27                                                                                              27

28
                                    δv⊤ B0 + v0⊤ δB + δv⊤ δB.                                   28

29                                                                                              29

30   Any action-dependent innovation discrepancy under the actual coupling con- 30
31   tributes its own expected absolute projection. These terms cannot be set to zero           31

32   because a fitted component resembles an invariant.                                         32
     92

1      The deposited perturbation catalogue varies the ReLU direction, action exposure,          1

2    and an action-dependent innovation-mean shift. Its positive bound is substituted            2

3    into the same incumbent gate. It also records the policy that would result from             3

4    falsely declaring the component null. This is a controlled model-misspecification           4

5    test with known perturbed primitives, not a learned-null discovery procedure or an          5

6    inference guarantee for an unknown transition law. The original investment laws             6

7    and the original cost comparisons remain unchanged.                                         7
