9         APPENDIX I: A LLOCATING RESOURCES TO AN ECONOMIC TOLERANCE                             9

10                                                                                               10
       A single resolution parameter imposes an economic restriction that the Bellman
11                                                                                               11
     problem does not impose. State coverage, action search and integration have differ-
12                                                                                               12
     ent error coefficients and different effects on work. Refining all three at the same rate
13                                                                                               13
     can therefore spend most of the service budget on the wrong source of error. We sep-
14                                                                                               14
     arate these choices while retaining the continuation, feasible-witness interpretation
15                                                                                               15
     and all-state comparison developed above.
16                                                                                               16

17                                                                                               17
                                I.1. A separated primitive budget
18                                                                                               18

19     Consider the investment family specified in Section L, with d ∈ {2, 3, 4} state           19

20   coordinates, discount β = 15/16, and uniform coordinate grids with N intervals. 20
21   At a state node use K + 1 feasible action fractions. Integrate the common scalar            21

22   innovation using M equal-probability bins and their midpoints. The three inte- 22
23   gers need not agree. Let Lt be the primitive graph modulus of the constructed               23

24   witness continuation, and let Dt be its action modulus. A downward numerical                24

25   displacement of the action grid is denoted by δa .                                          25

26     The state and action covering radii satisfy ht = d/(2N ) and k t ≤ 1/(8K ) + δa . The     26

27   innovation lies in an interval of length 1/16. Within a bin its expected absolute           27

28   distance from the midpoint is 1/(64M). Since changing the scalar innovation by              28

29   z − z′ changes the next state by at most d|z − z′ | in the ℓ1 norm, a certified numerical   29

30   query has an error allowance                                                                30

     94

1    its target-generation innovation count at that same state grid is 129 · 128/(17 · 8)             1

2    times larger. This is an arithmetic comparison of two declared allocations, not an               2

3    observation of the unexecuted diagonal service or a runtime ratio. Both witness and              3

4    conventional FVI receive the separated allocation in the experiment.                             4

6                   I.2. The optimal continuous allocation and its boundary cases                     6

7                                                                                                     7
       A useful planning problem minimizes the leading product N d KM subject to (98).
8                                                                                                     8
     This proxy isolates the primitive evaluation term; it is not the entire operation count
9                                                                                                     9
     or the measured wall clock. Write E = ε − η > 0 and suppose first that a, b, q are
10                                                                                                    10
     positive and all optimal resources exceed their required lower bounds.
11                                                                                                    11

12        P ROPOSITION 38—Error shares and clipped allocation: The unique interior mini- 12
13   mizer of the continuous proxy has                                                                13

14                                                                                                    14
                        a    d               b   1               q   1

17                                                                                                    17

23                                                                                                    23

26        P ROOF : Set xi = ci /ri . Minimizing the resource product is equivalent to maxi- 26
27   mizing ∑i pi log xi over 0 < xi ≤ x̄i and ∑i xi ≤ E. Strict concavity and the multiplier         27

28   conditions give pi /xi equal on unconstrained coordinates and larger on capped                   28

29   coordinates. This is precisely (102). The sum of its right sides is strictly increasing          29

30   until all caps bind. The unconstrained case yields (101). Upward dyadic rounding                 30

31   never enlarges an error term and multiplies N d KM by less than the stated factor.               31

1      The optimization is a standard separable resource-allocation calculation. Its role        1

2    here is to expose the error coefficients of the actual own-future policy construction,      2

3    including integration and implementation, rather than to claim novelty for the              3

4    multiplier argument. A finite experimental menu need not contain the continuous             4

5    optimum. The prototype uses a common, frozen dyadic menu for all generators                 5

6    and records all of it. It does not describe this menu as a globally optimal tuning          6

7    policy for either FVI or the witness method.                                                7

8                                                                                                8

9                                                                                                9
                                I.3. A complete tolerance frontier
10                                                                                               10

11     For method m, let a prospectively fixed ladder return certificates gm,r and cumu- 11
12   lative costs wm,r , including failed earlier rungs. Define                                  12

13                                                                                               13

14                     rm (ε) = min{r : gm,r ≤ ε},      Wm (ε) = wm,rm (ε) .             (103)   14

15                                                                                               15

16   An empty set is a capped failure, not an interpolated success. Sort the finite union        16

17   of all certificate values and zero. On each left-closed, right-open interval between        17

18   successive values, every method’s first crossing and complete work are constant. 18
19   Evaluating the first-crossing rule at the left endpoint therefore reconstructs the          19

20   entire positive-tolerance frontier exactly, even when successive certificates are not       20

21   monotone. An unbounded final interval is retained. Repetitions have separate costs,         21

22   while their mathematical checkpoints must be checked for identity.                          22

23     This finite partition supplies the appropriate interpretation of a threshold ad- 23
24   vantage. At a fixed resolution the witness method may cost more. At a tolerance             24

25   where it crosses one rung earlier, the smaller certificate may more than offset that        25

26   cost. Whether this happens, and over which tolerance interval, is determined by             26

27   (103); it is not determined by a favorable single threshold. Under an additional            27

28   homogeneous model gm (n) = Am /n and wm (n) = Cm             nκ , the continuous frontier   28

29   ratio is (CW /CF )( AW /A F )κ . This last expression is a conditional explanation, not a   29

30   fitted scaling law for the experiments. The release supplies the exact finite partition,    30

31   sensitivity around one, complete prefixes and separate fixed-resolution observa- 31
32   tions.                                                                                      32
     96

1    APPENDIX J: F ROM RETURNED POLICIES TO ECONOMIC RESOURCE DECISIONS                         1

2                                                                                               2
       An all-state upper bound and a comparison of actual policy costs answer different
3                                                                                               3
     economic questions. We now connect the policies returned by a construction frontier
4                                                                                               4
     to direct cost inference and then to a choice of implementation resources. The
5                                                                                               5
     construction data select the policies; independently generated evaluation paths
6                                                                                               6
     assess them. This separation permits a valid comparison of first-crossing policies
7                                                                                               7
     with different resolutions.
8                                                                                               8

9                                                                                               9
                          J.1. Simultaneous inference after construction
10                                                                                              10

11     Let H denote all construction information: primitive inputs, numerical labels,           11

12   fitted continuations, returned policies and complete certificate ladders. Conditional      12

13   on H, fix a finite catalogue of implemented-policy contrasts. Each implementation          13

14   includes its observation rule, original witness or nearest-node selector, true-state       14

15   feasible repair and action quantization. For a contrast i, let Di be the difference in     15

16   expected discounted costs under a specified initial law. A common-innovation path          16

17   score evaluates the two policies on their own endogenous trajectories. It is not the       17

18   difference between two regret bounds.                                                      18

19     Suppose the initial and innovation uniforms are partitioned into equal bins. 19
20   Independent uniform bin indices are drawn, and interval simulation encloses the            20

21   score for every continuous realization inside the drawn bins. All unresolved state,        21

22   selector and repair branches remain in the enclosure. If [ Ai , Bi ] is a deterministic    22

23   support interval, clip the path endpoints to that support. Write mi , mi for outward       23

24   sample-mean bounds on the lower and upper endpoint samples and vi,L , vi,U for             24

25   upper bounds on their unbiased sample variances. For n ≥ 2 define                          25

26                                                                                              26
                                  √           7( Bi − Ai )ℓ
27                   Ri ( v ) =       2vℓ/n +               ,   ℓ ≥ log(4K/α),         (104)    27
                                               3( n − 1)
28                                                                                              28

29   where K bounds the number of sampled contrasts.                                            29

30                                                                                              30

31        T HEOREM 39—Construction-conditional economic intervals: Under the stated             31

32   independent-bin model, conditional on any valid construction history H, with probability   32
                                    NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                     97

1    at least 1 − α all catalogue contrasts satisfy                                                    1

2                                                                                                      2
                Di ∈ [li , ui ] := [max{ Ai , mi − Ri (vi,L )}, min{ Bi , mi + Ri (vi,U )}].   (105)
3                                                                                                      3

4    The same coverage holds unconditionally. Subsequent selection among these covered con- 4
5    trasts, or evaluation at any deterministic implementation-price vector, needs no additional       5

6    union bound. This conclusion applies only to policies and initial laws included in the            6

7    catalogue; it does not create direct-cost observations for every unstudied tolerance or initial   7

8    state.                                                                                            8

9                                                                                                      9

10
       P ROOF : For each drawn bin sequence, conditional integration over its continu- 10

11
     ous realizations places the exact path expectation between the interval endpoints. 11

12
     Uniform mixing over the equal bins recovers the original continuous law. The lower                12

13
     endpoint expectation is thus no larger than Di , and the upper endpoint expectation               13

14
     is no smaller. Apply the bounded empirical-Bernstein inequality of Maurer and                     14

15
     Pontil (2009) to the endpoint samples and take the union bound, with outward                      15

16
     arithmetic included in the mean, variance and radius. Conditional validity for every              16

17
     H implies unconditional validity by integration. On this one simultaneous event, all              17

18
     ensuing deterministic comparisons of the covered quantities hold together. Q.E.D.                 18

19                                                                                                     19
       In particular, selecting the first policy to meet a specified certificate is permitted
20                                                                                                     20
     when that selection uses construction records rather than the subsequent evaluation
21
     paths. Repeated construction clocks are not independent draws from a neural- 21
22                                                                                                     22
     optimizer population. The statistical randomness here belongs to the evaluation
23                                                                                                     23
     design. A fixed pseudorandom stream implements a reproducible experiment; it is
24                                                                                                     24
     not itself a proof of the independent-bin assumption.
25                                                                                                     25

26                                J.2. Replacement-fee identification                                  26

27                                                                                                     27
       Write D = J W − J F . If witness is installed, replacing it by FVI changes total cost
28                                                                                                     28
     by k − D, where k ≥ 0 is the replacement fee. If FVI is installed, the change is k + D.
29                                                                                                     29
     Thus the two directional gains before fees are D and − D.
30                                                                                                     30

31     C OROLLARY 40—The break-even fee frontier: On an event where D ∈ [l, u], the                    31

32   two directional gains belong to [l, u] and [−u, −l ]. The unknown absolute cost difference        32
     98

1    satisfies                                                                                          1

2                                                                                                       2
                                  | D | ∈ [dist(0, [l, u]), max{|l |, |u|}].                   (106)
3                                                                                                       3

4    For every k ≥ max{0, u, −l } neither replacement strictly lowers expected cost. By contrast,       4

5    k ≥ max{0, l, −u} only removes a certified strictly profitable replacement. The two thresh- 5
6    olds generally differ. Strict inequalities in the first criterion certify that both replacements   6

7    strictly increase cost.                                                                            7

8                                                                                                       8
          P ROOF : Subtract the gain intervals from k and compare the resulting interval
9                                                                                                       9
     endpoints with zero. Taking the image of [l, u] under the absolute-value map gives
10                                                                                                      10
     (106). The assertions hold simultaneously for every nonnegative fee on the original
11                                                                                                      11
     coverage event.                                                                         Q.E.D.
12                                                                                                      12

13     The fee frontier is more informative than a conclusion at one generous charge. 13
14   The earlier 1/64 replacement decision is retained with its original interpretation                 14

15   and confidence account. The new analysis reports the two directional gain inter- 15
16   vals and both thresholds instead of selecting a favorable fee after seeing the data. 16
17   There is no welfare calibration hidden in the units: a decision maker must sup- 17
18   ply the economically relevant charge, or use the full frontier to see what remains                 18

19   unidentified.                                                                                      19

20                                                                                                      20

24                                                                                                      24

25                                                                                                      25
     ous intervals for Db = Jb − Jb0 . The identity interval at b = b0 is exactly [0, 0]. Let
26                                                                                                      26

7               APPENDIX K: J OINT POLICY- COST AND RESOURCE DECISIONS                                      7

8      Let a finite catalogue P contain implemented policies, including incumbents,                         8

9    repaired policies and conventional alternatives. Construction, verification and di- 9
10   rect evaluation are distinct charged activities. Denote their measured deployment- 10
11   relevant resource vector by r j ; the published timing convention must specify                         11

12   whether an evaluation shared by several candidates is a common fixed charge                            12

13   or an attributable marginal charge. In particular, a joint paired evaluation cannot be                 13

14   counted as zero or silently charged only to the preferred generator.                                   14

15     Conditional on construction information, suppose a simultaneous event satis- 15
16   fies Jj ∈ [ L j , Uj ] for every j ∈ P . For nonnegative prices θ, let k j (θ ) be a known             16

17   implementation charge, for example θ · r j plus an information fee. Define                             17

18                                                                                                          18
           bj(θ ) ∈ arg min{Uj + k j (θ )},        R(θ ) = Ubj + kbj (θ ) − min{ L j + k j (θ )}.   (110)
19                                                                                                          19
                       j∈P                                                  j∈P
20                                                                                                          20
     A fixed lexicographic tie rule makes the selection measurable.
21                                                                                                          21

22     P ROPOSITION 42—Actual net-cost frontier: On the simultaneous event, for every                       22

23   nonnegative price vector, including one selected after observing the intervals,                        23

24                                                                                                          24
                               0 ≤ Jbj + kbj (θ ) − min{ Jj + k j (θ )} ≤ R(θ ).
25                                                    j                                                     25

26                                                                                                          26
     A proposed replacement of incumbent i by j is certified nonworsening in net cost whenever
27                                                                                                          27
     a simultaneous upper bound on Jj − Ji , plus k j (θ ) − k i (θ ) and the replacement fee, is
28                                                                                                          28
     nonpositive. Otherwise a rule retaining i remains safe. Policy-specific certificate widths can
29                                                                                                          29
     be displayed alongside this frontier, but do not determine its ordinate.
30                                                                                                          30

31     P ROOF : Bound the selected true cost above by its upper line and the best cata- 31
32   logue cost below by the minimum lower line. All inequalities hold on the same finite                   32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                              101

1    event for every price, so a new price query does not consume another probability al- 1
2    location. The replacement assertion applies the paired upper bound directly. Q.E.D.        2

3                                                                                               3
          Absolute intervals can be considerably wider than centered paired differences.
4                                                                                               4
     For a decision between an incumbent and its final repair, the centered interval is
5                                                                                               5
     therefore the appropriate replacement input. A statistically unresolved interval
6                                                                                               6
     is not evidence of equivalence. In contrast, Corollary 26 supplies a deterministic
7                                                                                               7
     nonincrease, although it does not necessarily certify a strictly positive improvement
8                                                                                               8
     large enough to cover an implementation fee.

10                                                                                              10
                      K.1. Information technology and the price of conservatism
11                                                                                              11

12        An explicit theoretical observation technology gives the bit price units. Suppose     12

13   a device reports a dyadic cell in each coordinate at date t. With b bits per coordinate    13

14   it transmits db bits and requires τdb resource units, where τ is a technological           14

15   conversion factor. At unit resource price w, and with a setup cost Kb , its present cost   15

16   is                                                                                         16

17
                                   Cb (w, τ ) = Kb + wτdb ∑ Bt .                       (111)    17

18                                                        t< T                                  18

19   Each reported cell entails its own feasible action contract. In the retained sensor        19

20   experiment, Kb = 0 and the normalized price λ = wτ takes the declared values. This         20

21   supplies a structural interpretation and a sensitivity variable, not an estimate of        21

22   actual device costs. The dataset contains no empirical measurements from which w           22

23   or τ could be calibrated.                                                                  23

24        Theorem 41 and Proposition 42 apply to the finite bit catalogue with charges (111). 24
25   Their upper-minus-lower envelope is a valid bound on the cost of conservative              25

26   choice relative to the unknown true catalogue minimizer. Replacing that minimizer          26

27   by the lowest observed sample mean and calling it the ex post true optimum is not          27

28   justified. The retained exact price envelopes and their regret bounds answer the           28

29   identified decision question without making this substitution. A richer technology         29

30   with fixed charges, nonlinear bandwidth costs or precision-dependent delays can            30

31   be assessed by changing the known charge functions, but new unobserved policies            31

32   require new cost bounds.                                                                   32
     102

1      A similarly explicit interpretation applies to terminal repair. A controller that                        1

2    incurs cost K to install the additional gate, and q per deployed final action, is worth                    2

3    adopting only when its expected discounted policy gain covers those charges. The                           3

4    published centered gain interval defines the entire break-even range. Its mathe- 4
5    matical safety does not by itself imply economic adoption at positive computation                          5

6    prices.                                                                                                    6

7                                                                                                         7

8                           L.2. Matched services and realized adaptation                                 8

9                                                                                                         9
       The execution-before protocol fixes three generators: compiled witness, uniform
10                                                                                                        10
     multilinear FVI, and curvature-refined coordinate FVI. Every generator forms its
11                                                                                                        11
     own future labels and receives the same state-node count, feasible action fractions
12                                                                                                        12
     and integration bins. For each two-state price–horizon cell the common resource
13                                                                                                        13
     ladder is
14                                                                                                        14

15           ( N, K, M) = (4, 2, 1), (8, 2, 1), (16, 4, 2), (32, 4, 2), (64, 8, 4), (128, 16, 8).         15

16                                                                                                        16

17   The entire ladder is run in three isolated processes per cell and method, rotating                   17

18   method order. This yields 36 two-state services and 216 rungs. State dimension                       18

19   three uses four declared rungs through (32, 8, 4); dimension four uses three through                 19

20   (16, 8, 4), with the witness and uniform generators, both horizons, price one and                    20

21   three repetitions. Those additional 24 services contain 84 rungs. The comparison                     21

22   across dimensions uses the same target five, not a dimension-specific certificate                    22

23   threshold. This is a finite common-accuracy experiment at a coarse tolerance, not                    23

24   evidence of an asymptotic or dimension-free rate.                                                    24

25     The curvature comparator obtains an own-future pilot on a nine-node coordinate                     25

26   grid. For each axis it takes maxima of adjacent exact second differences across the                  26

27   other coordinates. It then bisects the interval maximizing squared length times                      27

28   the largest pilot curvature over that interval, with deterministic leftmost ties. A                  28

29   negligible positive floor permits a defined rule when the pilot is affine; the dominant              29

30   unit baseline of the earlier heuristic is absent. Pilots, allocation and the realized                30

31   axes are stored and charged. Realized nonuniformity, rather than the method’s                        31

32   name, determines whether the experiment supplies an adaptive comparison. This                        32
     104

1    remains a coordinate tensor algorithm; it is not presented as a sparse-grid or general                   1

2    adaptive-cell method.                                                                                    2

3      Each rung starts from primitives. The internal complete-prefix clock includes                          3

4    own-future integration, all earlier failed rungs, pilots, exact compilation, actor                       4

5    construction, all-state certification, deployment checks and durable checkpoints. 5
6    Separate process clocks include startup and the identical non-economic warm-up. 6
7    A single CPU is selected, numerical-library threads are fixed to one, and CPU                            7

8    frequency is not controlled. Peak resident memory includes the process. Primitive                        8

9    query counts are component counts, not complete FLOPs or bit-operation counts. 9
10   Independent policy evaluation has a separate joint service and is never added to an                      10

11   unrelated historical clock.                                                                              11

                        L.3. Actual first-crossing policies and replacement fees
30                                                                                                              30

31     For the four two-state economic cells and each target 4, 2, 1, the protocol selects                      31

32   repetition zero’s first-crossing witness and uniform FVI policies when both attain. 32
     108

1    It also selects the particular favorable R48 target-two pair: T = 3, p = 4, witness at                             1

2    N = 32 and FVI at N = 64. Those old frozen policies are evaluated as an additional                                 2

3    pair; their historical clocks are not reconstructed as part of the new service. Each                               3

4    selected pair is compared under the uniform initial law and point laws at (1/8, 1/8),                              4

5    (1/2, 1/2) and (7/8, 7/8), with full information or eight-bit observations. Finite                                 5

6    observations use robust cell-wide capacity repair and downward action quantum                                      6

7    1/4096.                                                                                                            7

8       Every sampled contrast uses 262,144 common interval paths, 40-bit equal initial                                 8

9    and innovation bins, and outward mean and variance bounds. All new policy-pair                                     9

10   and sensor contrasts share one family of at most 300 with error 1/100 and log                                      10

11   upper bound 13. The independent-bin model supplies the statistical assertion; the                                  11

12   fixed PCG64 streams supply reproducibility. Old R48 intervals retain their separate                                12

13   original confidence event. Simultaneous interpretation of both old and new families                                13

14   is at least 98 percent by a union bound, not automatically 99 percent.                                             14

        For each first-target-one controller, the protocol evaluates every integer preci- 10
11                                                                                                                  11
     sion b = 4, . . . , 16 under the uniform initial law. Each policy is compared with its
12                                                                                                                  12
     own 16-bit implementation; the reference self-comparison is exactly zero. Prices
13                                                                                                                  13
     per coordinate-bit are 1/16384, 1/4096, 1/1024. The deterministic all-state-account
14                                                                                                                  14
     minimizer and the upper-net-cost selector of Theorem 41 use precisely that same
15                                                                                                                  15
     grid. Neither is compared with an unobserved continuous optimum.

     110

1      For each economic cell, the record additionally gives all nonnegative information       1

2    prices by exact lower and upper affine envelopes. A retrospective price query within      2

3    that family is allowed by simultaneous coverage and is not a new experiment. Initial- 3
4    law dependence remains substantive: a uniform-state expected-cost decision need           4

5    not be optimal at a particular initial state, and a conservatively optimal all-state      5

6    bound may rationally purchase more information.                                           6

10                       L.5. The retained evidence and its interpretation                     10

11                                                                                             11

12     The new service does not rewrite the original nonlinear training studies. The           12

13   R42 tighter-target neural attainments, six of twelve scalar and five of six coupled       13

14   services, remain capped-service outcomes; the R44 residual refinements of seven           14

15   frozen failures remain diagnostics, not retraining successes. The 24 direct neural- 15
16   minus-ridge intervals remain unresolved. The R45 constructive comparison still has        16

17   nine of twelve neural and twelve of twelve spline attainments at its tightest target. 17
18   Fixed64 and adaptive precision still certify all 36 historical precision services, with   18

19   adaptive slower in 26 and about 0.98 percent slower in aggregate.                         19

20     The R48 target-one count, twelve of twelve for witness versus six of twelve for         20

21   each FVI method, also remains unchanged. At a relaxed tolerance 1.014 the old             21

22   FVI near misses all attain; the release supplies a complete sensitivity table rather      22

23   than reclassifying those original failures. The new common N = 128 state rung             23

24   uses separately declared action and integration budgets. It is not the unexecuted         24

25   diagonal (128, 128, 128) experiment. The original 48 constrained-policy intervals,        25

26   including six favoring FVI and none favoring witness, remain available with newly         26

27   exposed fee frontiers. Their adverse point-state pattern motivates inspection of          27

28   actual policies; it is not removed by a favorable all-state certificate.                  28

29     The complete development edition, unchanged historical companions and                   29

30   machine-readable preservation map retain all of these results and their proofs. 30
31   This revision adds a controlled resource allocation and actual implementation             31

32   decision, not a selectively favorable replacement record.                                 32

1       APPENDIX M: C OST- DIRECTED CONSTRUCTION AND IMPLEMENTATION                               1

2                                           EVIDENCE                                              2

3                                                                                                 3
       We now execute the distinction between constructing a safe policy and improving
4                                                                                                 4
     its actual economic performance. The new policies use the same nonlinear invest-
5                                                                                                 5
     ment primitives as Section L; neither the price parameter nor the terminal penalty is
6                                                                                                 6
     changed to favor the proposed action rule. All earlier continuation weights, owners,
7                                                                                                 7
     policies, clocks and adverse contrasts retain their historical identities. The recov-
8                                                                                                 8
     ered R50 main experiment and common-accuracy amendment had separate local
9                                                                                                 9
     pre-execution freezes. R51 is an explicitly known-design reproduction, with its own
10                                                                                                10
     Git source identity and runner clocks; the same draws do not provide additional
11                                                                                                11
     independent observations.
12                                                                                                12

13                                                                                                13

14                                                                                                14
              M.1. Prospective resource selection and a residual-driven comparator
15                                                                                                15

16     For the primary block, select powers of two N ∈ {4, 8, 16, 32, 64} and K, M ∈              16

17   {1, 2, 4, 8, 16} before constructing a policy. Among allocations satisfying the prim- 17
18   itive witness budget a/N + b/K + q/M ≤ ε − 10−8 , minimize the exact integer                 18

19   query proxy T ( N + 1)d (K + 1) M, with lexicographic ties. The reserve is checked           19

20   against the realized certificate; it is not a universal fixed-precision theorem. This rule   20

21   supplies an executed version of the separated allocation, rather than a retrospective        21

22   proxy calculation. Conventional FVI receives the same allocation but its own actual- 22
23   modulus certificate. The witness planning formula alone does not guarantee FVI               23

24   attainment.                                                                                  24

25     The catalogue contains seven economic cells: d = 2, 3, 4, T = 2, p = 1, ε = 5, and         25

26   d = 2, T = 2, 3, p = 1, 4, ε = 2. Witness and uniform FVI are each run on all cells. Two     26

27   additional services choose the smallest isotropic power-of-two allocation N = K =            27

28   M satisfying the same primitive target-five budget in d = 2, T = 2, p = 1. A further         28

29   service in that cell uses residual-driven FVI. Each of these seventeen specifications is     29

30   executed three times in separate single-CPU processes, with identical warm-up and            30

31   rotated service order. Repetitions estimate local timing dispersion, not a distribution      31

32   of economic tasks or neural initializations.                                                 32
     112

1      The residual-driven comparator queries its own future Bellman objective at inter- 1
2    val endpoints and midpoints. It inserts coordinate knots where the largest interpola- 2
3    tion surplus across the other coordinates is greatest, then equidistributes square-root   3

4    surplus mass along each axis, with dyadic rounding and explicit monotone-knot             4

5    checks. This last step follows the local quadratic interpolation-error scaling, rather    5

6    than the curvature-mass rule of the earlier graded block. All pilot queries, residual     6

7    comparisons, compilation and output are charged. Recorded geometry, not the               7

8    method name, determines whether adaptation occurred. This is a coordinate-tensor          8

9    comparator, not a claim to have executed the whole adaptive sparse-grid literature. 9
10     The original R50 design and source were frozen locally before their measured            10

11   execution. Its early bisection-only development test and subsequent equidistribu- 11
12   tion correction are retained with their original records. R51 freezes the recovered       12

13   algorithm before a known-design reproduction; it is not presented as a new blind          13

14   experiment. The present clocks belong only to this execution, and the earlier R49         14

15   evidence is unchanged.                                                                    15

     actual certificate fails, double only N, reconstruct from primitives and retain the
27                                                                                                              27
     failed attempt. Stop at first success or at N = 64. Both methods face this rule. There
28                                                                                                              28

17   of the witness incumbent’s actual expected cost. The simultaneous gain lower end- 17
18   points range from 9.4671e-05 to 0.023541; upper endpoints range from 0.0033545 to                              18

22   receive the same repair, it remains higher in 26, is lower in 0, and is unresolved in 1. 22
23   The residual-driven FVI comparison is unresolved both before and after repair. Thus                            23

24   the terminal construction repairs a genuine economic loss without manufacturing a                              24

25   reversal of the strong conventional comparison.                                                                25

26     The active evaluator records 3,670,016 interval paths, 7,340,032 cell-gate queries                           26

27   and 5,447,560 accepted changes across the two incumbents. No acquisition am- 27
28   biguity or broad-hull fallback is recorded. Its joint direct-evaluation time before                            28

29   durable record output is 127.95 seconds. That total is evaluation work, not a speed                            29

30   comparison between independently executed policies.                                                            30

31     For each incumbent the direct reduction is the centered final contrast. The remain- 31
32   ing witness–FVI difference after both repairs is an actual comparison of the two                               32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                 117

1    changed policies. The identity                                                               1

2                                                                                                 2
                      JW − JF = ( JW − JW + ) + ( JW + − JF+ ) − ( JF − JF+ )
3                                                                                                 3

4                                                                                                 4
     separates removable final-action losses from the difference that remains. It does not
5                                                                                                 5
     attribute every remaining discrepancy to a single representation mechanism. The
6
     deposited date-level action means, terminal state means, accepted and blocked pro- 6
7
     posals, and capacity/quantum repair counts permit this distinction to be checked. 7
8                                                                                                 8
     A finite-sample positive gain can justify a strictly positive replacement charge up to
9                                                                                                 9
     its simultaneous lower endpoint; a deterministic nonincrease alone does not justify
10                                                                                                10
     an arbitrary implementation fee.

12                 M.4. Actual-cost frontiers and complete resource boundaries                    12

13                                                                                                13
       The supplement reports every group’s four absolute cost intervals and every
14                                                                                                14
     direct contrast. For each new construction, its resource coordinate includes the full
15                                                                                                15
     own-future service and, separately, the joint evaluation charge. The reader can use
16                                                                                                16
     either a shared fixed evaluation cost for the catalogue decision or the complete
17                                                                                                17
     joint cost required to certify a particular comparison. We do not claim to have
18                                                                                                18
     measured independent marginal costs for four separately simulated policies when
19                                                                                                19
     the implementation shares their earlier trajectories. The pair clock is an explicitly
20                                                                                                20
     charged object, not an unpriced convenience.

     Pair sec. is a shared cost of the whole four-policy comparison, repeated in the display for clarity, not
21                                                                                                                  21
     independently attributable to each row. Construction medians use the same cell and fresh records; no
22   historical clocks are spliced. Absolute-cost intervals are wider than centered pair intervals. Charges enter   22

23   Proposition 42 with their explicitly chosen shared or marginal accounting convention.                          23

24     All stored coefficients, original owners, action scalars, model evaluation cate- 24
25   gories, transform relaxations, interpolation/corner operations, terminal derivative                            25

26   queries, whole-cell tests, exact fallbacks, numerical interval widths, serialized bytes                        26

27   and process peak resident memory remain in the records. The counters are primitive                             27

28   categories, not a complete FLOP or bit-operation count. The latter would require an                            28

29   additional cost model. Direct comparison time is not added to clocks from another                              29

30   host or to a historical failure in order to manufacture a new complete service.                                30

31     The retained sensor catalogue has a structural interpretation through equa- 31
32   tion (111). Its prices remain normalized primitives, and its confidence-envelope                               32
                                 NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                119

1    regret is relative to the best observed implementation in the finite catalogue. This re- 1
2    vision neither estimates a sensor technology nor substitutes a sample-mean winner           2

3    for the true expected-net-cost minimizer.                                                   3

4                                                                                                4

5                M.5. Historical findings that the new evidence does not replace                 5

6                                                                                                6
       The earlier 93 of 96 strictly higher witness-cost contrasts remain visible in Ta-
7                                                                                                7
     ble L.IV. Original curvature-FVI never changed its grid in the realized block. The
8                                                                                                8
     later genuinely graded method also remains an adverse conventional-comparator
9                                                                                                9
     experiment: it was less precise in all twelve matched final services and slower in all
10                                                                                               10
     twelve. These two findings concern the two specified heuristics, not all adaptive
11                                                                                               11
     dynamic programming. The present surplus comparator adds an independently
12                                                                                               12
     recorded realized partition, while uniform FVI remains the strong baseline.
13                                                                                               13
       Likewise, a successful final-action repair does not establish better earlier con-
14                                                                                               14
     tinuation learning, universal neural superiority, a guarantee for unrestricted non-
15                                                                                               15
     convex training, or a dimension-free policy construction. What is established is
16                                                                                               16
     a cost-directed feasible improvement with an explicit safety condition, its exe-
17                                                                                               17
     cuted original-economy specialization, and source-bound resource and actual-cost
18                                                                                               18
     comparisons. These results extend the NBO policy-evaluation and improvement
19                                                                                               19
     program without replacing its economic subject or erasing its previous evidence.
20                                                                                               20

        APPENDIX N: E XHAUSTIVE ROBUSTNESS IN THE ORIGINAL INVESTMENT
22                                                                                               22
                                           ECONOMY
23                                                                                               23

24     The action-null result permits a diagnostic in which the true policy and its eco- 24
25   nomic cost are held fixed while a critic’s irrelevant component changes. This avoids        25

26   confusing a new representation with a new economic policy. Before execution,                26

27   we fixed all eight R49 target-two checkpoint policies retained in the R51 package:          27

28   witness and uniform FVI for every two-state price–horizon cell. We enumerate                28

29   every one of the 2562 closed eight-bit observation cells for each policy. There is no       29

30   selection of cells by favorable cost or by the new results.                                 30

31     For each cell we retain the original robust capacity repair, the action lattice 1/4096,   31

32   and the convex final-date proposal. The centered outward interval for its actual            32
     120

1    conditional cost difference is the same object used in the inherited terminal gate. 1
2    We compare the global-width and contrast-aware gates after replacing g by g − n M ,                    2

3    for M ∈ {0, 1, 16, 4096}. Proposition 33 establishes the zero action-error allowance                   3

4    algebraically; it is not estimated from this grid. Closed-cell enclosures cover bound- 4
5    ary states, while execution retains the original observation tie convention. Symbolic                  5

6    cancellation precedes interval evaluation.                                                             6

29   implementation on every cell. Thus the reported invariance concerns the actual                         29

30   stored policy, not equality of two rounded average costs. The exact finite-state suite                 30

32   finite-sweep theorem.                                                                                  32

1      The experiment measures resistance to irrelevant continuation error. It is not a       1

2    new estimate of J witness − J FVI . The contrast-aware policies are identical to the un- 2
3    perturbed terminal-repaired incumbents, so repeating their cost simulation would         3

4    not create additional independent economic observations. The prior actual-cost           4

5    intervals, their confidence events, and the 93 witness-higher, zero witness-lower        5

6    and three unresolved R49 comparisons remain unchanged. Safe improvement of               6

7    either incumbent remains different from superiority over the competing policy.           7

8      Every service includes input loading, proposals, true-cost interval comparisons,       8

9    independent inherited-policy replay, amplitude decisions and durable cell output. 9
10   Machine-independent query counters, serialization time and peak memory are               10

11   retained separately. The supplementary work table is the cost of this exhaustive         11

12   diagnostic, not the construction cost of an alternative learned model. A full T- 12
13   pass investment-economy execution is not inferred from a final-date ablation. The        13

14   general finite-sweep result has explicit verified-error hypotheses; the continuous       14

15   economic experiment executes only the declared specialization.                           15

16                                                                                            16

17   APPENDIX O: F ULL POLICY SWEEPS IN THE NONLINEAR INVESTMENT ECONOMY                      17

18                                                                                            18
       The directed certificate is now evaluated beyond an exact-null identity and be-
19                                                                                            19
     yond the final decision. This section uses the original continuous-law two-state
20                                                                                            20
     investment economy, with state-dependent capacity, nonlinear costs and transitions,
21                                                                                            21
     and acquired-state implementation. It does not substitute a finite-state model for
22                                                                                            22
     that economy. The construction and direct-cost records were executed in the sep-
23                                                                                            23
     arately frozen R53 primary and adaptive studies; the present revision integrates
24                                                                                            24
     them into the paper and replays their full deposited outputs. That replay adds
25                                                                                            25
     neither new policies nor new independent samples.
26                                                                                            26

27                                                                                            27
                            O.1. Design and the economic endpoint
28                                                                                            28

29     The primary design has horizons T = 2, 3, price parameter p = 1, and two genera- 29
30   tors: the compiled witness construction and tensor FVI. Each service starts from the     30

31   primitives and executes every rung of the original prefix up to the declared check- 31
32   point. It checks that its reconstructed original policy has the same identity as the     32
     122

1    historical checkpoint. A five-bit observation contract in each coordinate then gives      1

2    322 closed cells per date, with downward action spacing 1/4096. Both methods use          2

3    the same acquisition and robust-feasibility rules.                                        3

4      Starting from that complete acquired incumbent, each service executes exactly           4

5    T all-date passes. At every pass all new dates are computed against the same old          5

6    complete incumbent and then adopted together. Each cell has nine robustly feasible        6

7    proposed actions and eight interval covers of the full continuous-action range. 7
8    Sixteen dyadic common-innovation bins enclose every unresolved expectation; the           8

9    last innovation is integrated analytically. Rectangle extrema enclose discontinuous       9

10   future actors. These calculations implement the directed certificate of Section H         10

11   with the verification gauge h = 0. They are not a claim that a zero critic generates      11

12   an effective policy, and their policy-evaluation cost is recorded.                        12

13     The primary numerical endpoint is the actual expected cost of each complete             13

14   returned policy under the original uniform initial law and continuous innovations. 14
15   Conditional on the frozen policies, 65,536 common-path rows per horizon produce           15

16   outward path-cost enclosures using forty-bit continuous-bin cells. The bounded            16

17   empirical-Bernstein account applies to the endpoint samples under the declared            17

18   independent-bin model. Dependence between policies through common paths is                18

19   retained. The primary protocol allocates a simultaneous family error of 1/100; the        19

20   adaptive extension has a separate 1/100 family. Combining the two families requires       20

21   the corresponding union bound, rather than counting their repeated policies as            21

22   independent economic objects.                                                             22

23     The distinction between cumulative gain and comparative ranking remains im- 23
24   portant. In the three-date experiment, the witness policy’s gain after three full         24

25   sweeps lies in [0.00344390, 0.01578829], with the displayed endpoints rounded out- 25
26   ward. Its final witness-minus-FVI interval is [−0.00287355, 0.00923410]. Thus the         26

27   cumulative own-incumbent gain is identified, while the final cross-method dif- 27
28   ference is unresolved. The latter is not evidence of equality and not an identified       28

29   reversal of the original ranking. Individual step gains can be statistically unresolved   29

30   even though the deterministic certificate guarantees nonworsening expected cost at        30

31   every true state.                                                                         31

        A positive interval identifies higher witness cost. An interval containing zero is unresolved, not

26                                                                                                             26

27     For an installed witness controller with a replacement charge τ ≥ 0, let [l, u] be                      27

28   the corresponding cumulative gain interval. The net benefit of replacement is in                          28

29   [l − τ, u − τ ] on the same simultaneous event. Hence every 0 ≤ τ < l is supported                        29

30   as strictly beneficial when l > 0, and every τ > u is ruled out as beneficial. The                        30

31   intermediate region is unresolved. This gives a full adoption-fee account for an                          31

32   actual improved policy, rather than only a reduced certificate for an unchanged                           32
     124

1    policy. These are normalized model units, not an estimated installation charge or a                       1

2    calibrated welfare exercise.                                                                              2

29                                                                                                             29
     The gap is the directed all-state upper bound against the original continuous-action Bellman optimum.
30    It need not decrease monotonically. Prefix work includes primitive construction and previous passes      30

31   through their recorded array outputs; the final service and inference records are charged separately in   31

32                                           the catalogue account.                                            32
                                  NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                  125

1      There are two distinct guarantees. A nonpositive directed upper advantage gives             1

2    safety for an accepted action. The lower interval cover of all feasible actions gives         2

3    the additional accuracy quantity εkt = supC (Utk − Lkt ). The computed recursion is           3

4    then Gtk+1 = β t Gtk+1 + εkt , initialized by a valid support bound. Its terms include cell   4

5    uncertainty, actor ambiguity, action covering, integration and arithmetic through             5

6    their actual endpoint propagation. They are not separately estimated from residual            6

7    samples and then presumed negligible.                                                         7

8      A coarse continuous-action cover or a wide future-actor range can make G con- 8
9    servative. The theorem guarantees that the returned policy does not worsen, not               9

10   that a particular numerical upper bound must decrease at every pass. Executing T              10

11   passes at nonzero tolerances also does not assert exact optimality: the remaining             11

12   gap is the displayed sum of propagated directed errors. The exact T-pass optimality           12

13   statement and its sharp coefficient have their own rational finite-model regression           13

14   tests.                                                                                        14

18                   O.3. A genuinely nonuniform conventional comparison                           18

19                                                                                                 19

20     The extension adds surplus-driven FVI at the same two-date primitive checkpoint. 20
21   Its adaptation history and the number of nonuniform date models are recorded,                 21

22   together with every pilot and every earlier construction rung. It is not the earlier          22

23   curvature rule that left its grid unchanged. Compiled witness and tensor FVI are              23

24   reconstructed on the same extension runner, receive the same two complete passes,             24

25   and reproduce their primary policy identities. A fresh path stream compares all               25

26   three methods and all passes within a separately frozen statistical family.                   26

27     This comparison tests an actual changed conventional representation, rather than            27

28   merely a new label for the tensor baseline. Its adaptive grid nevertheless remains a          28

29   tensor grid with nonuniform coordinates. It is therefore not presented as a sparse- 29
30   grid or non-tensor high-dimensional demonstration. The earlier failed curvature               30

31   rule, the adverse graded refinement, and the original direct-cost comparisons remain          31

32   in their original tables and records.                                                         32

                     O.4. Complete catalogue work at common actual-cost targets
18                                                                                                              18

19     A finite catalogue of returned policies supplies a precise work account. For                             19

20   each method we select the pass with the lowest simultaneous actual-cost upper                              20

21   endpoint. We charge that method’s entire observed construction-and-improvement                             21

22   service, including all executed passes and durable records, plus the entire shared                         22

23   cost-inference service. Charging the full shared service to each eligible method is                        23

24   conservative and avoids assigning an unobserved marginal inference time to a                               24

25   method evaluated only jointly. The separate publication audit reports the additional                       25

26   replay and document-production overhead.                                                                   26

27     For a target u, the eligible methods are exactly those whose selected actual-cost                        27

28   upper bound is at most u. Taking the least recorded complete-catalogue work                                28

29   among eligible methods defines the observed finite frontier. Its breakpoints and                           29

30   method choices are deposited without rounding; the printed table is descriptive. 30
31   This is a frontier for releasing a policy from an already executed finite catalogue. 31
32   It is not a claim about the minimum work of an unexecuted sequential stopping                              32
                                       NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                           127

12                                                                                                                12

19                                                                                                                19
       The work tables separately identify construction time, pair-node counts, coupling
20                                                                                                                20
     work, actor queries, actor-table bytes, process peak memory, and the cumulative
21                                                                                                                21
     clocks. A common-innovation certificate avoids a stored table over all state pairs,
22                                                                                                                22
     but the work bound in Proposition 36 makes its horizon dependence explicit. These
23                                                                                                                23
     records turn a constructive mathematical certificate into an executed and costed ver-
24                                                                                                                24
     ification method in the stated economy. They do not remove the tensor observation
25                                                                                                                25
     cover or establish a representation-specific scaling advantage.
26                                                                                                                26

27                                                                                                                27
                           O.5. Controlled approximate-null and false-null tests
28                                                                                                                28

29     The extension varies three departures from the exact action-null example: the                              29

30   ReLU direction, the deterministic action exposure, and a known action-dependent                              30

31   innovation-mean coefficient. Each departure ranges over 0, 1/4096, 1/256, and 1/16;                          31

32   the amplitudes are 1, 16, and 4096. Two original incumbents yield 384 controlled                             32
     128

1    cases, each evaluated on all 322 closed cells. A verified positive allowance replaces                     1

2    the zero certificate whenever the altered coefficient is nonzero.                                         2

3                                                                                                              3
                                                   TABLE O.VI
4                                                                                                              4
                       C ONTROLLED DEVIATIONS FROM EXACT ACTION - NULL STRUCTURE

                               Quantity                           Recorded value                               6

7                              Controlled cases                                 384                            7

8                              Closed cells per case                           1024                            8

                               Harmful false-null cell–cases                 116185
12                                                                                                             12
                               Maximum contrast allowance                315.00000
13                                                                                                             13
                               New statistical observations                        0
14                                                                                                             14

15    The catalogue changes known ReLU directions, action exposures and action-dependent innovation            15

16         means. Harm means a strictly positive lower true-cost difference for an action selected by the      16
     deliberately false-null rule. Corrected decisions pass the nonpositive true-upper check. Cell–cases are
17                                                                                                             17
                   deterministic repeated diagnostics, not independent economic observations.
18                                                                                                             18

19                                                                                                             19
       The corrected gate and the deliberately false-null gate are both replayed. Every
20                                                                                                             20
     selected corrected action is feasible and has a nonpositive true-advantage upper
21                                                                                                             21
     endpoint. For the false-null policy, a strictly positive true-advantage lower endpoint
22                                                                                                             22
     identifies a harmful decision, not merely an uncertain one. The full catalogue retains
23                                                                                                             23
     accepted, blocked and unchanged actions, including cases where large non-nullity
24                                                                                                             24
     makes the verified gate conservative. These are deterministic diagnostics for known
25                                                                                                             25
     perturbed primitives. They are not additional cost-inference samples, an automatic
26                                                                                                             26
     discovery of learned null structure, or a guarantee under an unknown misspecified
27                                                                                                             27
     transition law.
