29                                                                                                             29
                       APPENDIX P: E CONOMIC SCOPE AND CONCLUSION
30                                                                                                             30

31     The economic contribution is an explicit connection between a policy’s construc- 31
32   tion resources and its implementable cost. A primitive error budget can determine                         32
                                NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                             129

1    whether to refine the state cover, action search or integration. A complete finite     1

2    tolerance frontier states when one generator returns a certified policy earlier. In- 2
3    dependent comparisons of the returned policies then determine what can be said         3

4    about controller replacement and purchased information. These are different steps;     4

5    none can be replaced by the favorable outcome of another.                              5

6      The constructive backend and exact compiler make positive deterministic state- 6
7    ments under explicit moduli and effective-computability conditions. Their guaran- 7
8    tees are not success probabilities for unrelated nonconvex training algorithms. The    8

9    matched experiments and direct-cost intervals make finite, source-bound claims         9

10   about the declared economies, resource ladders and initial laws. Common-accuracy       10

11   dimension cells do not establish an asymptotic scaling law, and endogenous pre- 11
12   cision within a finite sensor catalogue is not an optimum over all information         12

13   technologies. The original broad theory, applications and adverse experimental         13

14   record remain part of the complete NBO development.                                    14

15     The cost-directed result adds a further positive statement: a feasible acquired      15

16   policy can be improved without sacrificing its incumbent cost guarantee, provided      16

17   its centered advantage is verified. In the executed final-stage specialization every   17

18   studied witness incumbent has a strictly positive identified reduction under the       18

19   declared initial law. Most witness–FVI differences remain unfavorable after both       19

20   methods are repaired. The improvement theorem and this comparative observation         20

21   are consistent: safe improvement relative to an incumbent is not dominance over        21

22   another method.                                                                        22

23     Neural Bellman Operators therefore provide a way to organize continuation            23

24   construction, feasible implementation, certification and economic resource choice      24

25   around the policy actually returned. Whether a particular representation or gen- 25
26   erator is preferable is decided by its full certified frontier and actual economic     26

27   consequences, rather than by the name of its function class.                           27

28                                                                                          28

29                                                                                          29
                  APPENDIX Q: S UBMISSION AND PRESERVATION MAP
30                                                                                          30

31     This article and its technical supplement are the active R54 exposition of the       31

32   same paper. Every R49 main-article and supplementary label is retained in its          32
     130

1    corresponding current document. The complete development editions retain the                         1

2    R48 main and supplementary bodies and add the current theory and evidence, with                      2

3    a reading notice distinguishing historical wording from the active empirical account. 3
4    The original R49 sources remain unchanged under preserved/R49; R48 sources                           4

5    and earlier theory/application companions are likewise retained. Preservation is                     5

6    not a new mathematical validation of every historical application.                                   6

7      The local revision contains ordinary sources, both immutable R49 evidence                          7

8    archives, the R48 publication input, newly executed records, generated tables, a                     8

9    point-by-point response and compiled documents. The direct numerical correction                      9

10   retains its original observations and replays the same streams with explicitly out- 10
11   ward cross-policy subtraction. The corrected intervals, not their raw predecessors,                  11

12   govern the active direct-cost tables. Source and evidence identities are checked                     12

13   before a clean offline Git-archive rebuild. The local delivery record distinguishes a                13

14   completed build from a remote publication: no remote branch or commit is claimed                     14

15   without a confirmed push. This limitation is administrative, not a substitute for the                15

16   substantive revised manuscript.                                                                      16

12     The constructive backend supplies a feasible reference without relying on the              12

13   success of nonconvex optimization. A trained backend addresses a different com- 13
14   putational question: whether features fitted to the operator’s own continuation              14

15   targets produce useful candidate actions at a prescribed resource budget. These              15

16   two backends are not identified with one another. In particular, fitting the hidden          16

17   weights below is not compilation of the min-plus construction in Theorem 22.                 17

18     Let the transition have the one-factor form                                                18

20                  Ft ( x, a, Z ) = µt ( x, a) + st ( x, a) Z,   Z ∼ Unif[−r, r ].               20

21                                                                                                21

22   The deterministic map µt may be nonlinear and the state may be multidimensional. 22

17      P ROOF : The scalar random variable w⊤
                                             j st Z is uniform on [− R j , R j ], or is identi-
                                                                                                17

18   cally zero. Integrating its positive part gives the displayed three cases. Linearity of       18

19   expectation gives (117); the affine innovation term has mean zero. Each feature re- 19
20   quires two inner products and a bounded number of scalar operations. The function             20

21   Φ is nondecreasing in each argument on R ≥ 0: in the interior its radius derivative is        21

22   ( R2 − z2 )/(4R2 ) ≥ 0, and outside the interior it is constant in the radius. Endpoint       22

23   evaluation with directed arithmetic, enclosing an uncertain branch rather than                23

24   choosing a favorable branch, therefore preserves containment. Negative output                 24

25   weights must reverse interval endpoints in the usual way.                          Q.E.D.     25

26                                                                                                 26

27     The formula is a property of this neural class under the stated innovation law, not         27

28   a general exact-integration result for arbitrary deep networks or unknown kernels. 28
29   Quadratic critics also have analytic expectations here; tree critics use valid bin            29

30   enclosures. Thus eliminating quadrature for the neural backend is a specific com- 30
31   putational facility, not a claim that conventional approximation necessarily needs            31

32   quadrature. On the active hinge region the equivalent expression (z + R)2 /(4R)               32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                  133

1    avoids subtracting two nearly equal squares. At R = 0 the separate branch is essen- 1
2    tial.                                                                                          2

3       The fitted construction proceeds backward. At each date it draws training states,           3

4    forms a finite feasible action menu, evaluates current cost plus the integrated own            4

5    subsequent critic, minimizes these labels, and fits the next critic. Its training labels       5

6    are not evaluations of the optimal Bellman continuation. Fitted value iteration with           6

7    general function classes has a substantial literature; see Munos and Szepesvári                7

8    (2008) and the tree-based construction of Ernst et al. (2005). Our use of training is          8

9    accompanied by a separate whole-cell verification obligation. No training mean- 9
10   square error is substituted for that obligation.                                               10

11      In the executed neural construction the hidden layer has width 32. All hidden and           11

12   output coefficients receive 120 full-batch Adam updates, followed by a ridge fit of            12

13   the affine and output coefficients on the learned features. Coefficients are rounded           13

14   to 24 binary fractional bits. The verifier evaluates this quantized function, not a            14

15   hypothetical unrounded optimizer. The fitted hidden weights, targets, states, seeds            15

16   and quantized output are retained. A fitted nonlinear network, a quadratic continu- 16
17   ation, an ExtraTrees continuation, and the two original constructive frontends are             17

18   therefore identifiable, distinct candidate generators. Whether their additional pro- 18
19   posals change the deployed policy is an empirical question that we report separately           19

20   from their fitting errors.                                                                     20

21                                                                                                  21

22                                                                                                  22

23                 S.1. Certified action search for the fitted neural continuation                  23

24                                                                                                  24
        The same integrated representation also permits a constructive action search,
25                                                                                                  25
     rather than a second unconverged optimization problem. Fix a reported state in
26                                                                                                  26
     the original economy, so the deterministic next state is µ( x, 0) + γa. Let the feasible
27                                                                                                  27
     deployed actions be {0, ∆, . . . , M∆}, with M∆ no greater than the whole-cell lower
28                                                                                                  28
     capacity. Interpret stored coefficients and the reported state as exact dyadic numbers.
29                                                                                                  29

30                                                                                                  30

31      T HEOREM 44—Algebraic neural action witnesses: For a continuation of the form               31

32   (116), its integrated Bellman objective in the original scalar investment action has at most   32
     134

1    2m interior breakpoints. Between consecutive breakpoints it is a quartic polynomial               1

2                                                                                                      2
                                          4       2
3                                       4a + q2 a + q1 a + q0 ,                                        3

4                                                                                                      4

5    with rational coefficients. An exact minimizer on the deployed action lattice can be found        5

6    among at most 22m + 13 candidate indices, independently of the number of lattice points.          6

7    Construction requires O(md + m2 + m log m) rational arithmetic and comparison opera- 7
8    tions, plus O(m) cubic-root isolation problems at the action precision. Coefficient bit lengths   8

9    and root-isolation work must also be charged. The selected action is an original fitted-critic    9

10   action witness, not a min-plus encoding.                                                          10

11                                                                                                     11

12     P ROOF : For feature j, write its preactivation mean as z j + s j a and its innovation          12

13   radius as R j . When s j ̸= 0, branch changes occur only at (± R j − z j )/s j ; for R j = 0      13

14   these coincide. Retain those breakpoints inside the feasible interval and its two end- 14
15   points. Lemma 43 makes each feature quadratic, affine or zero between breakpoints. 15
16   Adding a2 + 4a4 and the affine part of the critic gives the displayed polynomial. Its             16

17   derivative is 16a3 + 2q2 a + q1 and has at most three real roots in each of at most               17

18   2m + 1 pieces.                                                                                    18

19     Between stationary points and breakpoints the objective is monotone. Hence a                    19

20   lattice minimizer lies immediately to either side of a breakpoint or a stationary point,          20

21   or at a feasible endpoint. Isolate each stationary root in a rational interval of width           21

22   at most ∆/4 and include every index from the floor of its lower endpoint divided by               22

23   ∆ through the ceiling of its upper endpoint divided by ∆. This contributes at most                23

24   three indices per isolated root and contains the required neighboring lattice points. 24
25   The breakpoints and endpoints contribute at most 2(2m + 2) indices. Thus 9(2m + 25
26   1) + 4m + 4 = 22m + 13 is a valid bound. Extra indices and duplicate candidates                   26

27   do not invalidate minimization. Exact rational evaluation and lexicographic value– 27
28   action comparison settle ties.                                                                    28

29     Computing feature coefficients costs O(md), sorting the breakpoints costs                       29

30   O(m log m) comparisons, and literal formation of the polynomial on each piece costs               30

31   O(m2 ) operations. Rational isolation of the finitely many cubic roots terminates at              31

32   positive action precision, including multiple roots. This proves the construction and             32
                                     NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                   135

1    the stated separation between arithmetic counts and bit-dependent root-isolation                  1

2    work.                                                                                    Q.E.D.   2

3                                                                                                      3

4      The solver is implemented with rational root isolation and is checked against                   4

5    exhaustive exact lattice minimization, including saved fitted critics from each of                5

6    the three state dimensions. It minimizes the stored critic’s objective at the reported            6

7    state. Whole-cell safety still requires the true-advantage certificate; critic optimality         7

8    alone does not justify deployment. The recorded economic experiment used its                      8

9    prospectively fixed 17-action construction menu. Its policies and measured clocks                 9

10   are not retroactively replaced by this new action solver, and its cost intervals are              10

11   not attributed to untested replacement policies.                                                  11

1      P ROOF : For every innovation in a bin, the next state belongs to one of the                 1

       This construction stores one-state bands rather than a table over state pairs.
10                                                                                                  10
     It sacrifices dependence between two trajectories when taking separate hulls; in
11                                                                                                  11
     particular, actor ambiguity and broad residual ranges can make it conservative.
12                                                                                                  12
     For locally Lipschitz critics, a centered residual evaluation can be intersected with
13                                                                                                  13
     the natural interval. A valid generalized-gradient enclosure suffices for the line-
14                                                                                                  14

15                                                                                                  15

16                                                                                                  16

30     P ROPOSITION 47—Signed reference certificate: The interval G0 encloses ∇ J00                 30

31   throughout X0 . For a nonnegative proposed action a, enclose the gradient of the remain- 31
32   ing zero policy at every state µ( x, θa) + sZ, 0 ≤ θ ≤ 1. If [v− , v+ ] encloses the average   32

1    projection E[γ⊤ ∇ Jt0+1 ] over that segment, then                                                   1

4                                                                                                        4
     The signed interval may be intersected with Proposition 46. The construction is valid for the
5                                                                                                        5
     complete zero reference; it does not differentiate a subsequently changed, cellwise constant
6                                                                                                        6
     policy.

8      P ROOF : Differentiate the finite fixed-policy recursion. On the invariant compact                8

9    domain, the derivatives are bounded, so differentiation and the finite conditional                  9

10   expectations may be interchanged. For every shock path the chain rule has the                       10

11   enclosed Jacobian form in (121); backward induction and expectation preserve the                    11

12   enclosure. The fundamental theorem of calculus along the first action segment gives                 12

       P ROPOSITION 48—Reference stability and computational work: In the dimension-
21                                                                                                       21
     normalized investment family, for every d ≥ 2,
22                                                                                                       22

23                                                                                                       23
                  ∥ Dx µ∥∞ ≤ κ := 11/16,             ∥∇c(·, 0)∥1 ≤ 15/2,         ∥∇ g∥1 ≤ 10.
24                                                                                                       24

25   Consequently, with r stages remaining,                                                              25

26                                                                                                       26
                                  15 r−1                         1920
27                 Lip∞ ( Jr0 ) ≤    ∑
                                   2 j =0
                                          ( βκ ) j + 10( βκ )r ≤
                                                                  91
                                                                      ,           β = 15/16.     (123)   27

28                                                                                                       28

29                                                                                                       29
     For a partition with N leaves, A candidate and covering intervals per leaf, and q innovation
30                                                                                                       30
     bins, the literal cached range-query implementation uses
31                                                                                                       31

32                                                  O( TAqN 2 d)                                         32
     140

1    cell-membership work, in addition to primitive and critic evaluation. Signed reference tubes   1

2    over a full sweep use O( ANqdT 2 ) work and can stream their innovation children. Their        2

3    working state storage is O( NdT ) in addition to stored critics, cached bands and retained     3

4    O( TAN ) endpoint records. No q T recursion or full state-pair table is required.              4

5                                                                                                   5

6      P ROOF : In row j, the only state derivatives of the drift are 1/2 + (1 − x j+1 )/16         6

7    and 1/8 − x j /16, both nonnegative. Their sum is at most 11/16. The target, cyclic            7

8    imbalance and shortage terms have gradient ℓ1 bounds 5/2, 1 and 4 for current                  8

9    cost; the terminal target bound is 5. This gives the stated constants. The chain rule          9

10   yields Lr ≤ 15/2 + βκLr−1 with L0 ≤ 10. Its geometric expansion and βκ = 165/256               10

11   prove (123).                                                                                   11

12     A queried next-state box is tested against N leaves in d coordinates. There are              12

13   O( TANq) such root-bin queries. The backward cache therefore has the displayed                 13

14   membership cost. Each gradient tube with r remaining dates visits O( Nr ) state                14

15   rows and O( Ndr ) Jacobian coordinates for one first-innovation bin. Summing r                 15

16   across current dates gives O( ANqdT 2 ). Streaming bins requires storage for one               16

17   forward tube and its backward gradients, not all future shock combinations. Q.E.D.             17

18                                                                                                  18

19     The stability constant is a bound for this smooth reference, not a dimension- 19
20   free complexity theorem for solving arbitrary dynamic programs. Resolving a                    20

21   desired policy or all-state accuracy may require the leaf count, network width and             21

22   action cover to grow. The membership bound explicitly charges a quadratic leaf- 22
23   count dependence. The executed locally split partition removes a tensor-product                23

24   representation; it does not prove that a fixed 32-leaf partition resolves every eight- 24
25   dimensional economic policy.                                                                   25

26                                                                                                  26

27                                                                                                  27
                              T.3. What a tighter interval establishes
28                                                                                                  28

29     For fixed candidates, let Ct (C ) be the greater of Lt (C ) and the minimum of their         29

30   lower endpoints, including the incumbent’s zero. Then                                          30

31                                                                                                  31

32                  U − L = (U − C ) + ( C − L ) ,      U − C ≥ 0,     C − L ≥ 0.          (124)    32
                                 NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                 141

1    The first term measures uncertainty remaining between candidate lower informa- 1
2    tion and the selected upper bound; the second measures the additional continuous- 2
3    cover allowance. Their maxima need not add to the maximum of their sum. Actor               3

4    ambiguity, acquisition width, innovation enclosures and arithmetic can enter both           4

5    terms, so these are not separately estimated causal error components.                       5

6      More detailed refinement can be recorded without such a causal interpretation. 6
7    Keep the candidate actions fixed, replace each candidate upper bound by the                 7

8    minimum of its old and refined bounds, and replace the global lower bound by                8

9    the maximum of its old and refined bounds. Then Ur ≤ Ur−1 , Lr ≥ Lr−1 , and the             9

10   certificate improvement at step r is exactly                                                10

11                                                                                               11

12
                 (Ur−1 − Lr−1 ) − (Ur − Lr ) = (Ur−1 − Ur ) + ( Lr − Lr−1 ) ≥ 0.                 12

13                                                                                               13
     State subdivision, action-cover subdivision, innovation refinement and higher-
14                                                                                               14
     precision arithmetic can each supply an independent valid refinement. The present
15                                                                                               15
     execution records the cache-to-signed-tube refinement and both terms of (124); it
16                                                                                               16
     does not label an unexecuted six-way refinement experiment as measured evidence.
17                                                                                               17

18                                                                                               18
     APPENDIX U: P ROSPECTIVE ECONOMIC STOPPING AND LEARNED STRUCTURE
19                                                                                               19

20     A policy certificate determines whether a proposed change is safe at every state. 20
21   An economic stopping rule asks when the returned complete policy has attained a             21

22   specified expected-cost target under the initial law. Neither question is answered by       22

23   choosing the best recorded policy after all computation has finished.                       23

24                                                                                               24

25                               U.1. A first-attainment service                                 25

26
       Fix an installed policy π 0 , a target q ∈ (0, 1), a finite resource ladder, and cumula- 26
27                                                                                               27
     tive sample sizes at each stage. At a stage, construct a critic from primitives, transfer
28
     the old policy exactly to any refined partition, and execute a verified simultane- 28
29                                                                                               29
     ous sweep. Freeze the resulting complete policy before drawing that stage’s fresh
30                                                                                               30
     evaluation stream. At each declared look form a confidence interval for
31                                                                                               31
                                                πk       π0
32                                    Dk,q = EJ0 − qEJ0 .                               (125)    32
     142

1    Return the policy at the first nonpositive upper endpoint. A strictly positive lower                 1

2    endpoint ends the current stage and advances to the next resource stage. An unre- 2
3    solved interval leads to the next declared look. Exhausting the last stage returns the               3

4    safe incumbent and an explicit budget-exhaustion status. It is not a conclusion that                 4

5    the target is unattainable.                                                                          5

6                                                                                                         6

7      T HEOREM 49—Validity at prospective return: Suppose each accepted sweep satisfies                  7

8    Theorem 45. At each declared stage and look, assume the interval for (125) has conditional           8

9    failure probability at most αk,l given the information preceding the stage’s fresh evaluation        9

10   stream. If ∑k,l αk,l ≤ α, then, with probability at least 1 − α, every successful return satisfies   10

11   its stated expected-cost target. Every returned incumbent, including one returned after              11

12   budget exhaustion, is statewise no worse than the original installed policy. No independence         12

13   between different methods or different looks is required for the union bound. Each within- 13
14   stage endpoint sample must satisfy its stated conditional sampling assumptions.                      14

15                                                                                                        15

16     P ROOF : Condition on the history preceding each executed stage. Its frozen pol- 16
17   icy and fresh independent-bin stream satisfy the fixed-policy interval guarantee. 17
18   Integrating conditional failure probabilities and taking the union over the prespec- 18
19   ified potential looks gives a simultaneous event of probability at least 1 − α. The                  19

20   first-crossing rule selects one already covered interval on this event, so its nonposi- 20
21   tive upper endpoint implies (125) is nonpositive. Unexecuted later looks need not                    21

22   be computed. Statewise safety follows by composition of the deterministic sweep                      22

23   inequalities and exact policy transfer, independently of the economic inference                      23

24   event.                                                                                    Q.E.D.     24

25                                                                                                        25

26     The theorem also permits a countable predetermined ladder with summable                            26

27   failure allowances. The experiment uses the finite ladder specified below, not an                    27

28   unimplemented anytime extension. A process clock begins before construction and                      28

29   ends after its durable output. Failed stages, own-policy evaluation and inference are                29

30   charged to that service. A method reaching a target early must not be charged for                    30

31   hypothetical later stages, and a method failing early must not have its completed                    31

32   work omitted.                                                                                        32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                143

1      For the implemented bounded-sample account, each path is enclosed over its                  1

2    complete forty-bit initial and innovation cells. The last innovation is integrated            2

3    analytically. The interval in Theorem 39 applies at each declared look, using the             3

4    known support and outward sample moments. The new family limit is 2,048                       4

5    intervals with logarithm upper bound 14 and error 1/100; the planned maximum                  5

6    is 1,188 intervals. Earlier looks reuse prefixes within a stage and are covered by            6

7    the same finite union. Distinct stages use fresh streams. A fixed pseudorandom                7

8    seed establishes reproducibility under the declared independent-bin model, not                8

9    mathematical independence of a deterministic sequence.                                        9

10                                                                                                 10
       C OROLLARY 50—Economic adoption at return: Suppose the returned policy has
11                             0
                                                                                                   11
     cumulative gain G = EJ0π − EJ0π ∈ [l, u] on the simultaneous event. For every replacement
12                                                                                                 12
     charge τ ≥ 0, computation price λ ≥ 0, and recorded complete work W ≥ 0, its net gain
13                                                                                                 13
     belongs to
14                                                                                                 14

15                                 [l − τ − λW, u − τ − λW ].                                      15

16                                                                                                 16
     Thus τ + λW < l certifies profitable adoption and τ + λW > u rules out profitable adoption.
17                                                                                                 17
     These comparisons hold simultaneously over the entire nonnegative price plane on the
18                                                                                                 18
     original event.
19                                                                                                 19

20     P ROOF : Subtract the same known quantity τ + λW from both endpoints of the                 20

21   gain enclosure. The assertion is pathwise on the simultaneous event, so choosing              21

22   prices after observing recorded work requires no extra confidence allocation. Q.E.D.          22

23                                                                                                 23

24
       This is a decision at return, not a claim that the executed relative-cost stopping          24

25
     rule optimizes all possible prices or future computation schedules. The units of              25

26
     λ must convert recorded work into the model’s cost units. In an uncalibrated                  26

27
     theoretical economy the price plane is an explicit economic sensitivity analysis, not         27

28
     an estimated welfare magnitude.                                                               28

29                                                                                                 29
                          U.2. Learning an approximately null direction
30                                                                                                 30

31     Suppose the transition is µt ( x, 0) + Ba + sZ with unknown B ∈ [0, 1]d , known             31

32   nonlinear drift and known bounded additive action-invariant innovation law. Fit               32
     144

1    a direction vb on training observations. Independent validation observations Yi                     1

2    estimate B coordinatewise; each coordinate has mean Bj and range length at most                     2

3    w. Let the recorded mean enclosure be [m j , m j ] and let δbin cover numerical bin                 3

4    displacement. Form                                                                                  4

5                                                                                                        5
               d     h                                        i             q

              j =1

8    Only validation data, the fitted direction and the declared parameter prior enter the               8

9    gate.                                                                                               9

10                                                                                                       10

       T HEOREM 51—Confidence-qualified action-null correction: For a finite collection                  11

12
     of H validation boxes, each with at most dmax coordinates, 2Hdmax e−ℓ ≤ α B gives simulta- 12

13
     neous coverage of their true exposures with probability at least 1 − α B . On this event, for       13

14
     every fitted direction, amplitude M, and nonnegative feasible action pair,                          14


                         |( PBa − PBb ) M(vb⊤ y + c)+ | ≤ | M| sup |vb⊤ B′ | | a − b|.
15                                                                                                       15
                                                                                                (127)
16                                                            B′ ∈K N                                    16

17                                                                                                       17
     A whole-cell critic-advantage upper bound evaluated uniformly over the same box, plus
18                                                                                                       18
     the discounted right-hand side of (127), is a valid upper bound for the corresponding true
19                                                                                                       19
     advantage with the nuisance removed. Accepting only when that bound is nonpositive
20                                                                                                       20
     preserves safety simultaneously for all tested amplitudes and actions. The simplification is
21                                                                                                       21
     rejected whenever its uncertainty allowance prevents the gate from passing.
22                                                                                                       22

23     P ROOF : The bounded-coordinate inequality of Hoeffding (1963) and a union                        23

24   bound give coverage of (126); numerical mean and bin allowances preserve con- 24
25   tainment. On that event, use a common innovation to couple the two actual kernels. 25
26   Their next-state difference is B( a − b). The positive-part map is one-Lipschitz, so its            26

27   projected difference is bounded by |vb⊤ B| | a − b| for every realization. Integration              27

28   proves (127). The box event controls all linear projections, not merely a preselected               28

29   direction. Uniformly enclosing the rest of the critic advantage over that same param- 29
30   eter set prevents an unobserved true-parameter oracle from entering the remaining                   30

31   calculation. Subtracting a nuisance difference cannot increase the upper bound by                   31

32   more than its absolute allowance.                                                         Q.E.D.    32
                                 NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                              145

1      The experiment uses w = 1/2, ℓ = 14, 27 validation boxes and at most eight             1

2    coordinates, giving error at most 1/200. The direction is fitted on 256 observations     2

3    and then quantized; the exact stored direction is used in (127). Validation sizes are    3

4    512, 4,096 and 32,768. The guarantee concerns an unknown action-exposure vector          4

5    in a maintained structural model. It does not infer an arbitrary unknown transition      5

6    law, and coverage failures must remain in the evidence rather than being discarded       6

7    by reference to the simulator’s known truth.                                             7

8      An empty validation box invalidates the parameter qualification and rejects the        8

9    simplified certificate. It cannot authorize a policy change.                             9

12      APPENDIX V: P ROSPECTIVE RETURNS IN THE NONLINEAR INVESTMENT                          12

13                                        ECONOMY                                             13

14                                                                                            14
                                         V.1. Design
15                                                                                            15

16     Tasks are (d, T ) = (2, 2), (4, 4), (8, 6). Each method/task/target combination is     16

17   a separate from-primitives service. Targets q = 9/10 and 49/50 require ten- and          17

18   two-percent expected-cost reductions relative to installed zero investment. The          18

19   stopping statistic is the signed contrast J (π ) − qJ (0), not a ratio of plug-in mean   19

20   estimates. Two resource stages use respectively 256 and 1,024 training states, 32 and    20

21   128 non-tensor leaves, and four and eight innovation bins. Actions are downward          21

22   quantized at 1/4096 using the lower capacity over the complete leaf. Partition           22

23   geometry is chosen before the generator and is nested between stages.                    23

24     Trained ReLU, quadratic and ExtraTrees fitted-value methods use their own              24

25   future targets. The neural architecture and training are described in Section S. 25
26   ExtraTrees uses 16 trees, depth at most eight and minimum leaf size three; quadratic     26

27   FVI fits a ridge-regularized quadratic. The original two-dimensional frontends           27

28   are reconstructed at N = 8, 16, K = 4 and M = 4. Every method receives nine              28

29   common robust candidate actions and one fitted proposal, as well as eight intervals      29

30   covering the full continuous feasible action set. The new algebraic neural action        30

31   solver is evaluated separately on saved critics; it was not substituted for the frozen   31

32   experimental construction menu.                                                          32
     146

1      After one complete simultaneous sweep at a stage, inference uses cumulative             1

2    looks of 4,096, 16,384 and 65,536 interval paths. It stops on the first covered target    2

3    crossing, advances a stage at a strictly positive lower target contrast or exhausted      3

4    look budget, and never executes a later stage after success. All computation, in- 4
5    cluding failed stages and each method’s own inference, is retained. Three isolated        5

6    sequential processes repeat identical training and inference seeds, with rotated          6

7    method order, a fixed available CPU core and one numerical-library thread. These          7

8    measure reproducibility and clock variation, not a population of independently            8

9    trained networks. Clock frequency is recorded where available but not controlled.         9

10                                                                                             10

11                                                                                             11
                     V.2. The refinement and the complete returned policies
12                                                                                             12

13     The first cohort uses the residual/value cache. Its complete 66-process record          13

14   contains 39 target attainments and 27 budget exhaustions. In the eight-dimensional        14

15   task it makes no policy change. The separate sensitivity extension was motivated by       15

16   that completed result and frozen before its own production and fresh inference. It        16

17   retains the same targets, fitted constructions and common actions, while intersecting     17

18   the old cache comparison with the signed reference interval of Proposition 47. This       18

19   chronology matters: the extension is not described as prespecified before observing       19

20   the primary cohort.                                                                       20

21     All 66 signed-reference services attain their targets at the first resource stage. 21
22   This means one complete all-date sweep on 32 leaves, not T exact sweeps and not           22

23   attainment of the Bellman optimum. For the neural eight-dimensional, six-date             23

24   controller, the changed leaf counts by date are (26, 24, 24, 20, 14, 0). Its identified   24

25   cost reduction is generated by nonterminal decisions; it is not a terminal-repair         25

26   experiment relabeled as a long-horizon result. At the ten-percent target its cumula- 26
27   tive gain interval is [0.21452658, 0.29774911], with endpoints rounded outward. The       27

28   complete expected cost interval is [1.42162445, 1.47873990].                              28

29     By Corollary 50, the eight-dimensional return supports every replacement-and- 29
30   computation charge below its lower gain endpoint, in the model’s normalized units. 30
31   A charge above its upper gain endpoint rules out profitable adoption. The interval        31

32   between them remains unresolved. This translates the returned policy into a full          32

29   tensor-FVI returns coincide. Thus the policy improvement is not evidence that the                          29

30   neural generator dominates these alternatives. At the ten-percent target, quadratic                        30

31   FVI has the lowest recorded median complete-process work in each task. The                                 31

32   neural backend supplies a genuinely trained nonlinear continuation and analytic                            32
     150

1    integration, but those facilities must not be confused with an identified economic                          1

2    ranking.                                                                                                    2

3      The clocks include imports, primitive construction, critic fitting, acquired-policy                       3

4    preparation, verification, own inference, serialization and durable outputs. Method                         4

5    comparisons are within each task’s controlled runner; clocks from different task                            5

6    runners are not a hardware-controlled scaling regression.                                                   6

       An exact action witness has a definite but limited meaning: it removes optimiza-
8                                                                                                                     8
     tion error for the stored continuation at a reported state. The next result identifies

10                                                                                                                    10
     plemented economic policy. It also isolates a comparison that can be made without
11                                                                                                                    11

31   below U0 (C ). Both returned complete policies are statewise safe relative to the reference.   31

32   These inequalities alone do not order their actual expected costs.                             32
     156

1      P ROOF : Taking a minimum over a larger finite set proves the first inequalities. 1
2    The valid full-action lower bound is no greater than every feasible action’s ad- 2
3    vantage and hence no greater than either selected upper bound. This proves the          3

4    nonnegative gap inequalities and the strictness criterion. Safety follows from Theo- 4
5    rem 45. To see the last distinction, consider a single state and date, an incumbent     5

6    of cost 2, an old candidate of cost 1 with advantage enclosure [−1, −1/2], and a        6

7    new candidate of cost 5/4 with exact advantage −3/4. The new candidate has the          7

8    smaller upper endpoint and is adopted, although its cost is higher than that of         8

9    the old candidate. Both improve the incumbent. A lower cover of −1 makes both           9

10   certificates valid.                                                           Q.E.D.    10

12     In the new experiment the ReLU-exact mode retains every ReLU-menu action              12

13   and adds the algebraic witness. Both use the same fitted coefficients at a given        13

14   construction attempt and a critic-independent smooth-reference verifier. Thus the       14

15   corollary is an observed, testable comparison rather than a representation claim. 15
16   Exact center-objective regret, stricter upper endpoints, changed deployed actions       16

17   and independent expected-cost differences are reported separately.                      17

20                                                                                           20
       Let [l, u] cover J (π1 ) − J (π0 ) on a simultaneous event after both policies have
21                                                                                           21
     been selected. If adopting π1 rather than π0 incurs an additional replacement
22                                                                                           22
     charge τ and measured computational work difference ∆W, its net cost difference
23                                                                                           23
     at computation price λ ≥ 0 lies in
24                                                                                           24

25                                [l + τ + λ∆W, u + τ + λ∆W ].                       (136)   25

26                                                                                           26

27   A negative upper endpoint supports adoption; a positive lower endpoint rules it         27

28   out on the same event. Between those regions the comparison is unresolved. This         28

29   affine transformation is valid for every declared nonnegative price on that event. It   29

30   does not turn a normalized benchmark into an estimated installation fee. It links       30

31   a particular fitted-action computation to the same economic policy and resource         31

32   question as the original NBO construction.                                              32

1    Hence the function                                                                            1

                                z 7−→ Φ R (z + s j a) − Φ R (z + s j b)
3                                                                                                  3

4    is Lipschitz with constant at most min{1, |s j ( a − b)|/(2R)}. For R = 0 the difference      4

5    of the two hinge derivatives is bounded in absolute value by one almost everywhere,           5

10   depend on x. Summing the ridge contributions and discounting proves (137).                    10

       These formulas connect feature geometry to acquired-state accuracy without
18                                                                                                 18
     assuming that training discovers a null direction. They complement the confidence-
19                                                                                                 19
     qualified learned-direction result: estimated primitive coefficients must still range
20                                                                                                 20
     over the corresponding confidence set. In the present known-law action-search
21                                                                                                 21
     experiment, the direct signed reference enclosure determines adoption and the full-
22                                                                                                 22
     action lower cover determines the displayed Bellman error. The primitive moduli
23                                                                                                 23
     supply an additional certified account, not a retroactive claim that their conservative
24                                                                                                 24
     sum was the stopping criterion.

26                                                                                                 26
              APPENDIX X: E XECUTING FITTED NEURAL ACTION WITNESSES
27                                                                                                 27

28     The preceding study establishes a prospective economic return and a non-tensor              28

29   verification procedure. Its candidate-attribution audit also identifies a necessary           29

30   next comparison: the common action menu often determines the same policy re- 30
31   gardless of the fitted generator. The algebraic solver in Theorem 44 was tested there         31

32   as a solver, not used to create the policies whose costs were reported. We now                32
                                 NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                               159

1    execute that solver in new prospective services and evaluate the resulting policies        1

2    on new streams. The original investment laws, Bellman optimum and NBO research             2

3    object are unchanged.                                                                      3

4                                                                                               4

5                          X.1. A controlled action-search comparison                           5

6                                                                                               6
       The new protocol fixes the three tasks (d, T ) = (2, 2), (4, 4), (8, 6) before produc-
7                                                                                               7
     tion. It compares five modes. Common-only uses the nine robust actions without
8                                                                                               8
     fitting a critic. ReLU-menu uses a genuinely fitted 32-feature continuation and adds
9                                                                                               9
     its 17-menu proposal. ReLU-exact retains every one of those candidates and adds
10                                                                                              10
     the exact fitted-critic lattice minimizer. Quadratic-exact supplies the analogous exact
11                                                                                              11
     action search for its fitted quadratic continuation. ExtraTrees-menu supplies its own-
12                                                                                              12
     future tree-based proposal. All modes receive the same exact terminal-action search,
13                                                                                              13
     so an analytic terminal improvement is not assigned to neural representation.
14                                                                                              14
       The nonterminal verification rule is identical across generators: signed sensi-
15                                                                                              15
     tivity of the installed zero-investment policy from Proposition 47. Its state boxes,
16                                                                                              16
     innovation bins and primitive arithmetic do not depend on the fitted critic. Shared
17                                                                                              17
     candidate actions therefore have identical upper endpoints. This design implements
18                                                                                              18
     the premise of Corollary 53, rather than changing the candidate generator and its
19                                                                                              19
     error certificate simultaneously.
20                                                                                              20
       Each mode is reconstructed independently for each task, target and one of three
21                                                                                              21
     distinct training seeds. ReLU-menu and ReLU-exact use the same seed within
22                                                                                              22
     an attempt, but neither loads the other’s fit free of charge. Their fitted-parameter
23                                                                                              23
     identities are checked. Different seeds produce different fitted neural objects. The
24                                                                                              24
     three seeds are not a population of economic tasks, and their timing variation is not
25                                                                                              25
     described as a controlled timing-only repetition. The preceding fixed-seed timing
26                                                                                              26
     repetitions remain a separate experiment.
27                                                                                              27

28                                                                                              28
                               X.2. Prospective economic targets
29                                                                                              29

30     The two targets require ten- and twenty-percent expected-cost reductions from            30

31   the installed zero policy. Each target service starts from primitives. It has two          31

32   declared construction attempts, with training rows 256, 1024, acquired leaves 32, 64,      32
     160

1    and innovation bins 4, 8. The same locally split non-tensor observation geometry          1

2    and downward action spacing 1/4096 apply to every mode.                                   2

3      At each attempt a complete candidate is certified against the installed policy          3

4    and frozen before inference. Intermediate attempts are not deployed. In particular,       4

5    refinement does not apply the smooth-reference derivative formula to an already           5

6    changed discontinuous actor. Every attempt is safe relative to the installed zero         6

7    policy; no ordering between the policies of different attempts is assumed.                7

8      The own-service stopping estimand is J (π ) − qJ (0), with q = 9/10 or 4/5, rather      8

9    than a ratio of two estimated means. Its cumulative inference looks have 4096, 16384      9

10   and 65536 paths. A nonpositive upper endpoint returns immediately. A positive             10

11   lower endpoint advances the construction attempt; an unresolved interval advances         11

12   the declared look. After the final unsuccessful attempt the service returns a budget- 12
13   exhausted safe policy, not a claim that the target is impossible. Every attempted         13

14   fit, exact search, verification, inference and durable output belongs to the service’s    14

15   surrounding process clock.                                                                15

16     There are 90 separately executed target services. After each set of five modes has      16

17   returned, a separate 65,536-path stream compares ReLU-exact with each other re- 17
18   turned policy. This stream is independent of construction and stopping information        18

19   under the declared independent-bin model. It evaluates the original continuous            19

20   initial and innovation laws using forty-bit bin enclosures and analytic final integra- 20
21   tion. Negative path differences are retained. Full policy identities receive exact zero   21

22   contrasts; other overlapping intervals are not treated as identities.                     22

23     The new finite confidence family has error at most 1/100. Its maximum possible          23

24   interval count is                                                                         24

25                                                                                             25

26                            90 · 2 · 3 · 3 + 18 · 4 = 1692 < 2048,                           26

27                                                                                             27

28   covering own cost, target contrast and gain at every possible look, together with         28

29   the independent validation contrasts. The retained logarithm allowance is checked         29

30   by exact rational arithmetic. The first-attainment argument of Theorem 49 applies         30

31   conditionally at every fresh stage. This finite-look account is not an undeclared         31

32   confidence sequence valid at arbitrary future sample sizes.                               32

26     The common-only control pays no fitting cost and is a substantive comparator. 26
27   Quadratic-exact receives the same algebraic-search opportunity, while ExtraTrees                             27

28   retains its declared fitted-value construction. This prevents the exact solver’s avail- 28
29   ability from being conflated with a special verification privilege. The experiment                           29

30   compares the complete returned procedures and identifies their components; it                                30

31   does not presume that fitting a neural continuation must beat a common-action or                             31

32   conventional-value alternative.                                                                              32
     164

1                         X.4. Work, uniform accuracy and adoption                            1

2                                                                                             2

3                                                                                             3

4      The main table reports complete own-target process work at a matched economic          4

5    target. It includes failed intermediate attempts and all inference actually needed to    5

6    return. The supplementary work ledger separately records fitting, root isolation,        6

7    candidate evaluation, signed verification, path evaluation, serialized bytes and         7

8    resident memory. Different tasks run on different task runners; within each task pro- 8
9    cesses are sequential, use one available core and one numerical-library thread, and      9

10   rotate mode order. Available governor information is recorded, but clock frequency       10

11   is not controlled.                                                                       11

12     The fresh paired validation is additional study work. Its recorded timer ends          12

13   after endpoint evaluation and serialization, before the final comparison JSON is         13

14   committed. It is therefore reported as a partial validation-service clock, not a com- 14
15   plete process clock, and is not concealed inside the own-target time. The protocol’s     15

16   intended complete comparison-process timing was not implemented; this timing             16

17   boundary does not change the prospective stopping rule or its fully recorded own- 17
18   service work. The raw timing records are retained without retrospective adjustment. 18
19     Every returned policy also receives a one-sweep all-state loss account. At date t it   19

20   includes the old-reference continuation term β t Et0+1 as well as the directed greedy    20

21   allowance. The tables use a valid support bound for that reference error and cap         21

22   the result by the original total-cost support. They do not substitute the local U − L    22

23   gap for loss against the Bellman optimum. A successful expected-cost target under        23

24   the initial law is thus not mislabeled as exact Bellman convergence.                     24

25     The gain from replacing installed zero investment remains an actual economic           25

26   gain, subject to the stated confidence event and normalized primitives. Choosing         26

27   between two returned methods requires the additional incremental-cost calculation        27

28   (136). It charges the measured work difference and any replacement fee, and uses         28

29   the fresh paired policy-cost interval. The complete fee–computation-price regions        29

30   follow from that interval without choosing a favorable fee after seeing the outcome. 30
31   The experiment is a theoretical investment exercise, not an externally calibrated        31

32   welfare estimate.                                                                        32

1      APPENDIX Y: L OSSLESS SEARCH FOR TRAINED NEURAL ACTION WITNESSES                                1

2                                                                                                      2
       The fitted-witness transfer result separates an optimization error from the errors
3                                                                                                      3
     in acquisition, continuation evaluation and full-action verification. An exact solver
4                                                                                                      4
     eliminates the first error, but exactness alone does not make the solver economical.
5                                                                                                      5
     We now exploit the learned features to reduce the work of returning precisely the
6                                                                                                      6
     same action witness. The continuation, economic primitives and original Bellman-
7                                                                                                      7
     accuracy target remain unchanged.
8                                                                                                      8

10   Compare the original objective exactly on S, choosing the smallest index on a tie. 10
11   The weak inequality in the definition of S is essential. Floating-point endpoints            11

12   reject a point only when they prove that it cannot minimize the original objective;          12

13   they never supply an approximate answer in place of the exact comparison.                    13

       T HEOREM 55—Lossless neural action search: Suppose the intervals in (141) are
15                                                                                                15
     finite, ordered and valid. Then S contains every exact lattice minimizer. Exact rational
16                                                                                                16
     comparison on S, with the same smallest-index convention, returns exactly the witness of
17                                                                                                17
     exhaustive lattice minimization and of the terminating algebraic solver.

1      P ROOF : Let k ∗ minimize f k . For every j, f k∗ ≤ f j ≤ u j , hence ℓk∗ ≤ f k∗ ≤ b and      1

2    k ∗ ∈ S. This argument retains all minimizers, including ties. Exact comparison with            2

3    the same tie rule therefore returns the same index. The algebraic solver enumerates             3

4    lattice neighbors of every activation boundary and isolated stationary point on each            4

5    polynomial piece; its established exhaustive-search equivalence then gives the same             5

6    witness.                                                                                        6

9                            f k ≤ ℓk + η ≤ b + η ≤ uk∗ + η ≤ f k∗ + 2η.                             9

11   This proves (142) and the unique-gap statement. Under the additional growth                     11
                                     p
12   condition a survivor is within 2 η/µ of some element of M. There are at most                    12
            p
13   1 + 2⌊2 η/µ/δ⌋ lattice points within that distance of one lattice minimizer. Taking             13

14   a union, and then the total lattice cardinality, proves the last bound.              Q.E.D.     14

15                                                                                                   15

16     The generic screening implication uses only interval order. The implementation                16

17   contribution here is the exact reduction of integrated trained ridges, outward eval- 17
18   uation of their reduced objective, and exact recovery of the original NBO witness. 18
19   On a failed arithmetic check or a lattice exceeding the declared screening limit, the           19

20   implementation calls the original terminating algebraic solver. A fallback is charged           20

21   as work and cannot be recorded as an approximate successful screen. A wide but                  21

22   valid enclosure may retain the entire lattice; the theorem promises correctness in              22

23   that case, not a speed gain.                                                                    23

24                                                                                                   24

25     P ROPOSITION 56—Arithmetic work and working storage: Let N = K + 1, let                       25

26   mc = |C|, and let s = |S|. Forming the state-specific neural coefficients and performing        26

27   exact region reduction requires O(dm) arithmetic operations for an m-ridge continuation in      27

28   dimension d. The screen uses O( N (1 + mc )) fixed-precision interval operations, followed by   28

29   O(sm) rational operations to compare survivors using the original objective. The vectorized     29

30   implementation uses O( N (1 + mc )) interval working storage, in addition to the fitted         30

31   coefficients, and returns O(s) retained indices. These are arithmetic counts; the cost of       31

32   rational arithmetic also depends on operand bit lengths.                                        32
     168

1      P ROOF : Each ridge requires state and innovation projections and a constant                 1

2    number of exact range tests. Each remaining ridge contributes one scalar interval              2

3    expression at each lattice point. Pairwise outward summation of the ridge contri- 3
4    butions is linear in their number. The exact return evaluates the original m-ridge             4

5    objective at each survivor. The implementation retains ridge-by-lattice batches,               5

6    giving the stated storage rather than a streaming bound. Integer index comparison              6

7    resolves equal rational values. None of these counts eliminates the dependence on              7

8    d, lattice resolution or fitted width.                                              Q.E.D.     8

9                                                                                                   9

10     Terminal optimization is unchanged in the experiment. The screened intervention              10

11   concerns only nonterminal trained-ReLU continuations. In particular, time saved                11

12   by an analytic terminal formula is not credited to learned-feature reduction.                  12
