14                                                                                                  14
                         Y.3. Preservation of a prospective economic return
15                                                                                                  15

16     A solver is used inside a procedure, not in isolation. Consider a deterministic              16

17   prospective controller conditional on its construction data and random streams. At             17

18   each attempted resolution it constructs an acquired policy, verifies whole-cell ad- 18
19   vantages and continuous-action lower covers, and then runs the declared sequence               19

20   of cost-inference looks. It returns at the first certified economic target, advances           20

21   resolution when required by its rule, or reports budget exhaustion. Suppose its                21

22   branching decisions depend on these mathematical outputs, not on elapsed time or               22

23   solver-specific diagnostic counters.                                                           23

24                                                                                                  24

25     T HEOREM 57—Prospective transcript invariance: Run the same controller with                  25

26   the algebraic solver and the lossless screened solver. Suppose corresponding construction      26

27   steps produce identical stored continuations, acquisition partitions and other candidate       27

28   actions, and use identical inference streams. Then both controllers return the same policy,    28

29   have identical whole-cell verification endpoints and cost-inference endpoints, and stop at     29

30   the same resolution and inference look with the same status. Their exact fitted-optimization   30

31   loss is the same, namely zero on the declared action lattice. Their all-state Bellman-error    31

32   certificate is unchanged.                                                                      32
                                     NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                    169

1      The returned policies have exactly equal expected economic cost under every admissible           1

2    initial distribution. This equality does not require statistical inference. It does not assert     2

3    equality of computation cost.                                                                      3

4                                                                                                       4
       P ROOF : Condition on the common construction inputs and streams. Theorem 55,
5                                                                                                       5
     or the exact fallback, gives identical proposed action indices at every leaf and date.
6                                                                                                       6
     The remaining candidate menu and its ordering are the same. The same verifier
7                                                                                                       7
     therefore evaluates the same intervals and resolves the same incumbent-preserving
8                                                                                                       8
     comparisons, producing an identical acquired policy and continuous-action error
9                                                                                                       9
     account. Evaluating that policy on the same interval paths gives identical path
10                                                                                                      10
     endpoints, hence identical inference bounds and the same next controller deci-
11                                                                                                      11
     sion. Induction over inference looks and attempted resolutions proves the entire
12                                                                                                      12
     transcript assertion. No additional attempt occurs after a successful return. The
13                                                                                                      13
     inherited fitted-witness transfer and directed policy-improvement theorems apply
14                                                                                                      14
     to the identical witnesses and certificates. Finally, identical policies have the same
15                                                                                                      15
     controlled law and expected cost for every initial law.                                 Q.E.D.
16                                                                                                      16

17                                                                                                      17
       The theorem is conditional on actual parameter identities, not merely on a shared
18                                                                                                      18
     training seed. The empirical audit checks those identities. It does not apply to a
19                                                                                                      19
     wall-clock deadline rule: a faster solver can reach a different stage before a real-time
20                                                                                                      20
     deadline. Nor does a target that is not attained under the declared finite budgets
21                                                                                                      21
     become impossible in the underlying economic model.
22                                                                                                      22

23     C OROLLARY 58—Computation prices at an unchanged policy: Suppose both                            23

24   implementations return π and face specified computation charges C A , CS . At a nonnegative        24

25   price λ, their total expected policy-plus-computation cost difference is                           25

26                                                                                                      26
                          { J (π ) + λCS } − { J (π ) + λC A } = λ(CS − C A ).
27                                                                                                      27

28                                                                                                      28
     If an installed controller has a verified replacement-gain interval [l, u], a fixed installation
29                                                                                                      29
     charge τ gives net gain in [l − τ − λCi , u − τ − λCi ] for implementation i.
30                                                                                                      30

31     P ROOF : Cancel the identical policy costs in the first display and subtract the                 31

32   specified deterministic charges from the gain interval in the second.                   Q.E.D.     32
     170

1      This comparative statement holds for specified resource charges. Three measured            1

2    process repetitions do not estimate a distribution-free expected runtime, and the            2

3    study does not calibrate λ or τ. They do, however, permit a directly interpretable           3

4    work comparison at exactly the same economic and statistical accuracy, without               4

5    attributing different welfare to identical neural policies.                                  5

7      APPENDIX Z: E QUAL - POLICY WORK AT PROSPECTIVE ECONOMIC TARGETS                           7

8                                                                                                 8
       The preceding experiment asks whether an exact trained-neural witness changes
9                                                                                                 9
     a returned policy. A different, necessary question is how economically that same
10                                                                                                10
     witness can be computed. The lossless screen permits a controlled comparison in
11                                                                                                11
     which economic accuracy, fitted parameters, whole-cell verification and statistical
12                                                                                                12
     stopping remain identical. We integrate the separately frozen R58 execution into this
13                                                                                                13
     revision and independently reconstruct its solver, certificate and path calculations.
14                                                                                                14
     The integration creates no new training run or independent statistical observation.
15                                                                                                15

16                                                                                                16
                 Z.1. A matched intervention within Neural Bellman Operators
17                                                                                                17

18     The experiment retains the original nonlinear cyclic investment laws. Its tasks are        18

19   (d, T ) = (2, 2), (4, 4), (8, 6) and its targets are ten- and twenty-percent expected-cost   19

20   reductions relative to installed zero investment. Each task–target has two solver            20

21   modes and three sequential complete-process repetitions, for 36 services. Both               21

22   modes independently train the same 32-feature own-future ReLU continuation. 22
23   Exact stored-parameter hashes establish matching; the common seed alone is not               23

24   used as proof of matching.                                                                   24

25     The sole intervention is the nonterminal fitted-action solver. Algebraic search            25

26   partitions the hinge objective and isolates its cubic stationary roots. Screened search      26

27   performs exact activation-region reduction, outward lattice elimination and rational         27

28   comparison of survivors. Both use the same robust action spacing 1/4096, the same            28

29   smallest-index tie rule and the same exact terminal solver. They retain the nine             29

30   common actions and the critic-menu proposal; the exact action is an additional               30

31   candidate. The same signed installed-reference verifier supplies all whole-cell upper        31

32   endpoints and continuous-action lower covers.                                                32
                                  NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                            171

1      The acquired observation geometry is the existing locally split binary partition,     1

2    not a tensor grid. The two resource attempts use (256, 32, 4) and (1024, 64, 8) for     2

3    training rows, acquired leaves and innovation bins. All attempts are checked against    3

4    installed zero investment. Intermediate candidates are not deployed, and no deriva- 4
5    tive argument is applied to a changed discontinuous acquired actor. This experiment     5

6    is a prospective refinement service, not a claim that two refinement attempts are       6

7    two policy-iteration sweeps.                                                            7

8                                                                                            8

9                         Z.2. First attainment, including nonattainment                     9

10                                                                                           10
       At each attempted resolution the complete policy is frozen before inference. The
11                                                                                           11
     target estimand is
12                                                                                           12

13                                                                                           13
                            Dq = J (π ) − qJ (0),     q ∈ {9/10, 4/5}.
14                                                                                           14

15                                                                                           15
     Cumulative sample sizes are 4096, 16384 and 65536. A nonpositive upper endpoint
16
     of Dq returns immediately. A strictly positive lower endpoint advances the construc- 16
17                                                                                           17
     tion attempt. An unresolved interval advances the inference look; an exhausted
18                                                                                           18
     look budget advances the attempt. Failure to certify after the last attempt returns
19                                                                                           19
     a safe policy with status budget exhausted. The protocol specifies these rules before
20                                                                                           20
     its production execution, rather than selecting a favorable pass from a completed
21                                                                                           21
     catalogue.
22                                                                                           22
       The R58 stopping stream is distinct from the R57 stopping and comparison
23
     streams. Within a task, target and attempt, the two solver modes and timing rep- 23
24                                                                                           24
     etitions deliberately regenerate the same stream. Repeated computation of that
25                                                                                           25
     stream does not multiply sample size. The confidence family has error at most
26                                                                                           26
     1/100 under the maintained independent-bin model. Its conservative maximum
27                                                                                           27
     possible number of interval evaluations is
28                                                                                           28

29                                  36 · 2 · 3 · 3 = 648 < 2048,                             29

30                                                                                           30

31   including cost, target contrast and gain at every possible look. The frozen empirical- 31
32   Bernstein implementation retains its verified logarithm allowance. The union bound      32
     172

1    requires no independence between policies or looks. It does not authorize un- 1
2    planned sample sizes or an arbitrary future stopping rule.                                                   2

30   The two modes have identical intervals, not independent estimates. Endpoints are rounded outward.            30

31       The signed target contrast, rather than a ratio of estimated means, determines attainment. The           31

32             twenty-percent target in eight dimensions remains unresolved at the final budget.                  32
                                      NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                        173

1      Thirty services attain their target. Six exhaust their budgets: the two modes,                         1

2    each repeated three times, at (d, T ) = (8, 6) and the twenty-percent target. Their                      2

3    final target-contrast interval contains zero. The returned policy nevertheless has                       3

4    a positive gain interval relative to installation. Thus failure to certify the stronger                  4

5    target is not failure of policy safety, evidence of zero gain, or a proof that the                       5

6    economic target is impossible.                                                                           6

7      Every matched pair has identical fitted parameters, acquired actions, certificates,                    7

8    original-law path endpoints and stopping decisions. Consequently its policy-cost                         8

9    difference is exactly zero under every initial law, by Theorem 57. No separate paired                    9

10   statistical experiment is needed to establish this equality. In contrast, the distinct                   10

11   neural and conventional policies in Section X retain their own paired cost intervals                     11

12   and must not be assigned this identity conclusion.                                                       12

     A denotes algebraic search and S screened search. Counts sum all attempted resolutions in one service;
26                                                                                                            26
     all three repetitions reproduce them. The screened mode retains one point per nonterminal neural query
27                                                                                                            27
     and performs zero root isolations and zero fallbacks in this catalogue. Terminal work is common and
28                                    excluded from these neural counts.                                      28

29                                                                                                            29

30     The machine-independent records identify the mechanism. Across the frozen                              30

31   workloads, the screen removes noncrossing integrated ridges exactly and retains                          31

32   one lattice point per nonterminal neural query. That point is compared rationally                        32
     174

1    against the original objective. No production call uses the algebraic fallback. The      1

2    number of original-objective rational evaluations therefore falls to the number of       2

3    neural queries, whereas the algebraic mode compares several candidate points             3

4    per query and performs cubic-root isolation. The table charges all lattice points        4

5    evaluated by the interval screen; it does not count only survivors as total work.        5

6      The screen is not an approximate optimizer with a favorable tolerance. The             6

7    audit recomputes both solvers from every distinct saved model, compares every            7

8    recorded witness and endpoint hash, regenerates each whole-cell certificate, and         8

9    reconstructs the original interval paths. Distinct computations are memoized only        9

10   after their input identities are checked. Every service file and stopping transcript     10

11   is still compared. Separate exact fixtures verify all lattice endpoints for disjoint     11

12   small problems, signed weights, activation boundaries, ties, non-dyadic lattices and     12

13   fallback cases.                                                                          13

14     Median action-search times decrease in all six task–target groups, as do the           14

15   complete process medians. The full-service algebraic-to-screened ratios range from       15

16   approximately 1.028 to 1.101. These gains are smaller than the action-search gains       16

17   because training, common terminal search, verification and inference are unchanged. 17
18   The eight-dimensional unsuccessful target is included in the work comparison:            18

19   faster exhaustion is not described as faster successful attainment.                      19

20     The effect is a measured implementation gain within the trained NBO procedure          20

21   at exactly matching policies and statistical accuracy. It is not a ranking of neural     21

22   methods above common-only, quadratic or tree-based alternatives. The stronger            22

23   conventional comparisons, the common-menu attribution results and all unresolved         23

24   differences in the preceding studies remain part of the evidence.                        24

25                                                                                            25

26                                                                                            26
                         Z.4. Complete work and the economic decision
27                                                                                            27

28     Every displayed service clock surrounds a separate process from invocation             28

29   through exit and log flush. It includes imports, source checks, independently re- 29
30   peated fitting, action search, signed verification, own path evaluation and inference,   30

31   all attempted resolutions and durable records. Available frequency-governor in- 31
32   formation, processor affinity, numerical-library versions, output bytes and peak         32
                                 NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                               175

1    resident memory are retained. The parent driver’s own checkpoint-writing over- 1
2    head is outside that measured interval and is identified as controller overhead, not      2

3    assigned a zero cost.                                                                     3

4      Within a task, processes run sequentially on one available core with one                4

5    numerical-library thread, and solver order rotates by repetition and target. All          5

6    three measurements are printed in the supplement. Frequencies are observed where          6

7    available but not controlled. Different task runners do not supply a hardware- 7
8    controlled dimension experiment. Three fixed-work repetitions establish neither a         8

9    distribution-free runtime advantage nor a population of independently fitted poli- 9
10   cies. The three distinct fitted seeds in the preceding experiment answer a different      10

11   question and remain separately identified.                                                11

12     For a specified computation price, Corollary 58 converts an equal-policy resource       12

13   difference directly into a total-cost difference. Relative to the installed controller,   13

14   the same actual gain interval supports a larger region of installation fees and           14

15   computation prices when the specified implementation charge is lower. This is a           15

16   substantive difference in the cost of obtaining an unchanged economic answer, not         16

17   a claim that two representations of the same policy produce different welfare. The        17

18   model’s units remain normalized; no empirical installation fee, computation price         18

19   or welfare calibration is inferred from the timings.                                      19

20     The original accuracy objective remains visible throughout. Every returned policy       20

21   retains its one-sweep all-state loss account, including the installed-reference contin- 21
22   uation bound as well as its full-action directed gap. Lossless search changes neither     22

23   that account nor the amount of acquisition information. A successful initial-law          23

24   cost-reduction target is not substituted for convergence to the original Bellman          24

25   optimum. The constructive resource theory, fitted-witness transfer and directed           25

26   finite-sweep theorem continue to state the conditions under which that stronger           26

27   objective is controlled.                                                                  27

       The preceding lossless screen makes the number of exact comparisons depend on
5                                                                                                              5
     the separation of the best lattice actions. That dependence is useful when separation
6                                                                                                              6
     is substantial, but it is not a reason to assume a favorable regime. We now exploit the
7                                                                                                              7
     integrated objective directly. The resulting exact solver does not require a growth
8                                                                                                              8
     constant, a unique minimizer, or isolation of an algebraic root. We also separate this
9                                                                                                              9
     local optimization question from verification against the original Bellman optimum.

                         R61.1.1. Finite differences instead of root isolation

     Φr (z) = E(z + U )+ for U uniform on [−r, r ], with Φ0 (z) = z+ . The original invest- 20
21                                                                                                             21
     ment model has c4 = 4. Mixture probabilities and shifts can be absorbed into the

1    The last expression is nondecreasing for j ≥ 0, including when d2 < 0. This is a                     1

2    discrete curvature property, not global convexity of the fitted objective.                           2

3                                                                                                         3
       L EMMA 59—Exact minimization of one piece: Suppose 0 ≤ l ≤ u and c4 ≥ 0. The
4                                                                                                         4
     smallest minimizing index of pl , . . . , pu can be recovered using O(1 + log(u − l + 1))
5                                                                                                         5
     exact rational sign queries and at most four exact objective comparisons. If every second
6                                                                                                         6
     difference is negative, endpoints suffice. Otherwise let j0 be the first index with nonnegative
7                                                                                                         7
     second difference. In addition to the endpoints, it suffices to retain j0 and the first k ≥ j0
8                                                                                                         8
     with dk ≥ 0, when that index exists. If it does not exist, the right endpoint is the minimum
9                                                                                                         9
     of the remaining tail.
10                                                                                                        10

11     P ROOF : A sequence with decreasing first differences is discretely concave and                    11

12   has a minimum at an endpoint. If j0 exists, the prefix through p j0 +1 is concave,                   12

13   whereas the tail beginning at p j0 is convex. A minimum of the full sequence is                      13

14   therefore either the left endpoint or a minimum of that convex tail. The latter is at                14

15   the first nonnegative first difference, or at the right endpoint if all its first differences        15

16   are negative. The first such index is the smallest tail minimizer, including a run of                16

17   exact ties. Monotonicity of (145) locates j0 by integer bisection, and monotonicity of               17

18   d j on the tail locates k in the same way. Intervals with fewer than three indices are               18

19   handled by their endpoints. Exact comparisons of the retained values complete the                    19

20   argument.                                                                                 Q.E.D.     20

21                                                                                                        21

22
       T HEOREM 60—Root-free exact action witness: For (143) on { j/Q : 0 ≤ j ≤ j̄},                      22

23
     with ā = j̄/Q, form the rational activation partition, intersect each piece with the integer        23

24
     lattice, and apply Lemma 59. Evaluate the original objective at the union of the retained            24

25
     indices, choosing the smallest index on an exact tie. The result is precisely the original lattice   25

26
     minimizer, without a separation assumption or an algebraic root calculation.                         26

27
       A direct implementation uses                                                                       27

28                                                                                                        28
                      O (m + h)2 + (m + h) log(m + h + 1) + K log( j̄ + 2)
                                                                                    
                                                                                                 (146)
29                                                                                                        29

30   rational operations and comparisons, and O(m + h + K ) stored rational numbers and                   30

31   indices. These are operation counts, not unit-cost bit bounds. If b bounds the bit lengths of        31

32   all operands and Crat (b) bounds one rational operation or comparison, multiplying (146)             32
     178

1    by Crat (b) is a valid bit-cost account. For input coefficient bit length at most B, fixed degree   1

2    and denominator Q, a conservative bound is                                                          2

3                                                                                                       3
             b = O (m + h + 1)( B + log( Q + 1) + log( j̄ + 2) + log(m + h + 2)) .
4                                                                                                        4

5                                                                                                        5
       P ROOF : The activation formulas are exact on every piece. Its lattice intersection is
6                                                                                                        6
     the consecutive interval from the ceiling of its left endpoint times Q to the floor of its
7                                                                                                        7
     right endpoint times Q; empty intersections are skipped. These intersections cover
8
     every lattice action. The lemma retains a minimizing index for every nonempty in- 8
9                                                                                                        9
     tersection and its smallest minimizer. Exact comparison under the original objective
10                                                                                                       10
     is unaffected by the piecewise constants or the earlier common-constant reduction,
11                                                                                                       11
     and gives the original tie rule globally.
12                                                                                                       12
       Sorting breakpoints costs O((m + h) log(m + h + 1)) comparisons. Constructing
13
     the polynomial coefficients independently in each piece costs O(K (m + h)) opera- 13
14                                                                                                       14
     tions. The integer bisections cost O(K log( j̄ + 2)). There are O(K ) final candidate
15
     indices, and direct evaluation of the original objective costs O(K (m + h)). The im- 15
16                                                                                                       16
     plementation stores the features, breakpoints and candidate indices, rather than an
17
     array indexed by every lattice point. Finally, products of a fixed number of input ra- 17
18                                                                                                       18
     tionals have bit length proportional to the input lengths. Summing at most O(m + h)
19                                                                                                       19
     such terms admits a common denominator formed by their product; this gives the
20                                                                                                       20
     stated conservative operand bound, including powers of Q, the action-index bound,
21                                                                                                       21
     and the rational breakpoint calculations.                                                Q.E.D.
22                                                                                                       22

23     The theorem is useful in precisely the case where interval separation is inconclu- 23
24   sive. A flat piece, a tie between consecutive actions, or a large signed cancellation               24

25   does not prevent exact bisection. It does not assert that bisection is always fastest:              25

26   when the lattice is short relative to the activation partition, native exhaustive eval- 26
27   uation can be cheaper. The ablation below therefore includes both, as well as the                   27

28   original symbolic solver after the same exact reduction. Discrete curvature and veri- 28
29   fied interval exclusion are established numerical principles (Murota, 2003, Moore                   29

30   et al., 2009). The result here is their explicit, root-free realization for the integrated          30

31   NBO objective, with an exact original-witness contract and a bit-aware implementa- 31
32   tion account.                                                                                       32

       T HEOREM 62—Finite constrained recovery with a work budget: Suppose a finite
4                                                                                                      4
     rational action lattice is intersected with a bounded rational polytope, and every visited
5                                                                                                      5
     integer rectangle has a valid lower objective bound. Maintain an exactly evaluated feasible
6                                                                                                      6
     incumbent and a fixed lexicographic tie rule. Discard a rectangle only if it is infeasible, its
7                                                                                                      7
     lower bound is strictly greater than the incumbent value, or equality holds and its smallest
8                                                                                                      8
     possible index is not earlier than the incumbent. Split any other nonsingleton rectangle
9                                                                                                      9
     into two disjoint integer rectangles, and evaluate singleton actions exactly. Then exhaustive
10                                                                                                     10
     subdivision returns the original smallest minimizing feasible action. Switching after any
11                                                                                                     11
     declared node budget to an exact exhaustive solver preserves this conclusion and charges the
12                                                                                                     12
     preceding work in addition to the fallback.
13                                                                                                     13
       For side cardinalities N1 , . . . , Np , a depth-first implementation has depth at most
14                                                                                                     14

15
     ∑i ⌈log2 Ni ⌉ under midpoint splitting. Its rectangle stack stores O( p ∑i log( Ni + 1)) inte- 15
     gers. Without pruning it can still visit O(∏i Ni ) nodes. Objective arithmetic, rational bit
16                                                                                                     16
     lengths and exact-polytope feasibility work must be added to these combinatorial counts.
17                                                                                                     17

18
       P ROOF : Each split partitions the remaining integer indices without omission. 18
19
     A discarded infeasible rectangle contains no admissible competitor. Strict lower- 19
20                                                                                                     20
     bound exclusion removes no minimizer that could improve the incumbent. In the
21                                                                                                     21
     equality case no point can improve its value, and the index condition excludes
22
     an earlier tied minimizer. Every unpruned feasible singleton is compared exactly. 22
23                                                                                                     23
     Finite subdivision therefore retains the globally best value and the specified first
24                                                                                                     24
     minimizer. An exact terminating fallback solves the same remaining mathematical
25                                                                                                     25
     problem, whether or not it reexamines previously visited points. Its repeated work
26                                                                                                     26
     affects cost, not validity. Midpoint splitting halves the active side range, yielding
27                                                                                                     27
     the depth and stack bounds; a complete binary partition has fewer than twice as
28                                                                                                     28
     many nodes as its full rectangular lattice has points.                                 Q.E.D.
29                                                                                                     29

30     Replacing a solver by either theorem preserves the earlier prospective transcript               30

31   whenever that controller depends on the mathematical witness rather than elapsed                  31

32   time or solver diagnostics. This transfers exactness to the acquired policy and its               32
                                       NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                      181

1    certificates; it does not imply that a fitted-optimal action is optimal for the true                   1

2    continuation. That latter error remains in the existing transfer theorem and is                        2

3    examined by an independent all-state calculation next.                                                 3

4                                                                                                           4

5                R61.1.3. A direct certificate against the original Bellman optimum                         5

6      Let Ct be a finite cover of the original state domain with an unambiguous owner- 6
7    ship rule for shared boundaries. Starting from terminal bounds on g, maintain a                        7

8    piecewise lower function Lt+1 for the Bellman value and a piecewise upper function                     8

9    Ut+1 for the value of an explicitly constructed continuation policy. On a cell C, let                  9

10   Bt (C ) cover every action feasible at every state in C, and let At (C ) contain only                  10

11   actions feasible throughout C. Suppose verified endpoints satisfy                                      11

12                                                                                                          12

13             ℓt (C, B) ≤ ct ( x, a) + β t Pta Lt+1 ( x )            ( x ∈ C, a ∈ B ∩ At ( x )),           13

14              ut (C, b) ≥ ct ( x, b) + β t Ptb Ut+1 ( x )           ( x ∈ C, b ∈ At (C )).                14

15                                                                                                          15

16
     A lower cover may include additional infeasible actions, but every evaluated ex- 16
17
     tension must retain a valid lower enclosure on its feasible subset. All reached state                  17

18
     cells and the full original innovation law are included in the expectations. Define                    18

19                                                                                                          19
                       Lt (C ) = min ℓt (C, B),               Ut (C ) = min ut (C, b),
20                                B∈Bt (C )                                b∈At (C )                        20

21   and implement the minimizing upper candidate on C.                                                     21

22                                                                                                          22

23
       T HEOREM 63—Whole-domain Bellman bracket: The constructed complete policy π                          23

24
     is feasible and satisfies                                                                              24

25                                                                                                          25
                              Lt (C ) ≤ Jt∗ ( x ) ≤ Jtπ ( x ) ≤ Ut (C ),        x ∈ C.              (148)
26                                                                                                          26

27   Hence maxC {Ut (C ) − Lt (C )} is an all-state upper bound on loss against the original                27

28   continuous-action Bellman optimum. A declared tolerance is attained when its outward                   28

29   bound passes the corresponding comparison; no initial distribution or reference-zero gain is           29

30   needed.                                                                                                30

31     For a refining family, suppose the exact lower and upper endpoint maps at each fixed                 31

32   approximation level are order preserving and β t -nonexpansive in the supremum norm.                   32
     182

1    Suppose their one-step errors at the true next Bellman value, together with uniformly                   1

2    bounded numerical endpoint errors on the relevant input range, are at most ηt , inclusive               2

3    of robust-action approximation and state, action and innovation covering. If the terminal               3

4    lower and upper errors are each at most ηT , then                                                       4

5                                                                                                            5
                                                               T  j −1   
6                               sup{Ut (C ) − Lt (C )} ≤ 2 ∑ ∏ β i η j .                                     6
                                 C                            j=t i =t
7                                                                                                            7

8    In particular, uniformly vanishing such allowances give arbitrarily small Bellman brackets              8

9    in finite horizon. The runtime certificate is (148); the consistency statement does not require         9

10   the algorithm to observe J ∗ .                                                                          10

11                                                                                                           11
       P ROOF : Assume the next-date inequalities. Monotonicity of each action kernel
12                                                                                                           12
     gives Pta Lt+1 ≤ Pta Jt∗+1 . The complete action cover and the lower enclosure therefore
13                                                                                                           13
     imply Lt (C ) ≤ infa∈ At ( x) {ct ( x, a) + β t Pta Jt∗+1 ( x )} = Jt∗ ( x ). The chosen upper action
14                                                                                                           14
     is feasible at every state in C. Its continuation policy has value at most Ut+1 , so its
15                                                                                                           15
     current value is at most the chosen upper endpoint Ut (C ). Feasibility gives Jt∗ ≤ Jtπ .
16                                                                                                           16
     Backward induction proves the bracket.
17                                                                                                           17
       For consistency, let et− bound Jt∗ − Lt and let et+ bound Ut − Jt∗ . Nonexpansivity
18                                                                                                           18
     of the fixed-cover endpoint maps bounds the propagated next-date discrepancy by
19                                                                                                           19
     β t times its supremum norm. Adding the uniformly budgeted local and numerical
20                                                                                                           20
     endpoint errors consequently gives et− ≤ ηt + β t et−+1 and et+ ≤ ηt + β t et++1 , with
21                                                                                                           21
     terminal errors at most ηT . Adding the two unrolled inequalities proves the bound.
22                                                                                                           22
     Primitive uniform continuity and consistent enclosure rules supply vanishing al-
23                                                                                                           23
     lowances on compact domains when the robust feasible menus and full-action
24                                                                                                           24
     covers converge. These are hypotheses for consistency, not an uncharged value-
25                                                                                                           25
     function oracle.                                                                             Q.E.D.
26                                                                                                           26

27     The numerical implementation uses exact rectangle extrema of stored outward                           27

28   bounds. At the last date it integrates the original terminal cost analytically. At earlier              28

29   dates each innovation bin retains its exact original probability, and no midpoint is                    29

30   substituted for the bin’s conditional expectation. This calculation is a conventional                   30

31   constructive benchmark in the same NBO economy. It puts an attained all-state                           31

32   accuracy and the work paid for it beside the learned prospective services, rather than                  32
                                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                        183

1    presenting improvement relative to zero investment as a solution of the Bellman                               1

2    problem.                                                                                                      2

3                                                                                                                  3

4                  R61.1.4. The economic value of an exact implementation change                                   4

5                                                                                                                  5
       For two implementations returning the identical policy, let w0 , w1 be their mea-
6                                                                                                                  6
     sured per-service work, k0 , k1 their one-time compilation or installation work, and
7                                                                                                                  7
     λ ≥ 0 the price of the recorded work unit. Over H uses, with an additional imple-
8                                                                                                                  8
     mentation charge I, the new-minus-old total cost is
9                                                                                                                  9

10                                        λ{ H (w1 − w0 ) + (k1 − k0 )} + I.                                       10

11                                                                                                                 11

12   When w1 < w0 , the recorded computation saving pays for a positive setup dif- 12
13   ference only beyond the corresponding break-even use count. This condition is                                 13

14   reported separately from policy-cost comparisons and from the cold single-use                                 14

15   boundary. No electricity tariff, processor frequency, installation price or external                          15

16   welfare calibration is inferred from a hosted-runner clock.                                                   16

17                                                                                                                 17

18                R61.1.5. Common-reference cancellation at the original optimum                                   18

19                                                                                                                 19
       The width of an absolute value box can be dominated by state variation common
20                                                                                                                 20
     to all actions. Subtracting the same reference function from both Bellman endpoints
21                                                                                                                 21
     removes that variation without changing the decision problem. This is a use of the
22                                                                                                                 22
     original policy-relevant error principle, not a replacement of the Bellman objective
23                                                                                                                 23
     by a zero-policy comparison.

19                                                                                                      19
     tion to obtain the two recursions. The induction in Theorem 63 applies verbatim to
20                                                                                                      20
     these transformed equations: kernel monotonicity and the complete feasible-action
21                                                                                                      21
     cover give the lower excess bound, and implementability and the upper contin-
22                                                                                                      22
     uation bound give the upper excess bound. Both endpoints at a given true state
23                                                                                                      23

24                                                                                                      24

25                                                                                                      25

26                                                                                                      26

27                                                                                                      27

28     In the two-date investment economy, choose the original zero-investment policy                   28

29   as reference. Its final-date advantage is the original analytic final-cost difference. At          29

30   the initial date, use a common innovation to propagate the state difference caused                 30

31   by the proposed action and integrate the difference of subsequent zero-policy costs. 31
32   Centered polynomial identities evaluate that difference; the final innovation’s com- 32
                                 NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                              185

1    mon variance cancels, while its correct marginal law is retained. The construction        1

2    therefore does not assume a policy-value oracle, a differentiable acquired policy, or     2

3    a learned invariant.                                                                      3

4      The centered experiment is explicitly subsequent to the uncentered one. It retains      4

5    the unsuccessful original tolerance tests and applies a separately frozen five-rung       5

6    verification design. The same centered lower table can also certify the previously        6

7    returned learned and conventional policies. Such revalidation has its own cost            7

8    and does not retroactively alter the original prospective stopping criterion or its       8

9    measured clock. This connects the original Bellman-accuracy question to the actual        9

10   returned objects, rather than only to an alternative constructed policy.                  10

     APPENDIX R61.2: S TABLE ENCLOSURES AND CERTIFICATION OF A RETURNED                        13
                                            POLICY
14                                                                                             14

15                                                                                             15
       A valid one-step interval and a stable recursion are different requirements. The
16                                                                                             16
     first establishes a runtime bracket; the second controls how an approximation
17                                                                                             17
     allowance propagates when the next-date input changes. We make the distinction
18                                                                                             18
     explicit and then apply the common-reference construction to a policy that has
19                                                                                             19
     already been returned by a prospective service.
20                                                                                             20

21                                                                                             21

22                               R61.2.1. Fixed-cover stability                                22

23                                                                                             23
       At a fixed approximation level, hold the state cells, action covers, candidate ac-
24                                                                                             24
     tions, innovation bins and their probabilities fixed. An endpoint map may take
25                                                                                             25
     minima or maxima over the cells intersecting a transition box, average those end-
26                                                                                             26
     points with nonnegative weights of total mass one, add a stage-cost endpoint and
27                                                                                             27
     finally minimize over a fixed finite family of action intervals or feasible candidates.
28                                                                                             28
     A reference-shifted endpoint map may additionally add a fixed signed reference
29                                                                                             29
     advantage. These operations describe the exact endpoint maps beneath the outward
30                                                                                             30
     arithmetic used here.
31                                                                                             31

32                                                                                             32
     186

1      L EMMA 65—Stability of the executable endpoint maps: Every such exact endpoint                  1

2    map Et is order preserving and satisfies                                                          2

3                                                                                                      3

4                                   ∥Et f − Et g∥∞ ≤ β t ∥ f − g∥∞ .                          (149)    4

5                                                                                                      5
     Before intersection with an independent bound, it also satisfies Et ( f + c) = Et f + β t c for
6                                                                                                      6
     constants c. Intersecting a lower endpoint with an independently valid lower bound by
7                                                                                                      7
     taking a maximum, or an upper endpoint with an independently valid upper bound by
8                                                                                                      8
     taking a minimum, preserves order and the nonexpansive estimate when the independent
9                                                                                                      9
     bound is fixed. A numerical endpoint perturbation with verified uniform magnitude δt
10                                                                                                     10
     enters the propagated error as an additional local allowance, rather than being presumed
11                                                                                                     11
     nonexpansive because it is outward.
12                                                                                                     12

13                                                                                                     13
       P ROOF : For any nonempty finite index set I, both mini∈ I f i and maxi∈ I f i are or-
14                                                                                                     14
     der preserving and one-Lipschitz in the supremum norm. A nonnegative weighted
15                                                                                                     15
     average of total mass one has the same properties. A fixed additive term does not
16                                                                                                     16
     alter a difference, and multiplication by β t ≥ 0 supplies the factor in (149). The
17                                                                                                     17
     final minimum over actions is again one-Lipschitz. Each of these operations com-
18                                                                                                     18
     mutes with addition of a constant in the stated way. Finally, x 7→ min{ x, c} and
19                                                                                                     19
     x 7→ max{ x, c} are monotone and one-Lipschitz. A separate numerical perturbation
20                                                                                                     20
     is bounded by the triangle inequality.                                                 Q.E.D.
21                                                                                                     21

22                                                                                                     22
       Consequently, the consistency clause of Theorem 63 applies to the fixed-cover
23                                                                                                     23
     maps actually used in the computation. If the ideal lower and upper maps at the
24                                                                                                     24
     true next Bellman value have errors at most αt , and their numerical evaluation
25                                                                                                     25
     has uniformly bounded endpoint error δt on the relevant range of inputs, put
26                                                                                                     26
     ηt = αt + δt . Then the two one-sided errors satisfy
27                                                                                                     27

28                            et− ≤ ηt + β t et−+1 ,   et+ ≤ ηt + β t et++1 .                          28

29                                                                                                     29

30   Their sum gives the displayed two-sided finite-horizon bound. Different approxi- 30
31   mation levels may use different covers; the stability comparison is within each fixed             31

32   level. Arbitrarily small tolerances require all nonvanishing sources of error to be               32
                                     NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                     187

1    refined, including the action quantum when it is a binding discretization. A finite                  1

2    ladder at quantum 1/4096 is not, by itself, an executed zero-error limit.                            2

3      Inclusion alone would not justify this step. In a one-state example with contin- 3
4    uation v ≥ 0 and discount β, the endpoint βv + v2 is a valid upper bound on βv                       4

5    and is exact at v = 0. Its variation from 0 to 1 nevertheless exceeds β. Thus an ideal               5

6    error statement only at the true continuation cannot replace (149). The present proof                6

7    supplies the needed property for the actual min–max and probability-average maps;                    7

8    it does not assume stability of every conceivable enclosing procedure.                               8

24     The independent property used in (151) is obtained here by reconstructing the                   24

25   original reference-advantage gate at every acquired cell and date. Its nonpositive                25

26   upper endpoints imply A0t ( x, πt ( x )) ≤ 0 throughout the domain, and backward                  26

27   policy improvement gives Jtπ ≤ Jt0 . The revalidation also retains the entirely un- 27
28   clipped upper recursion and its final gaps. This permits the reader to distinguish a              28

29   justified independent bound from a numerically favorable truncation. A record of                  29

30   nonpositive values at sampled centers would not supply the required property.                     30

31     The verification grid is finer than the actor’s acquisition partition. Actor ranges             31

32   include all leaves intersecting each closed verification cell, including both sides of a          32
                                NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                             189

1    discontinuity. Rectangle extrema and original-law innovation bins then enclose the     1

2    continuation. These calculations have a real cost: the common lower-table construc- 2
3    tion, each fixed-actor upper recursion and the whole-process return overhead are       3

4    separate measured services. They do not retroactively reduce a prospective service’s   4

5    original stopping cost or certify an unexecuted high-dimensional case.                 5

6                                                                                           6
       Original-optimum accuracy of the implemented policy. A feasible witness is a local
7                                                                                           7
     output; a decision maker ultimately purchases a complete controller. Section R61.3
8                                                                                           8
     supplies a new bridge in the original investment economy. Although its state transi-
9                                                                                           9
     tion is bilinear, primitive state curvature dominates the possible loss of convexity
10                                                                                          10
     in the continuation composition. The resulting Bellman functions have explicit
11                                                                                          11
     Lipschitz and semiconcavity bounds at the original discount. Positive state interpo-
12                                                                                          12
     lation and continuous-law quadrature can therefore be budgeted at second order.
13                                                                                          13
     A separate action-transfer argument certifies the actual barycentric acquired actor
14                                                                                          14
     rather than only a fitted objective or a nodewise value table.
15                                                                                          15
       The prospective comparison in Section R61.5 applies this contract to pure neural
16                                                                                          16
     proposals, conventional fitted proposals, and their common-augmented policies
17                                                                                          17
     across dimensions, horizons and a full two-control economy. Original-optimum
18                                                                                          18
     accuracy, comparative expected cost and complete release work remain separate
19                                                                                          19
     endpoints. This completes another part of the original NBO construction without
20                                                                                          20
     identifying exact action search with a universally superior dynamic-programming
21                                                                                          21
     method.
22                                                                                          22

23     Convex dynamic programming and the additional primitive argument. Convex dy- 23
24   namic programs and uniform policy approximation are established subjects. Yang         24

25   (2020) constructs convex optimization-based Bellman approximations and policy          25

26   suboptimality bounds for continuous spaces. Our added argument verifies the            26

27   required regularity for the stated bilinear, capacity-constrained investment prim- 27
28   itives, derives coordinate interpolation and continuous-action errors, and carries     28

29   these allowances through the recorded acquired actor. It does not claim priority       29

30   for convex dynamic programming or assume that a signed-weight fitted ReLU is           30

31   convex. The mathematical object whose curvature is proved is the original Bellman      31

32   value.                                                                                 32

       The exact witness theorem answers an implementation question conditional on
4
     a continuation. An economist also needs to know how close the complete imple- 4
5                                                                                                5
     mented policy is to the original optimum. We now establish that connection in the
6                                                                                                6
     investment economy itself. The argument exploits a property not used by the earlier
7                                                                                                7
     cell-range calculation: despite the bilinear transition, the Bellman objective remains
8                                                                                                8
     jointly convex. This permits a second-order interpolation account and transfers
9                                                                                                9
     verified nodal decisions to an implementable policy over the entire state domain. It
10                                                                                               10
     does not assume that a fitted neural continuation is convex.

1      P ROOF : The state cost has gradient infinity norm at most 8/d, Hessian norm at         1

2    most 22/d, and diagonal second derivatives at most 5/d + 16/d2 . For the terminal         2

3    cost the corresponding bounds are 10/d, 26/d, and 9/d + 16/d2 . They follow by            3

4    differentiating the quadratic terms and the continuously differentiable squared           4

5    shortage. The bound on the cycle Laplacian is four. The shortage Hessian, where it        5

6    exists, is 1611⊤ /d2 ; the same second-difference bound holds across its kink.            6

7      For feasible ( x, a) and (y, b), put w = x − y. The difference between the transition   7

8    at their convex combination and the corresponding combination of transitions              8

9    is θ (1 − θ )w j w j+1 /16 in coordinate j. Convexity and the Gt+1 Lipschitz bound        9

10   therefore give a convexity defect of at most                                              10

14   The state cost supplies curvature 4/d, while the two-control action cost has Hessian      14

15   eigenvalues at least 7/4. Thus the state curvature of Q∗t is at least 4/d − βGt+1 /8, 15
16   which is at least 107/(128d) when Gt+1 ≤ 27/d. Partial minimization over the affine       16

17   capacity graph preserves the state convexity inequality. The minimizing action is         17

18   unique, including at a binding capacity constraint.                                       18

19     To propagate Lipschitz continuity, write a = c( x )u, where u lies in the fixed unit    19

20   simplex. Transfer the same u from x to y. Each action-cost partial derivative has         20

21   absolute value at most 13/16, and ∑ j u j ≤ 1. The action-cost transfer contributes at    21

22   most 13∥ x − y∥1 /(128d). The transition Jacobian at a fixed action has column sums       22

23   at most 11/16; the capacity transfer adds at most 3/64. Comparing a minimizing            23

24   normalized action in both directions gives the stated recursion for Gt . Its right side   24

25   maps 27/d below itself.                                                                   25

26     For a fixed u, the transition as a function of x has Jacobian norm at most 11/16 + 26
27   1/16 = 3/4. Its second-order composition term is bounded by Gt+1 /8. The action- 27
28   cost Hessian has norm at most 21/4, and the capacity gradient has squared norm            28

29   1/(64d). These facts give the recursion for Mt . The infimum over u preserves this        29

30   common semiconcavity bound: after subtracting the same quadratic, each fixed-u            30

31   function is concave, and an infimum of concave functions is concave. The right            31

32   side maps 54/d below itself. Convexity together with semiconcavity yields the             32
                                 NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                              193

1    claimed differentiability and gradient regularity; this conclusion does not require a    1

2    differentiable optimizing-action map.                                                    2

3      Along one state coordinate, the fixed-u transition has zero second derivative. 3
4    The squared norm of its first derivative is at most 85/256 + 23/(256d): the two          4

5    original nonzero entries are bounded by 9/16 and 1/8, and the capacity-induced           5

6    entries are bounded by 1/(16d). Combining this with the diagonal stage-cost bound        6

7    gives Dt . Finally, positive interpolation along one coordinate has error between zero   7

8    and Dt h2 /8. Applying the coordinate interpolations successively and adding the         8

9    bounds proves (152).                                                           Q.E.D.    9

10                                                                                            10

11                                                                                            11
       The theorem is a structural statement about these economic primitives. It is not a
12                                                                                            12
     claim that every controlled diffusion, recursive preference specification, or game
13                                                                                            13
     in the complete development edition has a convex Bellman problem. Nor does it
14                                                                                            14
     require a signed-weight neural fit to preserve convexity. Convex approximation
15                                                                                            15
     of dynamic programs has an established literature; the additional work here is to
16                                                                                            16
     verify the needed curvature for the original nonlinear capacity-constrained model
17                                                                                            17
     and connect it to the actual NBO actor and complete error account.
18                                                                                            18

19                                                                                            19

20                                                                                            20
                            R61.3.2. Certified nodal Bellman bounds

24   The lower function need not itself be convex; convexity is used for the unknown          24

25   true value and for the comparison argument, not asserted of arbitrary endpoint           25

26   arrays.                                                                                  26

29     In the execution, observation is rounded down at forty bits. An acquired state                    29

30   y ≤ x has no greater capacity than the true state. Each interpolated action is then                 30

31   rounded down at twenty bits using the lower endpoint of its arithmetic enclosure. 31
32   If Lij bounds the derivative of action coordinate i with respect to state coordinate j,             32
     196

1    and η bounds native interpolation error, a valid implementation allowance is             1

8
