9
     policy revalidation.                                                                     9

10                                                                                            10
                             R61.3.4. Arithmetic, work, and scope
11                                                                                            11

12     The native interpolation kernel forms nonnegative tensor weights and a weighted        12

13   sum of at most 2d stored binary64 endpoints. It is compiled without reassociation or     13

14   floating-point contraction. For K = (3d + 4)2d + 32, an explicit conservative absolute   14

15   allowance is                                                                             15
                                        −53
16                                   K2                                                       16
                              η=             max |zv | + K2−1022 .
                                          −53 v
17                                 1 − K2                                                     17

18   The final term conservatively covers underflow. Input coordinates, affine indexing,      18

19   finite endpoints and the binary64 format are checked. Roundoff expansion and the         19

20   ordinary interval cost arithmetic remain separate from the analytic approximation        20

21   bounds. Exact rational fixtures compare the native sum with its mathematical tensor      21

22   interpolant.                                                                             22

23     With N = (n + 1)d vertices, K A = A + 1 scalar actions or K A = ( A + 1)( A + 23
24   2)/2 two-control actions, the reference calculation uses O( TNK A qs 2d d) elementary    24

25   work under the explicit tensor implementation. Stored reference values use O( TN )       25

26   words and a policy uses O( TNm) words. Streaming batches avoid a state-pair              26

27   table. Proposal fitting, exact rational witness work, actor transfer, serialization,     27

28   independent cost inference and compilation are additional costs, not absorbed into       28

29   this bound. The analytic errors are second order in state, action and innovation         29

30   resolution; the state-count and tensor-evaluation factors still depend exponentially     30

31   on dimension. The result supplies a matched original-optimum comparison without          31

32   asserting that these costs have disappeared.                                             32
                                  NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                 197

1      APPENDIX R61.4: E XACT COMPUTATION AND THE ECONOMIC ACCURACY                               1

2                                          CONTRACT                                               2

3                                                                                                 3
       The preceding experiments establish distinct parts of the NBO procedure. We
4                                                                                                 4
     now examine how the exact implementation behaves away from one-survivor
5                                                                                                 5
     screening, what it costs inside a complete economic service, and whether the policies
6                                                                                                 6
     actually returned can be certified against the original Bellman optimum. The original
7                                                                                                 7
     nonlinear investment family and its continuous laws are retained. The additional
8                                                                                                 8
     two-control calculation is identified explicitly as a nested extension rather than
9                                                                                                 9
     being counted as another scalar-action policy service.
10                                                                                                10

11                                                                                                11
                        R61.4.1. Design, information sets, and accounting
12                                                                                                12

13     The completed comparison has four generators: a common-action controller                   13

14   with no fitted continuation, a quadratic continuation with native exact witnesses,           14

15   a trained ReLU continuation with lossless screening, and the same trained ReLU               15

16   construction with native finite-difference witnesses. The complete services use              16

17   (d, T ) = (2, 2), (4, 4), (8, 6), training seeds 60103 and 60209, and targets q = 9/10 and   17

18   q = 4/5. The target requires expected policy cost at most q times the expected cost          18

19   of installed zero investment under the declared original initial law. These targets          19

20   are not a claim about the optimum at every state.                                            20

21     Every service starts from the primitives. Its declared construction ladder uses            21

22   (256, 32, 4) and (1024, 64, 8) for training samples, acquired leaves and innovation          22

23   bins. The original action quantum is 1/4096. Verification uses the same signed               23

24   reference-advantage calculation, complete-cell feasibility rule and continuous-action        24

25   lower cover for all four generators. Sequential inference examines 4096, 16,384 and          25

26   65,536 original-law interval paths, stopping at the first declared condition. The            26

27   current execution has two hosted-worker repetitions of every mathematical design. 27
28   Repeating identical seeds, policies and paths on a second worker does not double             28

29   the number of independent economic observations.                                             29

30     The source freeze precedes the scientific services. A first execution failed in the        30

31   parent recorder after an exclusive-write file was opened a second time. Its completed        31

32   child outputs and failure logs remain in the attempt archive. A separately frozen            32
     198

1    correction changes that recorder to immutable numbered checkpoints and a single          1

2    final ledger; the mathematical child service, seeds, targets and stopping rules are      2

3    unchanged. The full corrected catalogue is used below, with no mixture of partial        3

4    first-run clocks and completed rerun clocks. The separate centered-Bellman amend- 4
5    ment was written after the uncentered frontier was observed. The fixed-policy            5

6    revalidation and native factorial protocols likewise identify which predecessor          6

7    results were known before their own frozen computations.                                 7

8                                                                                             8

9                                                                                             9

10                                                                                            10
                       R61.4.2. Complete services and actual policy costs
11                                                                                            11

12     Table R61.4.I reports every task–target–generator group on the complete return         12

13   boundary. It includes imports, fitting, the executed construction and search attempts,   13

14   whole-cell verification, sequential inference, durable scientific outputs, the process   14

15   log and the first durable parent receipt. Cold use additionally charges native com- 15
16   pilation; warm use starts with the compiled binary available. The source registry        16

17   gives every one of the 96 services, including the 16 exhausted budgets. In total, 80     17

18   services meet their declared economic target.                                            18

19     The screened and native ReLU configurations return the same fitted parameters,         19

20   action witnesses, acquired policy, verification arrays, stopping stage, path counts      20

21   and cost intervals. Their difference is therefore an implementation-work difference      21

22   at the same mathematical answer. The full-service comparison charges the native          22

23   request and response as well as the exact computation. The native query benchmark        23

24   below is not substituted for this complete return boundary, and a reduction in exact     24

25   comparison counts is not presumed to translate proportionally into service time.         25

26     A different generator requires a different comparison. Table R61.4.III reports         26

27   native-ReLU-minus-common and native-ReLU-minus-quadratic cost intervals for              27

28   each task, target and seed on a fresh final-policy comparison stream. Positive           28

29   intervals identify a more costly native-ReLU policy; exact identities are reported as    29

30   identities; other intervals containing zero remain unresolved. The matched native- 30
31   minus-screened contrast is exactly zero because their complete actor identities          31

32   coincide. Conventional methods receive the same verifier and economic criterion. 32

25                                                                                                              25
            R61.4.3. A language- and representation-matched action-search experiment
26                                                                                                              26

27     The eight-method stress experiment varies three training-style fixture seeds,                            27

28   two feature widths, three action-lattice cardinalities and five regimes. The regimes                       28

29   include mixed activation, activation crossing, large signed cancellation, an exact tie                     29

30   between neighboring actions and a flat objective. Every rational objective is checked                      30

31   against exhaustive Fraction evaluation with the original smallest-index tie rule. An                       31

32   additional fixed subset receives seven shuffled repetitions per regime and worker. 32
     202

1    Coarser endpoint bounds and large-lattice guard cases are retained whether or not                         1

2    pruning is effective.                                                                                     2

3      The eight methods separate symbolic reduction, vectorized enclosure, screening,                         3

4    exhaustive exact evaluation, native finite differences and adaptive subdivision. 4
5    However, one direct contrast in that catalogue combines two changes: native ex- 5
6    haustive search uses the unreduced objective, whereas the native piecewise solver                         6

7    first reduces activation regions. We therefore execute a further two-by-two com- 7
8    parison before drawing an algorithm-specific timing conclusion. All four cells use                        8

9    the identical C++ arbitrary-size rational engine, objective, lattice, exact tie rule and                  9

10   original-value postcheck. They cross activation reduction, off or on, with exhaustive                     10

11   or finite-difference search. Reduction is charged to both reduced implementations. 11
12   The same ninety fixed rational instances, rather than an outcome-selected favorable                       12

13   subset, are used on two newly allocated worker jobs.                                                      13

14                                                                                                             14
                                               TABLE R61.4.IV

26    of paired per-instance relative timings, not ratios of unrelated medians. Reduction, process creation,   26

27         serialization and parsing are charged to both methods. The unreduced pair and all four-cell         27

28                           repeated-query results are retained in the supplement.                            28

29                                                                                                             29

30     At a 513-point lattice, finite differences save a median of approximately 61–73                         30

31   percent relative to native exhaustive search at the same reduced representation,                          31

32   depending on width and worker. Every instance in those cells is faster. At width 32                       32
                                  NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                203

1    and lattice size 33, the median saving is negative on both workers, approximately           1

2    −18 and −19 percent. Thus the operation-count advantage at large lattices has               2

3    an observed small-lattice boundary; a root-free exact solver is not uniformly the           3

4    cheapest implementation. The separate width-eight, 33-point cell is close to the            4

5    process-overhead floor and is more sensitive to the worker and instance. The com- 5
6    plete four-cell repetition table retains these effects rather than attributing a language   6

7    change or activation simplification to screening alone.                                     7

8      Batching supplies another measured distinction. The complete ninety-query                 8

9    catalogue is evaluated at batch sizes one, eight and thirty-two for each native im- 9
10   plementation. Preprocessing, reduction where applicable, serialization, process             10

11   creation, parsing and exact original-objective checks are all included. Every batch         11

12   returns the same ninety exact answers. The batch study changes only the bound- 12
13   ary of an implementation service; it does not create a new fitted policy or a new           13

14   economic stopping experiment. Its full timings and byte counts are reported in the          14

15   supplement, separately from the complete policy-service clocks.                             15

16     Flatness and cancellation also clarify what a lossless result means. An interval          16

17   screen is allowed to retain every minimizer and many nonminimizers; it need                 17

18   not produce one survivor. The finite-difference construction instead uses the exact         18

19   polynomial piece, retaining the first minimizer even on a flat segment. Adaptive            19

20   subdivision can reach its node budget and call an exact fallback. A fallback preserves      20

21   correctness but charges its prefix work in addition to the subsequent solver. The           21

22   full stress tables report all fallback events, endpoint resolutions, recorded operand       22

23   lengths and interval-array memory. Endpoint coarsening after an exact rational              23

24   bound is not described as a change in internal floating-point precision.                    24

25                                                                                               25

26                                                                                               26

27                                                                                               27
                       R61.4.4. Two controls and an additional innovation
28                                                                                               28

29     The multi-action calculation uses saved, genuinely trained two-state ReLU con- 29
30   tinuations at two specified states. The two controls share capacity a1 + a2 ≤ 1/8. 30
31   Their exposure columns are (1/2, 1/4) and (1/4, 1/2), and the optional second in- 31
32   dependent shock has a different exposure. Quantum denominators 64 and 128 give              32
     204

1    45 and 153 feasible lattice points. Setting the second action to zero and disabling the                 1

2    second shock recovers the original action-dependent objective.                                          2

6                                                                                            6
                   R61.4.6. Economic interpretation and computational scope
7                                                                                            7

8
       For two implementations returning the same policy, the gain is a reduction in com- 8
9
     putation cost, conditional on the measured execution environment. The setup-and- 9
10                                                                                           10
     use formula above gives the full range of implementation charges and use counts
11
     supported at any stated price of the work unit. For different policies, a returned- 11
12                                                                                           12
     policy gain interval [l, u], a replacement fee τ and an incremental measured-work
13                                                                                           13
     price λ∆w give the net-benefit interval
14                                                                                           14

15                                                                                           15

16                              [l − τ − λ∆w, u − τ − λ∆w].                                  16

17                                                                                           17

18                                                                                           18

19   A positive lower endpoint supports replacement under that price specification;          19

20   a negative upper endpoint rules it out; the remaining cases are unresolved. This        20

21   algebra does not estimate an installation fee, an electricity price or a welfare nor- 21
22   malization from a hosted-runner clock.                                                  22

23     The experiments specify CPU affinity and record the available processor, compiler     23

24   and runtime metadata. Processor frequency is not controlled and hardware counters       24

25   were unavailable under the recorded permissions. Separate worker allocations            25

26   do not establish independent physical architectures. Rational operation counts,         26

27   observed operand-size diagnostics, interval-array bytes, process resident memory,       27

28   cold compilation and complete return times therefore remain separate quantities. 28
29   The new evidence supports a terminating, costed exact witness and an executable         29

30   original-optimum certificate within the original NBO development. It does not           30

31   require suppressing adverse conventional comparisons or inferring an unexecuted         31

32   scaling result.                                                                         32
     208

1       APPENDIX R61.5: P ROSPECTIVE ACCURACY AND COMPLETE ECONOMIC                           1

2                                          POLICIES                                           2

3                                                                                             3
       The original-optimum question is now asked before execution, for every declared
4                                                                                             4
     generator and economic cell. The previous service catalogue stopped on an initial-
5                                                                                             5
     law gain relative to zero investment. That remains a meaningful adoption question,
6                                                                                             6
     but it is not a certificate against the Bellman optimum. The present experiment
7                                                                                             7
     makes the latter the common accuracy contract and separately evaluates the actual
8                                                                                             8
     cost of each complete returned policy.
9                                                                                             9

10                                                                                            10
                         R61.5.1. Design and actual returned objects
11                                                                                            11

12     The catalogue has five cells: two-state economies with horizons two and three, a       12

13   four-state economy with horizon three, an eight-state economy with horizon two,          13

14   and the nested two-state, two-control economy with horizon two. The original scalar      14

15   law and discount 15/16 are unchanged. The last cell implements both controls at          15

16   every date, with the capacity simplex, cross-action cost and additional continuous       16

17   innovation stated in Section R61.3. It is not the earlier isolated two-control action    17

18   query.                                                                                   18

19     For each cell, the common comparator constructs nodal Bellman lower and upper          19

20   bounds from primitive costs and transitions. Each fitted generator constructs its own    20

21   future labels using 512 training states per date. The ReLU fit has 32 learned hidden     21

22   units and 120 optimization iterations; the quadratic fit uses the same number of         22

23   states. Four seeds, 6201–6204, are fixed before production. Each fitted continuation     23

24   supplies proposals on the same 32-leaf state partition. The scalar proposal uses the     24

25   retained exact root-free action recovery. The two-control proposal uses conventional     25

26   sequential quadratic programming from four fixed starts, followed by downward            26

27   lattice rounding and a feasibility check. Its approximate objective and termination      27

28   flag are not treated as a certificate. The original-optimum verifier supplies the        28

29   postcheck for both types of proposals.                                                   29

30     The finest proposed state grids have n = 64 in two states, n = 16 in four states and   30

31   n = 4 in eight states. Every preceding declared rung is executed and retained. Scalar    31

32   and two-control action meshes have sixteen subdivisions, except the eight-state cell,    32
                                     NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                        209

1    which has eight. Every continuous innovation component has four bins, except that                     1

2    cell, which has two. No extra rung is added after a target fails. The five common                     2

3    tolerances are 1/2, 1/4, 1/8, 1/16 and 1/32.                                                          3

4      At each nodal state we preserve both the pure fitted proposal and the proposal                      4

5    augmented by the same common action family. The latter chooses the smaller                            5

6    verified original-continuation upper endpoint, retaining the common candidate on a                    6

7    tie. The nodal actions are then interpolated barycentrically, observed at forty bits and              7

8    rounded downward at twenty action bits. These are explicitly new implementable                        8

9    policies. We do not call this operation revalidation of an unchanged discontinuous                    9

10   actor. The preceding fixed-policy study remains the place where the earlier actors                    10

11   are verified without changing them.                                                                   11

12                                                                                                         12

13                               R61.5.2. Matched all-state accuracy                                       13

14     Theorem 68 is applied to every actual returned actor. Its allowance includes                        14

15   the entire continuous-action optimum, off-grid state error, continuous innovation                     15

16   integration, arithmetic, state acquisition, action rounding and all remaining dates. 16
17   Neither an initial-state average nor a favorable cost difference can substitute for the               17

18   maximum over states and dates in the stopping comparison.                                             18

8    alone does not remove the loss of a fixed fitted proposal.                                  8

9      The main table shows the final rung and the full range over the four seeds. The           9

10   supplementary target and refinement tables give every target and every rung. In             10

11   particular, the accuracy of the common comparator is not assigned to a pure neural          11

12   policy. Pure policies have their own selected-action upper arrays and their own             12

13   complete error recursions. Augmented policies receive a certificate for the actions         13

14   they actually select, not a retrospective claim that the neural component alone             14

15   produced the result.                                                                        15

16     The new second-order account differs from the previous range-table calculation            16

17   in two ways. Positive interpolation preserves nodal information about the true              17

18   convex Bellman function, rather than paying its full variation over every reached           18

19   rectangle. Joint convexity also transfers nodal actions to an off-grid policy without       19

20   imposing smoothness on a discontinuous actor. Both steps use the proved primitive           20

21   regularity; neither is an empirical estimate of an unknown Bellman derivative. The          21

22   retained tensor implementation exposes its dimension dependence, including the              22

23   eight-state budget, rather than claiming a dimension-free solver.                           23

                      R61.5.3. Direct policy costs and the adoption decision
26                                                                                               26

27     All final-rung actors are evaluated under the original uniform initial law and            27

28   continuous innovation laws on 65,536 fresh common paths per cell. Forty-bit bins            28

29   enclose each continuous state and innovation realization, and outward propagation           29

30   encloses the cost of the implemented acquired policy. The path endpoints, not a             30

31   fitted value at the initial state, enter the bounded empirical-Bernstein calculation. A     31

32   single error budget of 1/100 covers the prespecified finite family across all five cells,   32
                                       NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                           211

1    seeds and variants. Repeated seeds define different fitted objects; common paths in- 1
2    duce dependence between their comparisons, which is allowed by the simultaneous                              2

3    coverage construction. Exact policy identities are reported as identities.                                   3

     212

1      Economic findings. The full catalogue gives a clear empirical ordering. All twenty       1

2    pure-ReLU-minus-common intervals are strictly positive: in each declared task              2

3    and seed, the pure fitted controller has higher expected cost than the common              3

4    comparator. All twenty augmented-ReLU-minus-common intervals contain zero                  4

5    and therefore do not rank those two controllers. The augmented variants attain the         5

6    shared original-optimum accuracy contracts, but this is not evidence of a neural cost      6

7    advantage. The improvement is in completing a verifiable NBO policy construction           7

8    and in quantifying the contribution and cost of its fallback, not in overturning           8

9    the observed conventional ranking. In 20 of the twenty pure-versus-augmented               9

10   comparisons, the paired gain interval has a positive lower endpoint. These iden- 10
11   tified replacement gains concern the actual fitted incumbent and its augmented             11

12   replacement; they are not gains over the common comparator.                                12

13     The sign convention is neural cost minus common cost. A wholly negative in- 13
14   terval identifies a lower neural-policy cost. An interval containing zero does not         14

15   establish equality or superiority. The supplement gives every marginal cost, every         15

16   corresponding quadratic contrast and every zero-investment adoption gain; all              16

17   four seeds are retained, not just the best-performing continuation. A change in the        17

18   selected witness or in an all-state upper bound is therefore not used as a substitute      18

19   for an economic comparison.                                                                19

20     For an installation fee τ ≥ 0, let [l, u] be the simultaneous interval for zero-policy   20

21   cost minus the proposed controller’s cost. Adoption has net gain in [l − τ, u − τ ]. 21
22   Every fee below a positive l is supported as beneficial; every fee above u is ruled        22

23   out; the remaining range is unresolved. The same calculation applies to replace- 23
24   ment of an installed pure fitted controller by its augmented controller using the          24

25   separately recorded paired contrast. These are actual complete-policy decisions in         25

26   the normalized economy. The intervals do not estimate an institutional installation        26

27   charge, and a gain over zero investment does not establish a gain over the common          27

28   Bellman comparator.                                                                        28

1                      R61.5.4. Complete work at a common economic contract                                    1

2                                                                                                              2
       The cost of verification is part of the method. The reference lower and upper
3                                                                                                              3
     arrays are computed from primitives for every rung. The fitting/proposal service,
4
     actor transfer, every selected-action verification and serialization are then added. 4
5                                                                                                              5
     To avoid inventing unobserved marginal costs, each method is charged the entire
6                                                                                                              6
     shared reference and the entire shared inference service. Pure and augmented
7                                                                                                              7
     policies are verified together; each is conservatively charged that joint work. Cold
8                                                                                                              8
     compilation is included in the complete-release total and separately identified in its
9                                                                                                              9
     receipt.

24                                                                                                             24

25                                                                                                             25

26     Closing the measured production boundary. The frozen per-method release field                           26

27   sums measured core components. The publication audit found that common- 27
28   certificate bookkeeping, some output records and the final actor-file serialization                       28

29   were outside those individual clocks. We preserve the field unchanged as a compo- 29
30   nent sum, rather than calling it an isolated complete runtime. The recorded outer                         30

31   production step spans 466 seconds at whole-second timestamp resolution; a trans- 31
32   parent two-second reserve gives a 468-second accounting envelope. Subtracting                             32
     214

1    the nonoverlapping component clocks leaves a shared unallocated residual. The          1

2    table charges that entire residual to every method, in addition to its own component   2

3    sum. These are conservative complete-production charges, not separately observed       3

4    method runtimes or statistical timing confidence bounds. Remote publication, doc- 4
5    ument production and replay have their own receipts. This correction changes           5

6    neither a scientific result nor a measured service clock.                              6

7      For each tolerance, a method is eligible precisely when its recorded actual-policy   7

8    certificate attains that tolerance. Minimizing the conservative complete-production    8

9    charge among eligible methods defines the finite release frontier. This answers        9

10   which executed complete catalogue supplies a certified controller at the stated        10

11   contract. It does not estimate the minimum work of a sequential algorithm that was     11

12   never run. The recorded first-crossing verification prefixes remain separate from      12

13   this complete-release quantity, since the present independent cost service evaluates   13

14   the final catalogue jointly.                                                           14

15     The component timings are descriptive for the recorded host and arithmetic           15

16   engine; the shared-residual charge is an explicitly stated accounting convention. 16
17   Processor frequency was not controlled, and hardware counters were unavailable. 17
18   The exact native witness retains its rational operand and request/response diag- 18
19   nostics. The new interpolation kernel reports explicit array sizes and arithmetic      19

20   allowances, but logical input/output bytes are not presented as measured memory- 20
21   bus traffic. No worker repetition is counted as an additional independent economic     21

22   observation.                                                                           22

23                                                                                          23

24                                                                                          24

25                                                                                          25
                             R61.5.5. Reliability and interpretation
26                                                                                          26

27     The four seeds define a finite fitting catalogue, not a probability model for opti- 27
28   mizer success in an unspecified future economy. A pure fitted actor that fails an      28

29   original-optimum target remains a failure in that catalogue even when its aug- 29
30   mented counterpart succeeds. The count therefore separates the ability of the fit      30

31   to supply a sufficiently accurate policy from the ability of the common verified       31

32   fallback to complete the economic contract.                                            32
                                        NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                          215

1      This execution advances the original NBO objective from a conditional action                               1

2    witness to a full original-optimum account across state dimensions, horizons and a                           2

3    genuinely implemented two-control policy. The preceding scalar exact-search result                           3

4    retains its stated function-class and lattice assumptions. The new policy theorem is                         4

5    independent of those assumptions once a feasible nodal proposal has been supplied. 5
6    This extends certification to other proposal generators without asserting a new                              6

7    exact solver for deep networks or arbitrary shock distributions. Earlier conventional                        7

8    wins, failed targets, adverse multi-action query timings and broader economic                                8

9    applications remain available under their original hypotheses.                                               9
